## estate / critical

### F-01 — `did:nostr` attribution on agent-events is forgeable by any `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` holder on the shared docker network at the documented pin
- Topics: ES-02.1, ES-04.1, ES-01.4
- Evidence: The drift note states the fix landed on "agentbox main b9e0ea9a8" (2026-10-12) while every file's `verified_commit` pins agentbox `3f05ae9e7ae13d103ad8d9fe06baed991fedb987` and ES-01.1 records the VisionClaw gitlink (`9fd49a9`) behind agentbox HEAD (`20499ef`). At the pinned revision, path 2 of `verifyAgentEventRequest` accepts the proxy-stamped `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` "trusted from any TCP peer" while management-api "binds `0.0.0.0`" (`agentbox/management-api/lib/agent-event-auth.js:157-160`, `:31-39`), so "a `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` holder on the docker network could emit events under another `did:nostr` that VisionClaw then ingested as attributed". Because the publish is loopback-only on the host, peer containers on `visionclaw_network` still reach the bind (the same reasoning as ES-10.8 D3). Residual even after the fix: "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1)".
- Failure: Any container sharing the bridge with a leaked or shared `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` publishes agent-actions attributed to an arbitrary `did:nostr`; VisionClaw's ingest records them as attributed (`IngestOutcome::Published{attributed…}`) and they drive provenance, beams and any downstream governance read.
- Confidence: high — the material's own dated note places the fix outside the verified pin; "which revision is actually deployed" is the open question (see Not judgeable).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift + Debt) — but the consequence that the fix is *outside the pinned revision* is unmarked.

### F-02 — The standalone Cloudflare tunnel can front the demo-open dev stack whose compose now defaults the total auth bypass ON, and the exposure is invisible to every gate
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.6, ES-10.8, ES-09.8
- Evidence: Compose defaults invert the fail-closed code defaults to demo-open (`RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1`, `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1`, `docker-compose.unified.yml:106-107`) and "VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE now DEFAULTS TO 1" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:90`). ADR-2039's bypass is "TOTAL — no NIP-98, no token, no peer check, across REST verify_access, the settings extractor and the WS handshake. Peer-agnostic BY DESIGN" (`src/utils/auth.rs:99`). The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted… anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel", its "Route is configured as a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname — no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo", and it is "invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit that only sweeps published ports". ADR-2119 "leaves this standalone file unaffected… `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` therefore does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel fronting a LAN-declared host" (ADR-2119:64-66). The listener-based invariant that would catch container-internal binds is only PROPOSED (ES-10.8 D5).
- Failure: A dev stack running with default env plus a hand-started (or forgotten) standalone tunnel exposes graph writes, WebSocket upgrades and settings mutation to the public internet with no authentication, and no artifact in the repo records or audits the exposed hostname.
- Confidence: high that the composition is possible and ungated; whether such a tunnel is currently running is not stated.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — each ingredient is marked (DRIFT1, CFT, LANGAP, D5); the composed incident scenario is not.

### F-03 — The raw-email break-glass tier is gated by supplying a value the material itself says is public
- Topics: ES-06.3, ES-06.2
- Evidence: Tier 1 is a bearer token that "gates ANY call". Tier 2 checks "the nostr_pubkey ARGUMENT… against the server allow-list", and the authors' own invariant states "a Nostr PUBLIC key is the publishable half, an identity/capability token, not a secret. Passing it as a tool argument is the design, not a leak." The operator pubkey is published in the repo (`agentbox/agentbox.toml:160`, `b4165401`), sits on the public relay allowlist, and the skill is told to read it from `AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX` at call time. A value that is public cannot simultaneously function as a capability: the note's defence contradicts its own premise.
- Failure: Any process holding the container-wide bearer token (which per ES-06.8 every MCP client holds) and knowing the operator's published pubkey calls `fetch_email_raw` with that pubkey and receives full raw mail headers and bodies with the egress filter bypassed — the entire privacy design of the Loom path defeated by one public string. Only an 8-char fingerprint is logged.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — it depends on whether the allowlist contents are in practice confidential, which the material does not state.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the material explicitly defends the design.

### F-04 — Two topics state opposite behaviour for degraded-boot pod writes: unsigned fallback vs fail-closed
- Topics: ES-04.4 vs ES-08.2, ES-04.5
- Evidence: At the same agentbox commit `3f05ae9e`, ES-04.4 shows "signer unavailable or the key will not decrypt → no header → UNSIGNED write" with "DIVERGENCE — pod signing can fall back UNSIGNED (legacy agentbox ADR-026). Combined with the `did:nostr:local` placeholder fallback that agent-identity.js:41-43 says the entrypoint still runs unconditionally, a degraded boot can silently produce a NON-SOVEREIGN identity." ES-08.2 states "RESOLVED ADR-2064 (2026-09-05): sign_requests now sets requireSigned on the adapter even when no signer could be built, so a falsy nip98 fails closed" and `_signedFetch` "throws typed SigningUnavailable… no request is emitted" — but only "unless integrations.solid_pod_rs.sign_requests=true… default keeps prior unsigned behaviour byte-identical (pod-signer.js:34-35)".
- Failure: Either the identity topic is stale (docs unreliable on a custody-critical path) or the default configuration is exactly the vulnerable one: with the flag off — the default — a degraded boot writes agent state to pods unsigned under a placeholder identity, losing attribution on durable records, or silently losing writes to a default-deny pod.
- Confidence: high that the two topics contradict each other at the cited revision; an engineer must determine which behaviour the code has.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: each topic marks its own claim; the cross-topic contradiction is unmarked.

### F-05 — Host-root equivalence by default: the agentbox container widens the host Docker socket `o+rw` and runs every program as one uid sharing PID 1's environment
- Topics: ES-10.12, ES-01.5, ES-05.10
- Evidence: With `role_isolation = false`, "the shipped default", "the entrypoint widens the host Docker socket o+rw for devuser (entrypoint-unified.sh:589-593)" and "every program but bootstrap and tailscale runs as devuser, and PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all of them". The override adds `group_add 965` ("the container drives docker WITHOUT sudo"). The flag-on mitigation cannot undo history: "the socket is the HOST inode, already widened by earlier boots. Skipping the chmod does not narrow it; only a host-side chmod does" (AB-36 R2). ES-02.7 shows this power in use: VisionClaw `docker exec`s into another container to supervise processes.
- Failure: Any compromised or misbehaving agent process, MCP server or npm dependency in the container is host-root-equivalent via the socket, and reads every secret in PID 1's env including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, gateway tokens and the bridge key path. Same-UID isolation is explicitly absent (ES-05.10: "Same-UID agents are not isolated by application-level policy").
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (ES-10.12, OPEN2/R2, AB-36)

### F-06 — Secret custody is plaintext on disk and in backups
- Topics: ES-10.10, ES-09.24
- Evidence: "SOPS never executed (legacy ADR-109, accepted 2026-05-09) — VisionClaw .env is PLAINTEXT today, no SOPS artifacts in tree" (D5). "VisionClaw backup-secrets.sh still writes ordinary ZIP with an integrity check, without explicit encryption or permission hardening" while the hardened agentbox `services/secret-backup` "refuses missing encryption authority and hardens output to 0600" — "Its existence does not replace the legacy script or prove deployed adoption" (D2). Every custodian, location, rotation cadence and incident window in the custody register is "UNCONFIRMED".
- Failure: One readable host path or one exfiltrated ZIP yields the API keys, tunnel tokens and relay keys for the whole estate; the two backup paths mean an operator may believe encrypted backups exist when the script actually in use writes plaintext.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D5, D2)

### F-07 — Erasure is structurally broken: deleting agent memory from the pod never revokes the searchable embedding
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9 (D5)
- Evidence: "deleteAgentMemory tombstone gap — this call graph never reaches RuVector. agentMemory.ts:196-204 has no reverse-tombstone dispatch", so "the embedding row persists and stays semantically searchable. Largest erasure hole." DATA-authority-erasure Invariant 6 "requires a dropped secondary write to be logged, not swallowed — this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence".
- Failure: A user deletes agent memory (or an erasure request is honoured on the pod); the content remains retrievable by semantic search against RuVector indefinitely, with no joined key-to-row lookup even available to find it. This is a compliance and custody incident that compounds silently with every deletion.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, D5)

### F-08 — No point-in-time backups, no cross-store restore, no RPO/RTO for the pod store or RuVector
- Topics: ES-08.8, ES-07.9 (D5)
- Evidence: "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only… no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO" (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md Known divergences). "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO."
- Failure: Disk loss or corruption on the pod volume destroys sovereign identities, mandates, receipts and brief records — the "durable authority artefacts" of ES-05.9 — with no restore path; even with partial stores restored, the stores were never consistent to begin with, so "restore" is undefined.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in both topics)

### F-09 — The released pod crate hand-rolls Bitcoin/Schnorr crypto, and the estate runs three unsynced sats ledgers against it
- Topics: ES-08.12, ES-03.12, ES-01.8
- Evidence: "the released crate owns the estate's whole Bitcoin transaction construction and HAND-ROLLS it: BIP-341 scripts, the TapSighash and key-path Schnorr on raw k256, with big-endian mod-n scalar arithmetic written out by hand" (ADR-2008:22-26); the rust-bitcoin port is "landed on main, unreleased". The crate's own README "reproduce[s] a critical finding saying not to carry value through the payment routes until it is fixed" (ADR-2008:72-75). Meanwhile "the estate runs THREE independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers and NONE of them is synced with the others" (sovereign-settlement.md:61), the host deposit handler "returns 501 so no value ever entered it", version pins skew "and no gate compares them".
- Failure: If any value ever moves, a scalar-arithmetic or sighash defect in hand-rolled crypto produces silent mis-signing or fund loss with no library audit trail to fall back on; the three-ledger split means even detecting the loss requires reconciling stores that were never consistent.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the incident is conditional on value flowing, which the material does not establish.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED/DIVERGENCE markers throughout; the README's own warning)

### F-10 — Release hygiene is enforced by a Dockerfile text-scan while the shipped binary carries a no-op stub
- Topics: ES-09.5, ES-09.11
- Evidence: "The binary still carries enforce_release_env_hygiene as a no-op stub (src/main.rs:169), so the ADR-2037 boundary is the CI gate and the profile name, not the compiler flag", and "ADR-2037 (proposed, implementation_status none): no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected." The blocking `dev-auth-release-gate` job is a "hermetic text assertion over the committed Dockerfiles/entrypoints… no --release cargo line may name dev-auth" — it inspects inputs, not artefacts.
- Failure: One pipeline edit, base-image swap or manual build that compiles `--release` with `dev-auth` ships the Bearer dev-session-token bypass and the stubbed hygiene check to production, and no gate examines the artefact to notice.
- Confidence: high on the described gap; the failure needs a build mistake to trigger.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-09.5)

### F-11 — Neither rationale gate can fire on a VisionClaw governance case, and the two gates read different tiers
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13, ES-05.14
- Evidence: "VisionClaw's 31400 carries only a d tag and a PanelDefinition with no tier or task-property field" (events.rs:211-217, elevation_actor.rs:239), "so the relay folds every VisionClaw case to the advertised default, medium, and check_rationale only bites at high or critical: NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case" (governance.rs:783, wrangler.toml:48). Additionally "the two gates read DIFFERENT tiers. The host reads the agent's DECLARED tier, the relay reads the EFFECTIVE tier" (governance.rs:773). Both forum ADR-2010 and agentbox ADR-2087 activations "wait on the same event — the owner's first decision on a real high-tier case", and the forum records vs ADR-2110 disagree on whether such a case exists ("Neither cites a `--list-cases` read of it").
- Failure: A high-consequence decision taken through a VisionClaw panel proceeds with no rationale recorded, violating the canon rule ("no surface may fabricate… absence renders as absence" is moot when nothing demands presence), and the estate's own acceptance evidence for two governance ADRs is blocked on a case whose existence is disputed between records.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium

Also "skill-count-check was RED and UNWIRED". ES-02.12 separately records the colloquy kinds as "NOT yet fixture-backed. Extending tests/fixtures/federation-identity.v1.json… is a merge requirement before any 38410 event is published to a relay" (OPEN).

### F-13 — The estate documentation spans at least five agentbox revisions; what is actually deployed is undeterminable, and 15 host ADRs already fail their own staleness gate
- Topics: ES-01.1, ES-02, ES-04, ES-07, ES-09.23, ES-09.24
- Evidence: `verified_commit` is agentbox `3f05ae9e` across files, while claimed resolutions cite `b9e0ea9a8` (agent-event auth, 2026-10-12), `c274ae233` (SONA event field), `6466e3931` (custody CI, 2026-10-03), `e5826a8` (canon tracking) and HEAD `20499ef`; ES-01.1 records the superproject gitlink `9fd49a9` "ahead"-lagging. Separately, "at visionclaw f223bbd40 FIFTEEN host records fail this gate, each naming the governed path that moved under it… a re-verification backlog".
- Failure: An operator acting on any "RESOLVED" note cannot know whether the running image contains the fix; conversely, stale ADR verification claims mean governance records assert code behaviour the tree no longer has. Incidents will be triaged against the wrong code.
- Confidence: high on the stated facts; the deploy-state consequence is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the commit spread and staleness count are stated as facts; the operational risk is unmarked.

### F-14 — A nightly automated cycle performs SSH, model calls, push and publication with a journal but no approval or denial path
- Topics: ES-05.11, ES-05.10, ES-90.2
- Evidence: "Nightly SSH, model calls, push and publication → POST /v1/exec/record appends tool.called and tool.completed… records only" — "journalled, never approved or denied (Phase 2 policing stays out of scope)" (ADR-2071:5-7,193). Broader coverage is explicitly route-specific: "A working task-spawn journal does not establish complete mediation of shell commands or nightly egress", and "Same-UID agents are not isolated by application-level policy". On the dream side, enforcement "still depends on the annexe runner honouring a failed required evaluator. This repo makes the failure, it does not veto the night".
- Failure: A degenerate nightly run — wrong model output, drifted corpus, or an evaluator failure the runner ignores — pushes and publishes autonomously; the journal records that it happened but nothing is positioned to stop it, and the same-UID environment means any agent process can initiate the same egress.
- Confidence: high on the described posture.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (GAP/scope notes in ES-05.11)

### F-15 — A transient signer load failure at boot permanently disables pod writes for the process lifetime
- Topics: ES-08.2, ES-04.4, ES-04.5
- Evidence: The signer lifecycle is "built ONCE, loaded lazily on first use, cached — a load failure is cached and never retried" (pod-signer.js:106-116). Under the ADR-2064 posture, `_signedFetch` then "throws typed SigningUnavailable… no request is emitted" for every subsequent call. The sibling pattern repeats elsewhere in the identity plane: NIP-07 "sessions do NOT survive a proxy restart" (session secret defaults to per-boot random, proxy.mjs:245) and deleting the AoE state file "does NOT rotate the token, because the proxy holds a last-good cache".
- Failure: A momentary key-decrypt failure or missing secret during a degraded boot turns every subsequent governed pod write, receipt mint and mandate write into a hard error until a full process restart — a silent, indefinite write outage of the sovereign-data path with only typed errors and no retry.
- Confidence: high on the cached-failure lifecycle; the permanent-outage consequence is inferred from the stated behaviour.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the lifecycle is stated as an invariant; its availability consequence is unmarked.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Deployed revisions.** Image digests, running container tags and the actual checked-out submodule commit per host — needed to know which of the "RESOLVED at commit X" fixes (F-01, F-13) are live in production.
2. **Tunnel state and Cloudflare dashboard configuration.** Whether the standalone cloudflared container runs anywhere, and the actual Public Hostname mappings — the material states these are not reviewable from the repo (F-02).
3. **Confidentiality of `PRIVILEGED_NOSTR_PUBKEYS` and bearer-token distribution.** Whether the allowlist contents and the email-gateway token are held more narrowly than "every process in the container" (F-03).
4. **Whether value has ever moved.** Balances/testnet-vs-real status of the three sats ledgers and which solid-pod-rs binary (0.4.x hand-rolled vs the unreleased port) is deployed (F-09).
5. **Operational backup reality and legal erasure obligations.** Whether backups are taken in practice despite no scripted RPO/RTO, what the pod store actually holds, and what data-protection duties the estate is under — the erasure hole's severity depends on duties the material cannot state (F-07, F-08).

## estate / premortem

# Post-mortem — production incident, twelve months forward

**Incident class:** data-protection and custody failure leading to loss of client trust. The operator lost a client because data the client believed erased remained retrievable, and separately, data written during a degraded boot carried no sovereign identity and was outside the access-control perimeter the client was promised.

---

## Root cause 1 — the erasure tombstone gap: deleted agent memory remains semantically searchable in RuVector

### R-1 — Client data survives an erasure request because the pod delete path never reaches the vector store
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9, ES-07.1
- Chain of events:
  1. A client requests erasure of their agent's memory. The operator calls `deleteAgentMemory` on the SolidPodService.
  2. The pod resource (`containerPath/key.jsonld`) is deleted via HTTP DELETE, returns 204, the operator confirms deletion to the client.
  3. The corresponding embedding row in RuVector Postgres is never touched. No reverse-tombstone dispatch exists.
  4. A semantic search over the memory namespace returns the deleted content — the vector, the text, and the metadata are all still there and still HNSW-indexed.
  5. The client (or their auditor) discovers the "deleted" data through a search result.
- Evidence:
  - "deleteAgentMemory() in the Pod does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory: the embedding row persists and stays semantically searchable. Largest erasure hole." — ES-07.9 D5
  - "this call graph never reaches RuVector. agentMemory.ts:196-204 has no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent" — ES-08.9
  - "Invariant 6 of docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md requires a dropped secondary write to be logged, not swallowed - this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence" — ES-08.9
  - "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO." — ES-07.9 D5
  - The historical governing document already calls this "an erasure hole" — ES-08.9 citing `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md`
- Warning signs visible today: The material explicitly labels this the "Largest erasure hole" and states no repair exists. No backup, no RPO, no RTO, no cross-store consistency. The defect is documented but unremediated.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

---

## Root cause 2 — unsigned pod writes from a degraded boot: client data written without sovereign identity or enforced access control

### R-2 — A degraded boot silently mints a placeholder identity and writes client data unsigned to a default-deny pod
- Topics: ES-04.4, ES-08.2, ES-04.1, ES-10.10
- Chain of events:
  1. The agentbox container boots with a key-decryption failure or a missing signer. The entrypoint unconditionally runs a `did:nostr:local` placeholder fallback (ES-04.4 citing `agent-identity.js:41-43`).
  2. `buildPodNip98` cannot build a signer. The load failure is cached and never retried (`pod-signer.js:106-116`).
  3. With `sign_requests` off (the default), the adapter falls back to `_rawFetch` and writes client data UNSIGNED to the Solid pod (ES-08.2).
  4. The WAC layer on the pod may accept or refuse depending on the ACL; the material notes "accepted or refused by WAC" — the data may land in an uncontrolled state.
  5. The data now carries no sovereign attribution, cannot be traced to the client's agent, and the operator has no record that it was written under degraded conditions.
  6. The client discovers their data was written without the identity guarantees they were promised.
- Evidence:
  - "pod signing can fall back UNSIGNED (legacy agentbox ADR-026). Combined with the did:nostr:local placeholder fallback that agent-identity.js:41-43 says the entrypoint still runs unconditionally, a degraded boot can silently produce a NON-SOVEREIGN identity." — ES-04.4
  - "sign_requests now sets requireSigned on the adapter even when no signer could be built, so a falsy nip98 fails closed instead of going out unsigned" — ES-08.2 (RESOLVED ADR-2064). BUT: "It remains reachable only with the flag off, where unsigned is the declared baseline" — ES-08.2
  - "nip98 is null unless integrations.solid_pod_rs.sign_requests=true (pod-signer.js:73) — default keeps prior unsigned behaviour byte-identical" — ES-08.2
  - "A load failure is cached and never retried" — ES-04.4
  - "every custodian, deployed location, rotation cadence and incident-response window in the agentbox custody register is UNCONFIRMED" — ES-04.5 D5
- Warning signs visible today: The default configuration (`sign_requests` off) is the unsigned baseline. The ADR-2064 fix only closes the hole when the flag is ON — but the flag defaults OFF. The custody register is unconfirmed. The failure is silent and cached.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

---

## Root cause 3 — the standalone Cloudflare tunnel fronts a demo-open backend and exposes anonymous API reads to the public internet

### R-3 — Client data exposed publicly because the standalone cloudflared compose fronts a demo-open VisionClaw instance with no profile enforcement
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.1, ES-09.8
- Chain of events:
  1. The operator brings up the dev profile (default), which ships `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1` — the demo-open posture (ES-10.3).
  2. Separately, the operator starts the standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` tunnel for remote access.
  3. The tunnel fronts whatever profile the backend booted — in this case, demo-open with anonymous `/api` reads enabled.
  4. The Cloudflare tunnel routes a Public Hostname to nginx port 3001. The route is configured in the Cloudflare dashboard, not in the repo, so no local review catches it.
  5. The tunnel is outbound-initiated, needs no published port, and is invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit that only sweeps published ports.
  6. Client data in the knowledge graph becomes anonymously readable from the public internet.
- Evidence:
  - "Standalone cloudflared (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml) fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted — with this demo-open posture, anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel, not just the LAN" — ES-10.3
  - "Route is configured as a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname — no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" — ES-10.3
  - "Outbound-initiated and needs no published port, so it reaches nginx port 3001 as an ordinary network peer and is invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit" — ES-10.3
  - "ADR-2119 makes the unified file's cloudflared opt-in under its own tunnel profile, but leaves this standalone file unaffected" — ES-10.3
  - "VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan therefore does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel fronting a LAN-declared host." — ES-10.3
  - Net shipped posture: "anonymous /api reads ON, owner-less boot permitted" — ES-10.3
- Warning signs visible today: The standalone cloudflared file exists, is unaffected by ADR-2119, and the compose defaults describe demo-open flags. The port-audit cannot see it. The route is not locally reviewable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

## Latent defects

### F-01 — Same-UID processes inside agentbox can forge agent identity attribution
- Topics: ES-02.1, ES-04.1
- Evidence: "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1), so attribution on the session and break-glass hop is bounded by the same-UID boundary (agentbox/management-api/lib/agent-event-auth.js:157-160)." — ES-02.1 Debt
- Failure: A compromised or buggy process running as devuser inside the agentbox container stamps an arbitrary `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` header over loopback, emitting events attributed to a different agent. Governance decisions are made on false attribution.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — The NIP-98 replay cache refuses all new authentication when full
- Topics: ES-04.3
- Evidence: "on a FULL cache the code REFUSES new auth (ReplayCacheFull) and must NOT evict the oldest live entry — evicting would let a flooder purge a genuine still-valid id and re-enable the very replay this layer prevents (nip98.rs:189-197). Bounded memory beats availability." — ES-04.3
- Failure: An attacker floods the authentication endpoint with unique valid-format tokens. The replay cache fills to `REPLAY_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES` (implied by `REPLAY_CACHE_PRUNE_THRESHOLD = 4096`). All subsequent authentication attempts are refused with `ReplayCacheFull`. Production authentication is denied.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Docker socket widened o+rw for devuser, already on the host inode from earlier boots
- Topics: ES-10.12
- Evidence: "the entrypoint widens the host Docker socket o+rw for devuser (agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:589-593)" and "the socket is the HOST inode, already widened by earlier boots. Skipping the chmod does not narrow it; only a host-side chmod does" — ES-10.12
- Failure: A compromised process inside the agentbox container uses the widened Docker socket to escape to the host, access all host resources including other containers' secrets and data.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Content address byte-identity between JS and Rust is not type-enforced
- Topics: ES-03.6
- Evidence: "JS sha12 hashes the raw string via crypto.createHash on the URN or payload string, Rust src/uri/mod.rs content_address hashes caller-supplied bytes directly - equivalence depends on callers feeding byte-identical canonical input on both sides, not enforced by the type system" — ES-03.6
- Failure: A Rust caller passes non-canonical bytes (e.g. with different whitespace or key ordering) to `content_address`. The resulting URN differs from what the JS side would produce for the same logical content. Two systems hold different identifiers for the same entity, causing a silent data split or reconciliation failure.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — Adapter contract versions frozen at 1.0.0 despite live churn

A consumer cannot tell from the version whether the contract it compiled against still holds." — ES-03.9
- Failure: A adapter implementation changes its write semantics. Because the version still says 1.0.0, a consumer that was compiled against the old contract continues calling it, corrupting data or losing writes silently.
