### F-01 — The only template declaring ADMIN_PUBKEYS ships a real, non-placeholder pubkey as default admin
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7, NF-10.6 (DD3)
- Evidence: `nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33` "ships a REAL 64-hex pubkey" — "the only template shipping a non-placeholder value where every other template ships example.com or a zeroed key" (NF-07.2 N3, NF-08.7 N5). ADMIN_PUBKEYS is read by the auth worker (`admin.rs:71`) and the relay (`auth.rs:185`) but declared in neither template, and "SETUP.md:119-123 never lists it either" (NF-08.7 N4, DD3). `/ingest` is the search worker's only mutating route, gated solely by this var (NF-07.1 N2, NF-07.2).
- Failure: a deployment that does not override the value runs with the kit author's key as search-worker admin — able to ingest or retract the index content that shapes what members find. Worse: since this is the only template carrying the var, an operator who copies it into relay/auth config grants that key full relay admin (admin kinds, channel zones, sealed originals, moderation mirrors).
- Confidence: high — the shipped key being real and not the deployer's is stated; whose key it is, inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the shipped key and the declaration gap are noted (NF-07.2 N3, NF-08.7 N4/N5, DD3, S2); the backdoor consequence is not flagged.

### F-02 — Default deployments run the preview worker's SSRF guard denylist-only, with no wall-clock timeout
- Topics: NF-07.5, NF-07.6, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable — nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13 … the allowlist ssrf.rs:100 ssrf.rs:114 is the real mitigation and the template does not set it — wrangler.toml:13 declares only ALLOWED_ORIGIN" (NF-07.6 N4). "there is NO wall-clock timeout — only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3). `/preview` is public at 30 req/60s per IP (NF-07.5).
- Failure: every stock deployment ships an open, resolver-unpinned fetch proxy: a rebinding domain passes the denylist and the worker fetches it; slow origins hold worker resources indefinitely; results are cached up to 10 days (OpenGraph TTL, lib.rs:32), so a poisoned preview persists.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — DIVERGENCE in NF-07.6 N3/N4 and S3 in NF-10.7.

### F-03 — Moderation and trust gates key on the raw signing pubkey while the whitelist gate resolves device keys to their owner
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-10.11 (KF-4, KF-9), NF-02.7, NF-10.7 (S4)
- Evidence: "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all — docs/security/known-findings.md:27, :44" (NF-10.11 N4). The pipeline resolves `effective_pubkey` at step 6b (nip_handlers.rs:878) but the ban/mute gate (nip_handlers.rs:928) and trust gates (nip_handlers.rs:940) run on the author. Device keys sit in relay D1, read at NIP-42 AUTH (NF-02.7).
- Failure: the moment DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED is switched on (ships "false" in both templates, NF-10.7 S4), a banned or suspended member publishes through their registered device key: whitelist passes (owner is a member), the ban/mute and TL gates see a fresh, unbanned pubkey. Ban evasion and trust-gate evasion in one step.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (conditional on the flag; default-off dormancy is itself marked)
- Marked by authors: yes — KF-4 and KF-9; dormancy marked S4.

### F-04 — Core governance guards compare pubkeys case-sensitively — the exact class the registry already fixed
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-6), NF-06.15, NF-02.5
- Evidence: "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim — known-findings.md:29 … the same class the registry closed in NF-06.15, left open in the core aggregate" (NF-10.11 N6). "NIP-98 accepts case-insensitive hex and returns event.pubkey verbatim, and the event id is recomputed over that string, so the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify" (NF-06.15 N1, governance_api.rs:98-100).
- Failure: an agent delegated as reviewer publishes its 31403 under an upper-cased rendering of its own key: the self-review guard (`CaseError::SelfReview`, governance.rs:1286, enforced at governance.rs:1738) misses and the agent decides its own case; the same trick bypasses the original-signer check on supersession (governance.rs:1835). The anti-corruption invariants of the human-approval chain fall to a case change.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — KF-6.

### F-05 — Governance reads verify a signature but no membership; the admin-gated reviewer aggregate is reconstructible from member-visible reads
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a), NF-06.9, NF-02.6
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read — known-findings.md:23, :24" (NF-10.11 N2). Cases and decisions are require_authed (NF-02.6 AUTHED list; governance_api.rs:561, :626); reviewer telemetry is require_admin (governance_api.rs:1407).
- Failure: any key holder on the internet reads the full case/decision stream — appeals, moderation context, human-written rationales — and rebuilds the reviewer-workload picture the operator deliberately admin-gated. It also lowers the cost of probing for the casing bypass in F-04.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — KF-2/KF-2a, recorded as a one-time operator decision.

### F-06 — ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is undeclared in every wrangler.toml, so zones configured encrypted can silently run plaintext
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.9, NF-05.16
- Evidence: "ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at zone_config.rs:86 but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker … a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (NF-08.2 N4). `is_encrypted` = gate AND zone.encrypted (zone_config.rs:127); only then does the ciphertext-shape gate apply (zone_config.rs:202, NF-08.9). The client has its own `encryption_enabled()` gate that keeps the feature dormant when unset (NF-05.16 N3).
- Failure: operator marks zones `encrypted = true` in forum.toml (the validator only rejects encrypted+public, NF-09.4 ENC) and never sets the var because no template or SETUP step names it: the relay accepts plaintext kind-42s into those zones, and — inferred — the client, gating on the same absent flag, passes posts through unencrypted. Members post into zones the config labels encrypted; storage and search see plaintext.
- Confidence: medium — relay-side non-enforcement is stated; the client pass-through chain is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — DOC-DRIFT (NF-08.2 N4); the plaintext-in-labelled-zone consequence is unflagged.

### F-07 — The client's relay URL has a second, divergent hardcoded fallback: a broken env injection silently publishes to a foreign host
- Topics: NF-05.7, NF-10.3 (O8)
- Evidence: "relay.rs reads window.__ENV__ inline — nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/relay.rs:974 — instead of calling the shared window_env helper, and the two resolvers' hardcoded fallbacks DIVERGE — relay.rs:22 versus utils/relay_url.rs:9" (NF-05.7 N2; O8 "re-verified and STILL LIVE", NF-10.3).
- Failure: any regression that drops or mangles `window.__ENV__` (base-path change, CSP, template drift) leaves the app looking fully functional while connecting to whatever host relay.rs:22 names — a host the operator does not run. Members' signed events, NIP-98 tokens and gift-wrapped DMs are published there; everything still verifies cryptographically, so nothing errors.
- Confidence: high — the divergent fallback is stated; the trigger is a config regression.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — ANOMALY O8 / NF-05.7 N2.

### F-08 — Operator-facing docs assert the opposite of enforced behaviour in at least four places
- Topics: NF-03.3, NF-10.8, NF-08.7, NF-06.17 (DD1, DD3)
- Evidence: README.md:441 calls NIP-42 "scaffolded" with "auth_required: false" while nip42 is the default (nip42.rs:115), the template ships AUTH_MODE nip42 (wrangler.toml:31) and every EVENT passes the gate (nip_handlers.rs:845) — DD1. The IDENTITY closeout still states the fixed trust-sweep OFFSET/ignored-write defects as current (NF-10.8 N1). The ADR-2010 row says "proposed, partial, INACTIVE" while the relay receipt machine is live (governance.rs:938, receipts.rs:301) — NF-10.8 N2. The relay wrangler comment "asserts the opposite: there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/" (wrangler.toml:10-13) versus auth.rs:185 — DD3.
- Failure: whoever handles an incident trusts the README: they believe writes are allowlist-gated, never configure RELAY_URL (whose blank shipped default already skips the relay-tag check, nip42.rs:168-172), audit the wrong trust behaviour, and skip the static admin var because the template comment says nothing reads it — compounding F-01 and F-11.
- Confidence: high — the contradictions are quoted verbatim.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — DD1, DD3, NF-10.8 N1/N2 (marked as drift, not as an operational hazard).

### F-09 — The material contradicts itself on whether the trust sweep can still commit a false audit row
- Topics: NF-03.9, NF-10.10, NF-11.12
- Evidence: NF-03.9: "Remaining: its unconditional audit INSERT can commit when the optimistic UPDATE changes zero rows; the conflict is detected after the batch. See … NF-10.10 for the residual case." NF-10.10, same date: "The audit INSERT is conditional inside the transaction; both result counts must equal one … The earlier false-audit probe … is not a current-code claim." NF-11.12 covers only keyset paging and counters, silent on the insert.
- Failure: if NF-03.9 is the true state, a concurrent edit between snapshot and batch commits an admin_log row recording a demotion that never happened — a false moderation fact in the table every admin mutation also writes to (NF-11.15 N4). If NF-10.10 is true, NF-03.9's own governing note is stale. Either way the audit trail's atomicity is unverified by this material.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; medium on which claim matches the code.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — twice, contradictorily (NF-03.9 "Remaining" vs NF-10.10 "not a current-code claim").

### F-10 — All relay sockets terminate on one singleton Durable Object whose per-IP rate limits evaporate on hibernation
- Topics: NF-03.1, NF-11.1, NF-11.2, NF-03.4
- Evidence: "The DO is a SINGLETON: every socket goes to get_by_name main — lib.rs:207" (NF-03.1 N1). "EVERY field except state and env is volatile in-memory cache. Hibernation wipes all of it" (NF-11.1); recovery restores only challenge, subscriptions and authed_pubkey per socket (session.rs:186-218, NF-11.2). `rate_limits` (mod.rs:85) is in the wiped set and absent from the recovery list; it backs admission step 1 (nip_handlers.rs:800).
- Failure: the whole forum's WebSocket plane is one object — a single failure/overload class for every member at once and a hard serialization point. Routine hibernation resets per-IP event rate limits, so a publisher cycling across wake boundaries is effectively unthrottled by the in-DO budget.
- Confidence: high on the singleton and volatility; the rate-limit reset is inferred from the recovery list's omission.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — both facts are stated plainly; neither is flagged as a hazard.

### F-11 — A by-the-book deployment finishes with no admin on the relay or the auth worker
- Topics: NF-08.1, NF-08.4, NF-08.7, NF-09.5 (S2)
- Evidence: forum.example.toml ships zeroed placeholder admin pubkeys that "FAIL validation by design" (forum.example.toml:66-72, NF-08.1 N3); ADMIN_PUBKEYS "is DECLARED in exactly one wrangler template — the search worker" and SETUP.md:119-123 never lists it (NF-08.7 N4); the auth-worker RELAY_DB id is "a ZERO placeholder that every deployment must override — shipped unusable on purpose" (NF-08.4 N3); SETUP's secret step covers two secrets (SETUP.md:127).
- Failure: the operator follows SETUP end to end and ends with no static admin anywhere on relay/auth and an empty whitelist — the first-admin APIs are unusable, so bootstrap requires out-of-band `wrangler d1` surgery the guide does not contain, and whoever performs it becomes an undeclared root of trust.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — S2 (NF-10.7), NF-08.7 N4/N5.

### F-12 — The cross-stack key-derivation contract and crypto vectors are guarded by scripts nothing runs and tests that soft-skip
- Topics: NF-09.3, NF-10.4 (O10), NF-09.6, NF-12.3
- Evidence: "neither anti-drift-lint.sh nor identity-vector-parity.mjs is invoked by ci.yml or audit.yml — both are manual" (NF-09.3 N2, S6). "The upstream-vector suite is soft-skipped, not failed, when fixtures are absent — nostr-bbs-core/tests/upstream_vectors/mod.rs:11 — and no workflow runs sync-fixtures.sh" (NF-10.4, O10). The e2e suites "are operator smoke tests, not CI gates — ci.yml runs neither" (NF-09.6 N1). derive_subkey is "a BYTE-FOR-BYTE cross-stack contract" (NF-09.3 N1; vector keys.rs:478, digest keys.rs:483).
- Failure: the ADR-2003 HMAC construction drifts between this repo and agentbox's JS mirror with no gate to catch it — device subkeys and identity silently diverge across stacks; meanwhile known-answer crypto vectors can be absent on every CI run without a red build, so a broken NIP-44/Schnorr path ships green.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — NF-09.3 N2, O10, S6.

### F-13 — A banned member can keep DMing every member: gift wraps are exempt from the ban gate by design
- Topics: NF-03.5, NF-03.6, NF-03.4
- Evidence: "Gift wraps are DELIBERATELY excluded from ban gating … The recipient-whitelist gate is what bounds them instead — nip_handlers.rs:56-65, exclusion visible in nip_handlers.rs:69-75" (NF-03.5 N2); the ban-gated kind list omits 1059 (NF-03.6); admission checks only the recipient (nip_handlers.rs:858-859).
- Failure: ban a harasser and their DM channel to every whitelisted member stays open — the wrap is signed by a fresh throwaway key, so no author-keyed enforcement can bind and none is attempted. Moderation's remaining lever is member-side reports, after the harm.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — NF-03.5 N2 frames it as deliberate; the operational consequence is unquantified.

### F-14 — Admin authority is split-brain: the auth worker unions three sources, the relay two
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-03.10, NF-02.6
- Evidence:

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem — nostr-rust-forum kit production incident

Scope note: everything below is drawn only from the diagrams-as-code distillation pinned at `verified_commit 66ca8d8c0b7d1471503f916c513089846aba27c3` (and `ebaf16c` for the kit-compatibility record). Every claim carries the citation the material gives, so an engineer can check it against the tree. Where the incident attribution required reasoning beyond what the material states, it is marked inferred. Ordering and concurrency are only asserted where the source diagrams assert them.

The three most likely root causes, ranked:

### R-1 — Zone encryption silently off: plaintext stored in zones members believe are end-to-end encrypted
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8, NF-09.4, NF-05.16, NF-03.12, NF-05.10
- Chain of events: 1. Operator defines zones with `encrypted = true` and non-public visibility in `forum.toml`; the validator accepts this (it only rejects `encrypted && visibility = public`, validate.rs:247). 2. The five workers are deployed from the templates — `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is declared in no wrangler `[vars]` block in any worker. 3. The relay's `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (zone_config.rs:127) evaluates false for every zone, so the `is_zone_ciphertext` write gate never fires (nip_handlers.rs:1157). 4. The client's `encryption_enabled()` is likewise off, so `ZoneWriter::prepare` takes the plaintext pass-through arm (store.rs:480-491) instead of "refuse with no key". 5. Months of confidential kind-42 content accumulate in plaintext in relay D1, readable by every read-cohort member and returned by relay text search (the client-side zk-tag discard at global_search.rs:733-742 only suppresses ciphertext hits). 6. When the gate is later enabled, only new writes are shape-checked; nothing retro-encrypts, and the exposure is discovered from the stored history.
- Evidence: NF-08.2 N4 states it outright: "`ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is read from env at zone_config.rs:86 but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker … a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF." NF-08.8 N7 repeats the own-binding pattern with no declared default. NF-05.16 N3 documents the dormant-gate client behavior (keys held keep decrypting; grants not re-opened until `encryption_enabled()` is true, zone_crypto/mod.rs:99). NF-03.12 CIPHER confirms the write gate is conditional on `is_encrypted`.
- Warning signs visible today: the explicit doc-drift note in NF-08.2 N4; the total absence of the variable from every template, unlike `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED` which at least ships `false`; the validator giving operators the belief (encrypted zones pass validation) while runtime gives them plaintext.
- Confidence: high that the silent-downgrade mechanism exists exactly as described; that it is *this* incident's root cause is inferred (medium).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Case-sensitive pubkey comparison defeats the self-review guard in the core governance aggregate (KF-6)
- Topics: NF-06.6, NF-06.13, NF-06.15, NF-10.11, NF-06.18
- Chain of events: 1. A key legitimately presents in two hex casings: NIP-98 returns `event.pubkey` verbatim and the event id is recomputed over that string, so both casings verify (governance_api.rs:98-100, NF-06.15 N1). 2. The agent registry canonicalises and was fixed for this class (NF-06.15), but the core aggregate was not: `record_decision`'s self-review guard compares pubkeys case-sensitively (governance.rs:1286, enforced at governance.rs:1738; supersession path governance.rs:1835). 3. An actor already admission-authorised for 31403 (admin or delegated reviewer — nip_handlers.rs:1054 via `response_admission` nip_handlers.rs:234) opens a case as `created_by` in one casing, then publishes the deciding 31403 under the other casing of the same key. 4. The equality check misses, `CaseError::SelfReview` never fires, and the actor decides their own case — including a Promote/Demote outcome (ADR-2013) that the downstream consumer applies.
- Evidence: NF-10.11 N6 (KF-6): "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim … the same class the registry closed in NF-06.15, left open in the core aggregate" (docs/security/known-findings.md:29). NF-06.6 note gives the enforcement sites. NF-06.18 N1 confirms an admin-flagged agent exists on the live deployment, so an authorised-but-self-interested decider is not hypothetical.
- Warning signs visible today: KF-6 sits in the known-findings ledger; the registry fix (NF-06.15) proves the codebase has already been bitten by exactly this class once; NF-02.10 and NF-06.17 N5 show other paths deliberately canonicalising case-insensitively.
- Confidence: high that the defect exists; that it is *this* incident's root cause is inferred (medium). Note: the supersession half of KF-6 fails closed (deny), only the self-review half fails open.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Preview-worker SSRF: denylist-only guard, no resolve-pin, no timeout, allowlist unset in the template
- Topics: NF-07.5, NF-07.6, NF-07.7, NF-10.7
- Chain of events: 1. A member posts a link; a client calls `GET /preview`. 2. The guard validates URL text (scheme, ports, private-range and metadata IP literals) but the Workers runtime exposes no resolve-then-pin primitive, so a hostname that resolves to an internal/private address at fetch time passes every text check — acknowledged in code (ssrf.rs:13). 3. The real mitigation, `PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, is not set in the template (wrangler.toml:13 declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN`). 4. There is no wall-clock timeout — only a 3-redirect cap and body-size caps — so a hanging origin ties the fetch open. 5. Extracted `og:title`/`og:description` (parse.rs:31, parse.rs:40) are returned to the requester, giving a read-back channel for anything the probe can reach.
- Evidence: NF-07.6 N3 ("NO wall-clock timeout"), N4 (denylist-only, DNS-rebinding-vulnerable, in-code acknowledgment, template does not set the allowlist); NF-10.7 S3 restates it as a deployment-shaped divergence; NF-07.7 shows the content read-back.
- Warning signs visible today: the vulnerability is acknowledged in the code's own comment; the mitigation variable ships unset; the missing timeout is labelled a divergence in the material itself.
- Confidence: high that the defect exists; that it is *this* incident's root cause is inferred (medium).
- Severity: high

---

Further latent defects noticed during the investigation:

### F-01 — Admin bootstrap gap plus a real third-party admin key shipped in a template
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-07.2, NF-10.6, NF-10.7
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is read by the auth worker (admin.rs:71) and relay (auth.rs:185) but declared only in nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33 — which ships a REAL 64-hex pubkey, the only non-placeholder value in any template; the relay template asserts the opposite (wrangler.toml:10-13); SETUP.md:119-123 never lists it.
- Failure: a by-the-book deployment has no static admin bootstrap on relay or auth (first admin only via manual D1 surgery); any deployment that keeps the search template's var gives an unknown third party admin over `POST /ingest` — arbitrary vectors and `publicLabels` injected into the member-facing search index.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DD3, S2, NF-08.7 N4/N5)

### F-02 — Governance read endpoints are effectively anonymous, and the reviewer aggregate is reconstructable
- Topics: NF-02.10, NF-06.9, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-2/KF-2a — the governance reads verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read (docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24).
- Failure: any key holder (never a member) enumerates cases, decisions and receipts; reviewer identities and activity patterns are reconstructed from the decisions read — moderator exposure / doxxing surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (known-findings debt, operator decision)

### F-03 — Schema DDL executed on every request, before CORS and authentication
- Topics: NF-08.5, NF-02.1, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-3 — `ensure_schema` runs its DDL on every request, before the CORS short-circuit and any authentication (docs/security/known-findings.md:26); NF-02.1 describes the bootstrap as per-cold-start (lib.rs:162), a milder claim — the two descriptions should be reconciled against the code.
- Failure: an unauthenticated attacker drives any route; each request issues the full idempotent DDL batch against D1 — row/write budget exhaustion, bill amplification, and a request-latency floor imposed ahead of every gate.
- Confidence: high (that the finding is filed; per-request vs per-cold-start needs code confirmation)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-3)

### F-04 — Moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey while the write gate resolves the owner
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-03.5, NF-03.6, NF-02.7, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-4/KF-9 — the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all (docs/security/known-findings.md:27, :44).
- Failure: with `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED` on (ships `false`, NF-02.7, S4), a banned or silenced member publishes kind-42 through a registered device key: the whitelist gate passes (owner is a member), the suspension/silence and ban gates pass (raw device key is not the banned principal) — ban evasion.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-4/KF-9)

### F-05 — decision_id is a 64-bit truncation of the event id and already a primary key
- Topics: NF-06.8, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-5 (docs/security/known-findings.md:28); changing it is a migration.
- Failure: two distinct 31403 event ids sharing a 64-bit prefix collide on the primary key; the second insert fails or the decisions are conflated — a recorded governance decision silently lost. Organic collision probability is negligible; an attacker would need ~2^64 targeted grinding, so this is an integrity edge, not a practical attack.
- Confidence: medium (defect filed; concrete failure mode inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-5)

### F-06 — Kind 31405 is both PanelRetired and the append-only governance audit log
- Topics: NF-06.1, NF-06.2, NF-10.11, NF-10.6
- Evidence: `KIND_GOVERNANCE_AUDIT_LOG` is numerically the same kind as `KIND_PANEL_RETIRED`, both 31405 (governance.rs:32, governance.rs:1226); the two roles are distinguished only by whether the caller supplies a `seen_audit_ids` set; a legitimate PanelRetired is validated under the append-only audit rules (docs/security/known-findings.md:43).
- Failure: a retirement event whose d tag is already seen on the audit path is rejected as a duplicate — a panel cannot be retired (availability of the governance surface); conversely an audit entry is indistinguishable from a retirement on the wire, so downstream consumers can misclassify.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-8, DD7)

### F-07 — Contradictory closeouts on the trust-sweep audit INSERT
- Topics: NF-03.9, NF-10.10, NF-11.12
- Evidence: NF-03.9's dated note says the residual is that "its unconditional audit INSERT can commit when the optimistic UPDATE changes zero rows; the conflict is detected after the batch … see NF-10.10 for the residual case", while NF-10.10 (same date) shows the INSERT conditional on `changes = 1`, both counts required to equal one, and five exact-SQL tests. Both cannot describe the current code.
- Failure: if NF-03.9 is live, a concurrently-edited whitelist row yields zero UPDATE changes but a committed audit row — a false demotion fact in `admin_log`. If NF-10.10 is live, the governing closeout and NF-03.9 are stale and the next engineer either re-fixes working code or mistrusts the sweep's audit trail.
- Confidence: medium (the contradiction is certain; which side is live is not determinable from this material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (dated Open/Drift notes on both sides)

### F-08 — Schema-repair results discarded, and the auth schema is still hand-pasted operator SQL
- Topics: NF-08.6, NF-09.5, NF-10.7
- Evidence: the two `ALTER TABLE events` additions run under `let _ =` so a real failure is silent (nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/lib.rs:731; the migration's own header comment carries them as hand-applied text, migrations/0009_events.sql:12-17); SETUP.md:47 still has the operator hand-run the auth schema SQL; governance tables are created twice by duplication.
- Failure: on a D1 created before migration 0009, a failed ALTER for `d_tag`/`received_at` is invisible — `save_event`, which writes both columns, then fails on every publish: a total write outage with nothing logged. On the auth side, a missed paste leaves endpoints failing at first use.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, NF-08.6 N4; S1 residual)

### F-09 — SESSIONS and KV are the same physical namespace bound twice
- Topics: NF-08.4, NF-10.3
- Evidence: nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:24 and :39 bind the same namespace id under two logical names; the material flags key-collision risk across the two uses (O11a, live).
- Failure: a key written through one binding collides with the other's keyspace — rate-limit buckets corrupting session-adjacent entries or vice versa; the failure mode depends on key formats and stays invisible until a collision pattern emerges in production.
- Confidence: high (fact); failure mode inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (O11a)

### F-10 — Silent hash-embedding fallback can query a model-built index in the wrong space
- Topics: NF-07.3, NF-07.4
- Evidence: if the Workers AI binding is absent or inference fails, `embed_texts` falls back to a deterministic 3-pass hash embedding, L2-normalised (embed.rs:80, embed.rs:115, embed.rs:121); the 409 model-drift guard compares the declared model, and the model string lives in KV — not the per-query embedding method actually used.
- Failure: an intermittent AI-binding failure mid-operation embeds queries in the hash space against a `bge-small-en-v1.5`-built RVF index — confidently wrong semantic neighbours returned with no error surfaced; search correctness silently degrades.
- Confidence: inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Application outcomes are a set, but the derived Ord invites a max-reduction
- Topics: NF-11.9, NF-11.10
- Evidence: the note at governance.rs:919-935 states the three application outcomes are mutually exclusive claims, the derived Ord ranks declaration order, and "a consumer that reduces them with max displays a success as a failure"; `ladder_rank` (governance.rs:999) is the sanctioned comparator.
- Failure: an operator dashboard or downstream consumer that folds Applied / NotApplied / AppliedManually with `max()` reports an applied decision as not-applied (or the reverse) — wrong manual intervention or rollback driven by the receipt API.
- Confidence: medium (the trap is author-stated; the mis-consuming client is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (authorial warning note)

### F-12 — Unlabelled requests fold to the medium default and skip the rationale gate
- Topics: NF-06.12, NF-06.13, NF-08.8
- Evidence: an entirely unlabelled request folds to `ESCALATION_DEFAULT_TIER` (governance.rs:525), declared `medium` in a var the template itself calls a SCAFFOLD whose authoritative schema is owned elsewhere (nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:48, NF-08.8 N4); the rationale requirement fires only at High/Critical (governance.rs:730-733).
- Failure: an agent omits task-property tags on both panel and request; the case lands at tier medium — no rationale recorded, weaker oversight — and a consequential action is decided with no reasoning trail, all "correctly" per the default.
- Confidence: high (mechanism); failure scenario inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (declared SCAFFOLD / drift note)

### F-13 — NIP-42 relay-tag check skipped whenever RELAY_URL is blank, which is how the template ships
- Topics: NF-03.2, NF-03.1
- Evidence: `own_relay_url` unset or blank skips the relay-tag check only — deliberately failing open on the defence-in-depth check (nip42.rs:168-172); the template ships `RELAY_URL` blank (nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:38).
- Failure: in a default deploy, AUTH events are no longer bound to this relay by tag; the per-session unpredictable challenge still binds the ceremony, but one guard is structurally off and a misconfigured deployment is indistinguishable from a correct one.
- Confidence: high (mechanism); low for practical exploitability
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (explicitly acknowledged fail-open rollout note)

### F-14 — The cross-stack custody derivation parity proof is manual, not gated by CI
- Topics: NF-09.3, NF-12.3, NF-10.2
- Evidence: neither `anti-drift-lint.sh` nor `identity-vector-parity.mjs` is invoked by ci.yml or audit.yml — both manual; only `validate-forum-config.sh` is wired in (.github/workflows/ci.yml:107, NF-09.3 N2, S6). `derive_subkey` is a byte-for-byte cross-stack contract with agentbox's JS mirror (keys.rs:253, vector keys.rs:478, digest keys.rs:483).
- Failure: a refactor of the Rust HMAC construction passes every CI gate; the agentbox JS mirror drifts; subkeys derived on either side diverge — identity or deposit-address derivation splits across the estate with nothing red until funds or identities mismatch.
- Confidence: high (the gate gap); failure inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (S6 / NF-09.3 N2 divergence)

### F-15 — The entire realtime relay plane is one Durable Object
- Topics: NF-03.1, NF-11.1, NF-11.17
- Evidence: every socket goes to `get_by_name "main"` (lib.rs:207); every DO field except `state`/`env` is volatile in-memory cache wiped by hibernation (relay_do/mod.rs:81-103); an eviction drops at most one window of pending activity reads (read_cache.rs N4).
- Failure: a single object hosts every session, cache and rate-limit table; one object-level fault, eviction storm or CPU overrun degrades the whole forum's WebSocket plane simultaneously, with no isolation between communities — and the D1 free-tier read budget has already been exhausted once under load before caching existed (read_cache.rs:5-15).
- Confidence: inferred (topology is stated; the failure reasoning is mine)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## ontomark / critical

### F-01 — The still-running VisionClaw build job ships the unfixed fail-open redactor and the frontmatter credential leak
- Topics: OM-00.2, OM-00.3, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-00.2 marks S1.1 ("prose redactor failed OPEN on a regex compile error") and S1.2 ("credential scan was body-only but render publishes frontmatter") as RESOLVED 8d654d1 — in ontomark. OM-00.3 EXT states: "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires" (`vault/src/projection.rs:229`, `vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160,200`), and OM-05.5 shows that job is live: `cargo build -p vault` then `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge --out output/vault` (`ontology-publish.yml:168-174`), with "ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires this job, gated, so it keeps running" (OM-05.5 n2).
- Failure: A regex compile failure in the legacy job silently skips private-link redaction and publishes unredacted private content into the pod resource pack; independently, a credential placed in any frontmatter value (title, sources) passes the body-only scan and is published verbatim. The "RESOLVED" labels in the register describe the ontomark copy only; production runs the other copy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (EXTERNAL in OM-00.3; the register's RESOLVED framing against the estate exposure is the contradiction)

### F-02 — Promotion is two renames with a window where `out` does not exist; a crash between them leaves no bundle at the published path
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-00.2
- Evidence: OM-03.5 N1 (an INVARIANT): "swap_in is two renames, not one: between them out does not exist" (`build/mod.rs:260-263,270-274`); the previous tree survives only as `.publication-retired-PID` (`mod.rs:256-262`). The module doc still says "promote atomically" (`mod.rs:12`) — DOC-DRIFT F5 in OM-00.
- Failure: The build process is killed (OOM, deploy restart, CI cancellation) after `out → .publication-retired-PID` but before `stage → out`: the bundle path consumers read (Loom's `promote_vault_build`, the visionGraph site pipeline) is empty until the next fully successful build clears the stale retired directory and re-promotes. Any reader hitting the rename window sees ENOENT even without a crash. All pre-promote refusals protect the old bundle; the promote step itself does not.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — DOC-DRIFT covers the word "atomic"; the absent-`out` window is stated as an INVARIANT and not flagged as a defect

### F-03 — `write_to(publish_out)` runs after promote, so the bundle and the publication tree can permanently hold different generations
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.4
- Evidence: OM-03.5 N2 (DIVERGENCE): "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation" (`build/publish.rs:349`, `build/mod.rs:913-915`).
- Failure: With `--publish-out quartz/content` (the visionGraph invocation, OM-05.5): bundle promote succeeds, then `write_to` fails on disk-full or permissions. The process exits failed, but `site-data` already holds generation N+1 while `quartz/content` serves generation N — the public site and the machine bundle disagree until the next successful build. There is no rollback of the already-promoted bundle.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.5 N2)

### F-04 — The compat flag named for link redaction silently disables the held-back directory rule, so held pages ship in machine artefacts
- Topics: OM-02.3, OM-00.1, OM-00.5, OM-05.6
- Evidence: OM-02.3 n1 (DIVERGENCE): "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private (held_back is only applied in the link path `ir/projection.rs:623`); a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it `build/publish.rs:216`". The switch is `--no-redact-private-links` (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:195-257,503`, `profile.rs:411`) — a name that promises one behaviour but toggles a second publication-boundary behaviour. The conformance harness itself runs this path (`conformance.rs:164,179`), and the legacy vault build "kept private links" (OM-05.5).
- Failure: An operator passes `--no-redact-private-links` to reproduce legacy bytes (or follows the parity recipe). A `public: true` page under `misc/` is excluded from `publish/` but appears in the scaffold index, page API, search index, OKF and potentially `ontology.ttl` of the same bundle: content the estate held back is published through the machine surfaces, inconsistently with the publish tree.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (KEEP S3.4, DIVERGENCE OM-02.3) — the flag-name versus blast-radius mismatch itself is unmarked

### F-05 — Stale ADR-2136 tells operators the machine bundle is `--vault all`; running it produces the public publish including `working/` markdown
- Topics: OM-06.5, OM-00.5, OM-05.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-06.5 A2: "agentbox ADR-2136 names AB-P6 as the private `--vault all` machine bundle, the value the public publish passes" (`ADR-2136:84`); A4 marks it DECIDED as `build --scope machine` (`DEPRECATION.md:462`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:140-141`) with ADR-2136:84 still stale. OM-05.2: `--vault all` sets `with_working_publish=true` (`main.rs:490-491`), staging public working pages into `publish/working` (`build/publish.rs:151-155`).
- Failure: An agentbox operator follows their own ADR and runs `ontomark build --vault all --out pod-bundle`: they get the public publish — working prose as markdown in `publish/working`, no machine-only plain records, no `scope` field — and believe they built the pod-resource pack. The bundle they distribute has neither the intended contents nor the intended shape, and `--scope machine` refuses `--vault all`, so the error never surfaces as an error.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN AB-P6 / DECIDED, DOC-DRIFT on ADR-2136:84)

### F-06 — Three divergent wikilink resolvers turn legal fragment links into phantom IRIs published in the ontology
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-01.5, OM-00.1, OM-04.2
- Evidence: OM-02.1 n1/n2 (DIVERGENCE): three resolvers — `Corpus::build` keeps `#fragment` and `.md` (`ir/model.rs:336-342`), `PrivateLinks::resolve` strips them (`build/redact.rs:210-229`), shacl's has no slug key and is case-sensitive (`shacl.rs:1201-1207`); "a fragment link redacted as private is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string". Turtle keeps every relation target "declared or not" (`turtle.rs:337-342`) while the tiers drop undeclared targets (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298,309-310`). Curated links diverge again: `target_slug` verbatim (`input/slug.rs:65-75`, `ir/model.rs:424`) versus `slugify` in the resolve fallback (`ir/model.rs:342`), differing "on kebab input with leading, trailing or doubled dashes" (OM-01.5 note3).
- Failure: A public page contains `[[Some Page#section]]`; the build resolver misses it, emits an edge to `namespace + some-page-section` — a class that does not exist — into `ontology.ttl`; the graph tiers silently drop it, so TTL and tiers disagree; a SHACL export resolves the same link a third way. A curated link with a leading-dash target produces an IRI no other derivation makes, dangling in every consumer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-02.1, OM-01.5, OM-00.1 LINK)

### F-07 — The attribution roster changes Turtle and the page API but not `content_digest`, so digest-keyed consumers serve stale data
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-04.1
- Evidence: OM-03.6 N1 (DIVERGENCE): "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest, which hashes pages, journals and the vocabulary only" (`build/mod.rs:621-626` vs `:861-879`).
- Failure: `attribution.yaml` is edited (an author added or reassigned). The published Turtle and page API change; `content_digest` does not. Any consumer that treats `content_digest` as the identity of what the build produced — caches, mirrors, audit comparisons — concludes nothing changed and keeps serving the attribution-stale artefacts. Two builds with identical digests now have different outputs, breaking the digest's contract as "what was read" versus what was emitted.
- Confidence: medium — the divergence is stated; which consumers key on `content_digest` alone is not shown in the material
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.6 N1)

### F-08 — NGG1 tiers silently lose relation identity and edges, and the one walk that assigns categories is untested for agreement
- Topics: OM-04.4, OM-04.7, OM-00.3, OM-00.5
- Evidence: OM-04.4 (DIVERGENCEs): all 12 relation keys become the same `EDGE_RELATION` byte and "the dedup key merges distinct relations" (`graph_tiers.rs:283,307-316`; `ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:142-144`, a published wire value — OPEN S3.1); "Edge to a target that is not a selected node is silently dropped" (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298,309-310`) with `stats.json` reporting declared against shipped; OM-04.7: "only the third, NGG1's ancestry walk, is untested for agreement" against the closure/Whelk pair that a build test does pin (`build/mod.rs:1543`).
- Failure: A consumer of the explorer tiers sees one edge where two distinct relations exist (e.g., `influences` and `depends-on` between the same pair collapse to one), never sees relations whose target fell outside the selection, and can be shown a wrong category ring — none of it signalled anywhere except a stats delta, and the category walk has no test that would catch it diverging from the closure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN S3.1, DIVERGENCE OM-04.4/OM-04.7)

### F-09 — The same class gets different labels and domains in different artefacts of one bundle; aliased domains are canonical in the tiers but raw in the Turtle
- Topics: OM-04.5, OM-04.7, OM-00.1
- Evidence: OM-04.5 (DIVERGENCE): scaffold label falls back to title (`indexes.rs:129`), closure to title then slug (`closure.rs:109-113`), NGG1 to the slug (`graph_tiers.rs:254-256`); domain is title-cased in search (`indexes.rs:328`), `unclassified` in the page API (`page_api.rs:147-148`), alias-folded in NGG1 (`graph_tiers.rs:81-90`), exact-match in WebVOWL (`webvowl.rs:91`). OM-04.7: "a class in an aliased domain lands in the canonical domain tier but carries the unfolded string in `ontology.ttl`" (`turtle.rs:302-304` vs `graph_tiers.rs:81-90`).
- Failure: A consumer joining page API records to tier nodes to Turtle subjects by label or domain gets mismatches for any record without an explicit label, any empty-domain page, and every aliased-domain class: one logical class presents as two domains across the bundle it ships in.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-04.5, OM-04.7)

### F-10 — The reasoner cap is a hard-coded 2000 with no configuration; corpus growth past it refuses every build
- Topics: OM-04.3, OM-05.3
- Evidence: OM-04.3: `reason_capped` with `RELEVANT_TRANSITIVE_CAP = 2000` (`reason.rs:473`); over the cap → `RelevantOverCap` → exit 2; and "the cap is the constant 2000 and `vault.toml` has no reasoner section" (`reason.rs:477`).
- Failure: The ontology grows past 2000 relevant existentials over transitive properties. Every subsequent build — validate-clean or not — refuses with exit 2 and there is no operational knob: the publication pipeline is down until a code change and a new release ship. This is a deliberate guard wired as a single point of failure for the whole pipeline.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the build-down failure mode is inferred from them
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — stated as an INVARIANT; the no-override operational consequence is not flagged

### F-11 — CLI contract drift: exit 2 unreachable from validate/gate/conflicts, `--stats` mislabelled, `--profile` silently ignored, and `--vault` defaults differ per verb
- Topics: OM-05.1, OM-05.3, OM-05.6
- Evidence: OM-05.3 n2 (DIVERGENCE): "validate, gate and conflicts never return 2 from their own code (only clap does), so refusal is distinguishable only on build" (`main.rs:363,426,470`) against the crate-doc 0/1/2 contract (`main.rs:5-13`). OM-05.1 (DIVERGENCE): `--stats` documented as "print the counts" but only forces JSON (`main.rs:214-216`); global `--profile` resolved for every verb but never consumed by `conflicts`, `repair bodies`, `repair fences` (`main.rs:437,585,629`). Unmarked bullet: `repair` defaults `--vault all` while validate/gate/conflicts/build default `knowledge` (`main.rs:100,117,130,204`).
- Failure: A pipeline branching on exit 2 ("refused, do not retry") cannot distinguish refusal from check failure for three of the six verbs. An operator relying on defaults runs `ontomark validate` and never validates `working/` — pages that `--vault all` publishes. A profile tuned for validation silently does nothing to `conflicts` output.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — exit-2, `--stats` and `--profile` carry DIVERGENCE markers; the per-verb `--vault` default asymmetry is unmarked

### F-12 — The emitted bundle diverges from contract C3 as stamped: two markers, extra fields, three unlisted files, and caches that must key on scope
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-03.7, OM-00.2, OM-06.5
- Evidence: OM-03.6 N2 (DIVERGENCE): "C3 as stamped shows one marker and no dirty or ontology_digest field, code writes two markers, both extra fields and scope on a machine bundle" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:137-138` vs `build/mod.rs:897,903-911`). OM-00 F10 (DOC-DRIFT): emitted but not in C3 — `api/pages/_domain-index.json` (`mod.rs:710`), `context/v1.jsonld` (`mod.rs:723`), `data/.generation.json` (`mod.rs:910`). OM-06.5 L4: "a consumer cache keyed on ontology_digest must also key on scope".
- Failure: A consumer, validator or audit written to C3-as-stamped rejects or silently ignores the second marker and the extra fields; strict artifact-list checks fail on the three unlisted files; a mirror that caches by `ontology_digest` alone cannot distinguish a public bundle from a machine bundle with byte-identical `data/` (which the design guarantees, OM-06.1) and serves the wrong scope.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, DIVERGENCE)

### F-13 — Every page is read twice, census then load, so classification and parse can run on different bytes
- Topics: OM-03.3, OM-01.2
- Evidence: OM-03.3 N2 (DIVERGENCE): "each page is still read twice, census then load, so a file edited between the two is classified on one text and parsed from another, a time-of-check gap the digest no longer widens" (`ir/projection.rs:117` vs `input/page.rs:452`).
- Failure: A page saved between the census read and the load read is counted under the old bytes and built from the new ones: `api/census.json` reports counts that do not match what was published (e.g., a page counted as private that publishes, or vice versa), and any downstream reconciliation against the census fails or masks a real discrepancy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.3 N2)

### F-14 — No mutual exclusion between concurrent builds sharing `out` and `publish_out` is described anywhere
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1, OM-06.3
- Evidence: Staging directories are PID-suffixed (`.publication-PID` `build/mod.rs:365-380`; `.publish-staging-PID` `build/publish.rs:334-349`), showing concurrent runs were anticipated, but the material describes no lock covering the pair of independent swaps at `mod.rs:913-916` (`promote(out)` then `write_to(publish_out)`).
- Failure: Two builds to the same destinations (scheduled plus manual, or CI racing a hotfix) interleave: the final `out` is whichever build swapped last, and the final `publish_out` is whichever `write_to` swapped last — they can come from different builds, yielding a bundle/publication pair that never existed as one generation, with exit 0 from both runs. Inferred: the material does not state exclusion, so a lock may exist outside the cited files.
- Confidence: low; inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Documented data flows that do not exist: the Whelk disjointness blocker, `parse_page`, `Report.unsatisfiable`, the census `rejected` counter
- Topics: OM-01.5, OM-01.6, OM-03.7, OM-02.5, OM-00.2
- Evidence: `input/consistency.rs:3-6` says both write paths read `whelk_inconsistent`, but "no production caller; only tests/probe_consistency.rs:119 builds it" (OM-01.5 note2) — disjointness reporting outside the build rests on the `check_disjoint_pair` heuristic ("refuse taxonomic, self, or no shared parent", `input/consistency.rs:100-131`). `parse_page` is documented as VisionClaw's entry point with no caller in this workspace (`input/page.rs:592-596`). `Report.unsatisfiable` is always empty and "cannot carry its documented meaning" (`build/mod.rs:110-114,930` vs `:642-648`). `Census.rejected` is constant 0 and the `conflicting_` counter is never bumped (`ir/projection.rs:61-62,147,171`).
- Failure: A reviewer or on-call engineer trusting the module docs believes reasoner-grade disjointness checking runs in the `conflicts` verb and that `Report.unsatisfiable` and `census.rejected` carry live signal; all three are dead, so real disjointness conflicts surface only when they are severe enough to make classes unsatisfiable at build time, and reports read as clean when they are not measuring anything.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, DOC-DRIFT, INVARIANT markers on each item individually)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Real exposure of the VisionClaw job output** — what actually consumes `output/vault` and the `pack-pod-resources.py` pack, how widely it is distributed, and the status of the four VC-60 retirement gates; needed to size F-01.
2. **Concurrency and supervision in production** — whether schedulers can run overlapping builds to the same `out`/`publish_out`, whether any host-level lock or serializing queue exists, and whether anything supervises or re-runs a promote that died between renames (F-02, F-14).
3. **The secret scanner's pattern set** — the 11 `CredentialKind` regexes and the frontmatter render format; whether multiline, quoted or anchored YAML values can evade the rendered-block scan, and the false-positive rate on the real corpus.
4. **Corpus contents** — whether any `public: true` page actually sits under `misc/`, whether fragment wikilinks, dashed curated targets, aliased domains or an `attribution.yaml` exist today; this decides whether F-04, F-06, F-07 and F-09 fire on real data or only on inputs not yet written.
5. **Loom's promotion crash recovery** — what happens to a `.promotion-in-flight` directory and the rvdb pair if the Loom process dies between its renames; the material shows the staging pattern but no recovery or cleanup story.

## ontomark / premortem

Post-mortem basis: the anomaly register and topic maps as verified at ontomark `ee9e04a`, VisionClaw `4806604`, VisionFlow `3abc1af`/`fa374b0`, visionGraph `360bc99`, agentbox `85136a1`. Every claim below carries the citation the register gives; anything I reasoned past the material is labelled inferred.

---

### R-1 — The deliberately kept-alive VisionClaw producer still shipped the unfixed SEV-1 credential and redaction faults into client-facing pod resources
- Topics: OM-00.3, OM-00.2 (S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3), OM-05.5, OM-06.5
- Chain of events:
  1. The estate runs two producers. VisionClaw's workflow builds with its in-tree `vault` crate, not ontomark (`ontology-publish.yml:168`), and ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires that job "gated, so it keeps running" (`ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment-one-producer-explicit-projections.md:11`).
  2. That crate still carries the faults ontomark closed only in itself at 8d654d1: S1.2, the credential scan that reads the body while the render publishes re-rendered frontmatter (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160`, `vault/src/build/publish.rs:200`); S1.1, the prose redactor that fails **open** on a regex compile error (`vault/src/projection.rs:229`); S1.5, gate/conflicts reading only the first vault under `--vault all` (`vault/src/main.rs:855-858`).
  3. An author commits a page with a token in a frontmatter value, or prose naming a private page. The legacy scan never sees the frontmatter value (S1.2); if the redactor hits a compile error it publishes unredacted instead of refusing (S1.1). The legacy build also "kept private links" (OM-05.5).
  4. The output feeds `pack-pod-resources.py` (`ontology-publish.yml:194`) — the bytes enter pod resources delivered under client custody.
  5. Detection fails twice: the job's own gate sees the first vault only (S1.5), and the fixed ontomark gates never run in this job ("this VisionClaw workflow never invokes ontomark", OM-05.5 n2).
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT states verbatim that "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires under its four gates"; the S1.1/S1.2/S1.5 rows give the inherited file:line origins above; OM-05.5 gives the build command and the pack-pod-resources consumer.
- Warning signs visible today: the register itself ranks these as SEV-1 publication-boundary faults and records them as live in the external job; ADR-2136:84 still carries the stale `--vault all` machine-bundle wording (OM-06.5 A2/A4), showing retirement tracking is already drifting.
- Confidence: high — the live faults, the delivery path to a client, and the absence of the fixed gates in that job are all stated; only the triggering edit is hypothetical.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Promotion is two sequential non-atomic swaps over two destinations; a failure or crash leaves split generations, or no bundle at all
- Topics: OM-03.5 (N1, N2), OM-03.1, OM-05.5
- Chain of events:
  1. visionGraph publishes with `ontomark build --vault all --out site-data --publish-out quartz/content …` (`publish.yml:158-163`), so one run writes two destinations.
  2. Nothing touches `out` until promote (OM-03.1 invariant, `build/mod.rs:913`); promote writes a sibling stage dir then `swap_in`: rename `out` → `.publication-retired-PID` if it exists, then rename stage → `out`. Between the two renames `out` does not exist (`build/mod.rs:256-277`, N1).
  3. Only after that does `publish::write_to` stage the publish tree beside its target and swap it in (`build/publish.rs:334-349`, `build/mod.rs:913-916`) — the stated N2 divergence: "a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree … the two no longer one generation".
  4. A crash or kill between the renames in step 2 is outside the restore path, which covers only a rename error (`build/mod.rs:270-274`); the old bundle's only copy sits in `.publication-retired-PID` until the next swap clears it (`build/mod.rs:256-262`).
  5. The deployment then serves mismatched halves: public markdown citing a generation the data bundle no longer matches (or briefly, no bundle), and nothing on disk reconciles the two trees.
- Evidence: `build/publish.rs:349` and `build/mod.rs:913-915` (order), `build/mod.rs:260-263` (window), `build/mod.rs:12` vs `:359-360` (DOC-DRIFT: module doc still says "promote atomically").
- Warning signs visible today: N2 is a labelled DIVERGENCE in the register; the conformance receipt compares bytes (`docs/HANDOFF.md:27-29`), never crash windows or cross-destination consistency.
- Confidence: high for the split-generation outcome (explicitly stated); the crash-between-renames missing-`out` consequence is inferred from the stated window and restore conditions.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The publication boundary is one rule enforced in several copies; pages and links that fall between the copies leak held or private identity into public artefacts
- Topics: OM-02.3 (n1), OM-02.5 (n5), OM-03.3, OM-02.4, OM-02.1 (n2), OM-00.1 (HOLD, LINK, RED)
- Chain of events:
  1. The hold-back rule exists twice with different scopes: `is_unpublished` (`_misc`, first component only — `input/page.rs:38`, `input/page.rs:583-588`) feeds `excluded_identities_of` and hence the collision check and the marker set (`ir/projection.rs:234`, `:546`, `:572`), while `held_back` (`_misc` plus `misc` at any depth — `build/publish.rs:130-142`) is what staging and the link path actually enforce (`build/publish.rs:216`, `ir/projection.rs:623`, `:668`).
  2. A page held only by the wider rule is forced private in the projection and skipped from publish/, but its identities are absent from the marker set and the collision check.
  3. A public wikilink naming that page is therefore not masked; the resolver's fallback fabricates `namespace + slugify(target)` (`ir/model.rs:342`), publishing the held page's title as a dangling edge in `ontology.ttl` and the indexes.
  4. Separately, the compat projection applies no hold-back at all: a `public: true` misc/ page enters every machine artefact while publish/ skips it (`ir/projection.rs:623` vs `build/mod.rs:612-616`, `build/publish.rs:216`), and the parity path itself runs redaction off (`conformance.rs:179`).
  5. And the publish tree's render redacts wikilinks only: `stage_redacted` takes PrivateLinks, not the prose Redactor (`build/publish.rs:194`, render at `:163-169`), while the Redactor is applied to records (`ir/projection.rs:377`) and re-applied to the api/markdown mirror (`build/mod.rs:786`) — so plain-text names of private pages reach the public site in one rendering and are masked in the other.
  6. The register's own canonical note says the goal is "one redacted page view … (SEV-4, open)" (OM-00.1 RED) — the unification never happened.
- Evidence: each gap is individually labelled in the material: OM-02.3 n1, OM-02.5 n5, OM-03.3 divergence, OM-02.4 divergence, OM-00.1 HOLD/LINK/RED subgraphs.
- Warning signs visible today: the DIVERGENCE labels themselves; three resolver copies and two hold-back lists ranked R1–R3 by blast radius on the publication boundary (OM-02.6).
- Confidence: gap 4 is stated (high); the narrow/wide marker mismatch is stated with the dangling-edge leak inferred from the stated fallback (medium); the publish-tree prose gap follows from stated signatures and redaction points but is not explicitly asserted (medium, inferred).
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Publish-tree markdown bodies are never marker-redacted, unlike the records and the api/markdown mirror
- Topics: OM-02.4, OM-03.4, OM-00.1 (RED)
- Evidence: `stage_redacted(vaults, links)` takes only PrivateLinks (`build/publish.rs:194`) and its render does "frontmatter re-rendered, links redacted, body verbatim" (`build/publish.rs:163-169`); the prose Redactor cleans records (`ir/projection.rs:377`) and is re-applied to the mirror (`build/mod.rs:786`); OM-00.1 RED names "one redacted page view … (SEV-4, open)".

- Confidence: medium (inferred from stated signatures and redaction points).
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — the SEV-4 item; the publish-tree gap itself is not named)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — The compat projection applies no hold-back: a public misc/ page enters ttl, indexes, page API and OKF but not publish/

- Evidence: "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private (held_back is only applied in the link path ir/projection.rs:623) … stage_redacted skips it build/publish.rs:216" (OM-02.3 n1); compat is selected whenever redaction is off (`build/mod.rs:612-616`) and `--no-redact-private-links` is a `Some(false)` profile override (`ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:503`, `profile.rs:411`); the parity test runs it (`conformance.rs:179`).
- Failure: any build run with redaction off — an operator reproducing the parity receipt, or a profile change — publishes held-back content into every machine artefact while publish/ omits it; consumers reconciling publish/ against ttl receive content the hold-back policy withholds.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — S3.4 KEEP)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — The marker set and collision check draw from the narrow hold list while staging enforces the wide one
