## sidestr-rs / critical

### F-01 — Released `sidestr-hitch` 0.1 contains a channel-cheating flaw: a commitment's owner can spend its own revocation leaf
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3, SR-01.1
- Evidence: 0.2.0 is breaking because "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`); the business section calls it "a hole in which a party could cheat with its own penalty path"; the snapshot invariant repeats that version-1 keys "let the owner spend its own revocation leaf" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`). Meanwhile `sidestr-hitch` is one of the eight *published* crates (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:30`, `../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`), so 0.1.x with this flaw is public.
- Failure: any 0.1 channel — including channels opened by third parties who pulled the published crate — can be cheated: a party broadcasts a stale commitment and uses its own penalty path to spend its revocable output, defeating the counterparty's punishment mechanism. The estate runs no channels ("Nobody in the estate runs one"), which contains but does not remove the exposure the published crate creates.
- Confidence: high that the flaw exists in 0.1 (stated); the exact theft mechanics are inferred from "cheat with its own penalty path".
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (described in the changelog and snapshot docs as the reason for the breaking change, but carries no Tension/Debt/Drift/Open marker and references no advisory).

### F-02 — No migration path off 0.1: 0.2 refuses version-1 snapshots, so vulnerable channels must be closed with the vulnerable binary
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.5
- Evidence: "only version 2 snapshots restore; a 0.1 (version 1) snapshot is refused … so such a channel must be closed with 0.1" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`); the header states "a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2" and "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version".
- Failure: an operator upgrades to 0.2.x while a 0.1 channel is open; the new binary refuses the recovery snapshot, so the channel cannot be watched, responded to, or cooperatively closed. The operator must keep the 0.1 binary — carrying the F-01 flaw — in production to close out old channels, racing a counterparty who may exploit that same flaw during the wind-down.
- Confidence: high (all elements stated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as an "Invariant" note, not flagged as a standing operational risk).

### F-03 — The entire channel system is inoperable as shipped: every timing-critical, money-bearing duty lives in a host that does not exist
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: the machine performs no IO and the host must supply peer transport, wallet funding, atomic snapshot storage, chain lookups and broadcasting (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:105`); the scheduler duties (retries, proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts) are the host's, only the constants are exported (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`); the host must persist each `ForwardRecord` before the add (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:108`, `:173`) and retry retryable actions (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`); claimable HTLCs go to chain only `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` blocks before expiry (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`) and claims are republished (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:578`). "The estate has no host for any of it (VisionClaw ADR-2111 is proposed and unimplemented)" (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-...md:95`).
- Failure: taking production responsibility means first building an unlisted subsystem. Any lapse in it — a missed tick, a broadcast not retried, a ForwardRecord not persisted before the downstream add — forfeits an HTLC at expiry or loses a penalty race; the kernel's safety properties are conditional on host code nobody has written or audited.
- Confidence: high on the gap; the specific loss mechanics are inferred from the stated timing and persistence duties.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in SR-04.5 covers the missing host); the money-loss consequence of the timing duties is unmarked.

### F-04 — The "reference oracle is the compatibility contract" claim is contradicted by deliberate divergences from the reference
- Topics: SR-03.2, SR-04.5, SR-04.2, SR-01.2
- Evidence: ADR-0001 makes the reference engine the contract (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-...md:27`) and the header says compatibility "is judged against pinned upstream revisions" (SR-01). Yet `sidestr-evm` "deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`), the port relocates Hitch's in-document timers to the host scheduler (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`), and the marked Tension has the port dropping off-curve points where Hitch sends a reject (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`).
- Failure: once Rust followers/producers are adopted under N-10 alongside the JavaScript engine that actually runs the estate, implementations that pass the same oracle suites still differ in durable-state timing, scheduling and wire edge behaviour — e.g. a Rust node silently drops where the engine replies reject (peer hang), or resumes from snapshots whose commit points differ from the producer's.
- Confidence: medium; divergences stated, production consequence inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the off-curve case is marked Tension; the `prepare`-commit and scheduler divergences are unmarked.

### F-05 — One supervisor and one JavaScript producer, shared across two chains: a single point of failure for the whole estate
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-01.3
- Evidence: agentbox supervises producer, mirror and faucet (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`); the producer "runs the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" (`...README.md:117`); the diagram shows a single `JP` node feeding both `sidestr:dreamlab` and `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4`; the Rust libraries are dashed as "native producer not deployed", with adoption open (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`).
- Failure: the agentbox supervisor or the single JS producer process dies → both chains stop producing blocks simultaneously; no failover, second producer or independent signer set is described anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: medium; single-instance topology is shown, the halt consequence is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — SR-01.4's Open covers "deploy and prove a native producer"; the shared-fate, single-instance aspect is unmarked.

### F-06 — Mempool eviction permanently discards accepted transactions with no requeue or notification
- Topics: SR-02.4
- Evidence: on admission/validation disagreement "the builder walks the mempool in order and removes transactions that admission accepted but current block state rejects" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:874`), matched by exact bytes (`state.rs:759`). The contrast — immature locks are turned away at admission so "they can be submitted again once their height arrives instead of being evicted from a produced block" (`state.rs:631`) — implies eviction is terminal and resubmission is the sender's problem. No parking, retry or notification is described.
- Failure: rule state changes as earlier candidates enter a block; a previously accepted transaction is silently evicted; automated payment flows stall because nobody resubmits, and the operator has no signal that valid-intent transactions vanished.
- Confidence: medium; terminal-drop semantics inferred from the stated contrast.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-07 — A close that gets reorganised away still kills the channel: `un_spend` never reopens it
- Topics: SR-04.4
- Evidence: "`un_spend` undoes a reorganised spend without reopening the channel" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:333`).
- Failure: the counterparty's unilateral close confirms, the node reacts (claims, penalties), then a reorganisation removes the close; the channel is un-spent but remains permanently closed. Funds sit behind delays, capacity is destroyed by a transaction that no longer exists, and the parties must fund a new channel — triggered by chain reorganisation, not misbehaviour.
- Confidence: medium; the non-reopening behaviour is stated, the cost/downtime impact is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-08 — The second chain, `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4`, is shown with a producer but no mirror and no faucet
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: the SR-01.4 diagram draws `M["mirror"] --> D` and `F["Rust faucet"] --> D` only; `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:11`, sealed 2 Oct) receives only the producer edge `JP --> D2`. No mirror or faucet node exists for it anywhere in the material.
- Failure: txbt4's history lives only in the producer; loss of the producer's store loses the chain's data with no mirror copy, and new participants cannot obtain funds because no faucet serves the chain — the estate's service set works for one chain but not two.
- Confidence: medium; based on diagram edges — the prose does not affirmatively deny a txbt4 mirror, but none is shown or cited.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-09 — Set-aside and pending states escalate to protective unilateral closes
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.3
- Evidence: a collided or rejected signed state "is kept as an alternative whose HTLCs stay bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent by something else" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:20`, `src/protocol/machine.rs:561`); while an update is pending "nothing is forwarded, settled or announced and no further update is accepted or proposed" (`mod.rs:27`); the router tick then triggers "protective force close when only a set-aside state binds" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:578`, `:570`).
- Failure: a lost message or an update collision — no malice required — leaves forwards bound only by a set-aside alternative; the next router tick force-closes the channel: delayed funds, on-chain fees, lost capacity, and a service outage caused by the protocol's own recovery heuristic.
- Confidence: medium; escalation path stated, blast radius inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-10 — The activation-gate records contradict each other on whether source parity is complete, and on the published-crate count
- Topics: SR-01.3, SR-01.1
- Evidence: VisionFlow "records source implementation as partial" (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:115`) while "the master board closes source parity in N-9" and annotates the releases on that closed row (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:100`, `:114`) — same estate, opposite statuses. In the same file, the SR-01.3 diagram says "Published: seven reusable crates released" while SR-01.1 says "Eight published crates" and eleven members (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`).
- Failure: SR-01.3's own invariant says source evidence may promote implementation status but not activation status — yet the records that gate promotion disagree about implementation status itself. An operator reading ADR-2012 stalls adoption; one reading only the board trusts a closure the canon record does not reflect.
- Confidence: high on the contradictions (both cited within the same topic file).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no — the README and ADR-0001 drifts are marked Drift; these two contradictions are not.

### F-11 — The sealed live chains can never activate any of the validated rules: assets, pools, markets and EVM are permanently inert on the estate
- Topics: SR-02.4, SR-03.4, SR-01.4
- Evidence: the sealed `sidestr:dreamlab` document "reaches `containmentDigest` without a `rules` field" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:5`, `:28`), likewise no `evm` rule (SR-03.4 Open); both chains are sealed, so the documents cannot change.
- Failure: delivering any SR-02 or SR-03 capability requires sealing a brand-new chain and migrating users to it — the operator inherits capability debt that cannot be paid on the chains they run, and every feature launch becomes a chain launch.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in SR-02.4 and SR-03.4).

### F-12 — The distillation's own verification commits disagree across files
- Topics: SR-02 front-matter vs SR-01, SR-03, SR-04 front-matter
- Evidence: SR-02 pins `visionflow: e49fe42477f9296c37ee71afb941ffb869d8ca74`; SR-01, SR-03 and SR-04 pin `visionflow: 62d16e02fe3bdd5551e4433b2d42552ec93adb12`. Sidestr-rs is `dc8e1ca` in all files.
- Failure: the topics were verified against different snapshots of the decision records; a reader cannot tell which cross-file statements are co-temporal — e.g. the N-9/N-10 board state SR-02 cites may not be the state SR-04 cites — so any cross-topic conclusion is unverifiable from this material.
- Confidence: high on the mismatch; medium on its impact.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-13 — Stale operational documents (marked): README crate table and ADR-0001 reference count
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-01.2
- Evidence: the README "still lists seven published crates and no teller row" against eleven members with `webledgers-teller` publishable (`../sidestr-rs/README.md:41`, `../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`); ADR-0001 says CI pins "all four reference repositories" but the workflow pins six — spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-...md:27`, `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`), with Reef repinned `648487a`→`2bd3cb8` (`ci.yml:25`) and the teller added at `7c00cea` (`ci.yml:30`).
- Failure: an engineer onboarding from the README gets the wrong crate set and publish status; an auditor working from ADR-0001 misses two of the six oracle sources, and a silent oracle repin changes what "compatible" means without the contract record moving.
- Confidence: high (both stated).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, in SR-01.1 and SR-01.2).

### F-14 — The 1 sat = 1 gwei mapping leaves sub-gwei EVM value unrepresentable on withdrawal
- Topics: SR-03.1
- Evidence: "One sat maps to one gwei inside the overlay" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:5`); the withdrawal invariant only requires the coinbase to pay "the required sidechain satoshis". No rounding or dust rule for sub-gwei balances appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: EVM state accumulates wei amounts below 1 gwei that can never be paid out in sats; contract balances and coinbase payouts cannot be reconciled exactly, and stranded dust grows over the life of the chain.
- Confidence: low — the mapping is stated, the rounding/dust behaviour is not shown.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-15 — Channel transactions can be built under the Knots unified sighash, tying funds to a fork client's consensus
- Topics: SR-04.1
- Evidence: the crate builds funding, commitments and HTLC paths "for both ordinary BIP-341 and the Knots unified sighash" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:7`, `:22`).
- Failure: any channel constructed in the Knots mode produces transactions valid only under a fork client's signature rules; if the estate's chains or watch infrastructure ever run standard consensus, those transactions — including penalty and claim paths — become invalid and the funds are stuck.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the dual mode is stated, but the material never says which mode the estate chains use or what selects it.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Mempool and producer-state durability: whether the JavaScript producer or the Rust core persists the mempool across crash/restart, and what exactly is lost when it dies.
2. The production chain's trust topology: how many signers or validators the JS engine has, whether the producer's baked pins match CI's (`blaketestnode f1da4a6`), and what failover exists besides the single agentbox supervisor.
3. Host-side custody design behind proposed ADR-2111: where channel snapshots (which contain the signing key, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`) would be stored, key management, and access control for the reserve attestations.
4. Whether `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` actually has its own mirror and faucet instances somewhere the SR-01.4 diagram does not show.
5. The unspecified rule edges: the market resolver's trust model and proof (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:202`), and EVM withdrawal rounding/dust handling for sub-gwei values.

## sidestr-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: client-loss production incident

Prepared twelve months after the incident from the SR-01–SR-05 distillation at the verified commits. Nothing else about the incident is available to this document, so the root causes below are ranked hypotheses grounded in defects the material already records; every claim carries its citation, and "inferred" marks where reasoning extends past what the material shows.

---

### R-1 — Channel funds lost through an incomplete host: an unpersisted forward, an unticked deadline, or an unbroadcast claim
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Chain of events:
  1. A service embeds `sidestr-hitch` and supplies a partial host — relay I/O and wallet funding, say — because no reference host exists to copy ("The estate has no host for any of it"; ADR-2111 proposed and unimplemented).
  2. The router emits a `Forward` action; the host sends the downstream `add` before durably persisting the ForwardRecord the action carries.
  3. The process crashes and restores only the channel snapshot; the router's in-memory forward is gone.
  4. The downstream peer reveals the preimage; the router no longer knows the forward and never emits the upstream settle.
  5. The upstream HTLC times out on-chain; the operator paid downstream and collected nothing upstream — direct sat loss. Variants with the same root: the host never ticks, so the `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` deadline passes before the claim is broadcast; or `watch_outputs` republishing never runs and a claim never confirms.
- Evidence: host duties enumerated with no implementer at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:70`, `:105`, `:121`; the SR-04 summary states the estate has no host. Forward durability contract: record created before the add, host persists it — `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:7`, `:108`, `:173`; atomic persist-or-restore-previous at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`. Deadline machinery requiring a tick: `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:578`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:578`. SR-04.5 states 0.2.0 *widened* the host boundary by the scheduler and chain-lookup duties. Retry is the host's job: `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`.
- Warning signs visible today: SR-04.5's diagram lists seven host-supplied boxes, none of which exists; the Open marker records that no estate host supplies the complete boundary (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`, `:114`); the atomic-persist precondition at `protocol/mod.rs:30` is a contract with no counterparty.
- Confidence: high that the boundary gap is real and money-bearing; the specific loss path (forward vs deadline vs claim) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The 0.1→0.2 breaking migration: version-1 channels either strand or are stolen through the single-party revocation key
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Chain of events:
  1. Channels are opened under hitch 0.1, whose revocation construction "let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf."
  2. The service upgrades to 0.2.x. Restore refuses version-1 snapshots outright, so live 0.1 channels cannot be operated by the 0.2 host.
  3a. If 0.1 can no longer be run, the channels are unmanageable; recovery depends on counterparty cooperation or on funding/unfunded timeouts the host no longer schedules — funds stranded.
  3b. If 0.1 channels keep running somewhere, a counterparty broadcasts an old commitment and sweeps its own revocation leaf immediately; the honest peer's penalty path is beaten and the client's channel balance is taken.
  4. Either branch loses or strands client funds, and closure requires the old version per the recorded caveat.
- Evidence: the flaw and fix — "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" (SR-04 summary); `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`. Breaking commitments — "Commitments now use a two-party revocation key, so the transactions differ and a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2" (SR-04 summary). Snapshot refusal: `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`. Business caveat: "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version" (SR-04). Two-party key construction: `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:740`, `:753`; the oracle expects an owner self-spend to be refused at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/tests/oracle.rs:465`.
- Warning signs visible today: both the theft flaw and the hard snapshot refusal are recorded; nothing in the material shows a 0.1 close-out tool, a migration path, or any process for draining 0.1 channels before an upgrade.
- Confidence: high that the defect pair is exactly as documented; medium, inferred, that it was this incident's mechanism (it requires 0.1 channels to have been live in the intervening year).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Cross-engine divergence: the live chain is produced by the pinned JavaScript engine while the Rust port carries recorded departures from the reference
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.4, SR-03.2, SR-04.5
- Chain of events:
  1. The live chains are produced by the upstream JavaScript engine under agentbox (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`); the Rust workspace is a validated library, with a Rust faucet and mirror already in the supervised estate.
  2. A Rust producer/validator is enabled over the year — the open item invites exactly this ("deploy and prove a native producer") — while the port carries deliberate departures: the EVM rule "commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`), and Hitch drops an off-curve point without the `reject` the reference sends (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`).
  3. A block or message lands in a disagreement region — reference prepare-path state effects, or a peer's reject-driven retry behaviour — and the two engines treat it differently.
  4. The Rust component and the JS producer hold different views; the mirror serves a divergent tip or the faucet pays against a block the reference engine later rejects.
  5. Client value moves on one view and is unwound on the other, while both sides point at their own green suites.
- Evidence: the contract itself — "a green Rust-only test suite cannot prove wire or consensus parity on its own" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`). The EVM departure: SR-03.2, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`. The Hitch departure: SR-04.5, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`. Native producer not deployed: SR-01.4 diagram and its Open. The chain document constrains nothing about which engine is authoritative — it reaches `containmentDigest` with no `rules` field (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:28`). Parity is proven only over regenerated fixture shapes (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:186`).
- Warning signs visible today: two deliberate departures already recorded against a contract that says the reference defines compatibility; the open deployment invitation with no stated equivalence criteria beyond fixture parity.
- Confidence: medium; the divergences are stated, the incident path is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — README understates the published surface: seven crates, no teller, against eleven members
- Topics: SR-01.1
- Evidence: `../sidestr-rs/README.md:41` lists seven published crates and no teller row; `../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:3` names eleven members and `Cargo.toml:43` makes `webledgers-teller` publishable.
- Failure: an integrator or auditor working from the README omits the teller crate from dependency review and threat modelling; a client-facing service consumes an unreviewed published component.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-02 — The compatibility ADR records four reference repositories; CI pins six
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: `../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27` says "all four"; `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14` pins spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller.
- Failure: an engineer reproducing the oracle environment from the ADR validates without Reef (wallet BIP 21) or teller (JCS) coverage and signs off a parity claim on half the oracles.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-03 — The channel snapshot is full key material and its protection is a convention, not an enforcement
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: the snapshot contains "the signing key, the revocation basepoint and per-state secrets, and preimages" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`); "Snapshot storage must be protected as wallet key material" (`:24`). No host exists to uphold this (SR-04.5 Open).
- Failure: a host persists snapshots in an ordinary database, log or backup path; exfiltration yields the channel signing key and preimages — the channel can be signed for and its HTLCs claimed by the attacker.
- Confidence: high that the exposure is stated; the exfiltration scenario is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Finality gate plus unguaranteed relay delivery: a lost `revoke` freezes the channel while HTLC clocks run
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.5
- Evidence: until the last reveal "nothing is forwarded, settled or announced and no further update is accepted or proposed" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:27`, `:4`); peer messages are host-carried kind-23600 Nostr events (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:105`); retries are the host's ("Hitch tries twenty times", `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`).
- Failure: the relay drops or delays the final `revoke`; the receiver can neither settle nor fail the pending HTLC in-channel; only the tick's `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` chain path (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`) rescues it, and only if the host ticks and broadcasts — otherwise the offered amount expires to the counterparty.
- Confidence: medium, inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Off-curve point silently dropped instead of rejected, diverging from the reference peer
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.5
- Evidence: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check; the port records this as a deliberate, tested departure" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`).
- Failure: a JavaScript counterparty waits on a reject that never arrives; its retry and unfunded timers fire while the Rust side has discarded the peer; opening attempts wedge until the host's — unimplemented — scheduler rescues them.
- Confidence: high that the departure is as stated; the stall scenario is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-06 — Set-aside alternative states bind HTLCs with no expiry of their own; the only exit is a force close
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.3
- Evidence: a collided or rejected signed state is "kept as an alternative whose HTLCs stay bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent by something else" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:561`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:20`); the router's tick "settles, fails or force-closes forwards whose downstream state is lost or held only by a set-aside alternative" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:570`, `:578`).
- Failure: a hostile or buggy peer repeatedly triggers collisions, parking set-aside states; each bound HTLC either forces an on-chain close (fees, delayed funds, client-visible outage) or, without a ticking host, locks value indefinitely.
- Confidence: medium, inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Claim timing margins assume timely host broadcast; no fee-bumping behaviour is stated anywhere
- Topics: SR-04.4
- Evidence: the tick takes a claimable HTLC to chain `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` blocks before expiry (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`); claims are "republished after six blocks" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:578`); broadcasting is a host duty (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`).
- Failure: host batching or a fee spike delays the claim past expiry; the counterparty times out the HTLC on-chain; the preimage is known but the sats are gone. Nothing in the material describes RBF or fee-escalation for time-critical claims.
- Confidence: medium, inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Refund redeems "half a sat each"; odd collateral cannot halve
- Topics: SR-02.3
- Evidence: split "locks collateral and mints YES plus NO"; refunding redeems "paired outcomes at half a sat each" (SR-02.3 diagram, transitions at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:260`). No even-collateral admission rule appears in the material.
- Failure: a market opened with odd sat collateral reaches refunding; the last sat is unpayable or the rounding direction differs from the reference's behaviour — an accounting divergence inside the rule the asset overlay exists to keep conservative.
- Confidence: low, inferred (the rule is stated; the odd-amount gap is my reading)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Overlay ordering is consensus but no chain document expresses it
- Topics: SR-02.1, SR-01.3
- Evidence: "overlay order is consensus behaviour and cannot be rearranged as an implementation detail" (SR-02.1 Invariant); the compositor installs assets before pool before markets (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/overlays.rs:65`); the only sealed chain documents shown carry no `rules` field at all (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:28`).
- Failure: when a rules-bearing chain is activated, ordering lives only in crate versions; a rule added between releases (the bridge rule is the expected next one) silently defines new consensus, or two producers on different crate versions split the chain.
- Confidence: medium, inferred

- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Admission judges maturity "at the next height," and the admission/block disagreement window is acknowledged but its production-time ordering is unstated
- Topics: SR-02.4
- Evidence: admission refuses locks not matured "at the next height" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:721`, `:628`, `:717-728`); "when admission and block validation still disagree, the builder walks the mempool in order and removes transactions that admission accepted but current block state rejects" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:874`); eviction matches exact bytes (`:759`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/tests/eviction.rs:16`).
- Failure: a produced block lands at a height other than the one admission assumed; admitted transactions become invalid in-block; late eviction forces block reassembly (stall) or, if ordered wrongly, seals a transaction the reference engine rejects.
- Confidence: low, inferred (the window is stated; the production-time ordering is not)

- Topics: SR-05.1, SR-05.2
- Evidence: the statement fixes a "final origin tip" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:351`, `:499`); duplicates are refused only within one attestation via sorted ids (`:499`); "Neither crate implements or activates the consuming `bridge` rule" (SR-05 summary; `../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0003-keep-reserve-attestations-origin-neutral-and-private.md:32`).
