## solid-pod-rs / critical

### F-01 — Git smart-HTTP push authorizes only at the pod root: per-resource WAC, sidecar Control elevation, the lockout guard and quota do not apply to the git write path
- Topics: SP-06.12, SP-04.5, SP-04.6, SP-06.6
- Evidence: SP-06.12 shows exactly one authorization on the whole smart-HTTP surface — "enforce against the POD-ROOT container ACL" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4313), mode chosen by `is_write` (solid-pod-rs-git/src/service.rs:127) — after which the CGI (solid-pod-rs-git/src/service.rs:354) writes the repository. The sidecar-elevation rule (reading/writing an `.acl` demands Control of the protected resource, solid-pod-rs/src/wac/mod.rs:252), the lockout guard (runs only in the LDP PUT/POST/PATCH handlers, solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:705) and the quota reservation (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2354) all live in LDP handlers; none appears on the git path. The material itself states the smart-HTTP surface is delegable ("history rewriting and discard are not delegable to a Control holder, **unlike this smart-HTTP surface**", SP-06.12 note, lib.rs:3665).
- Failure: A collaborator granted acl:Write on the pod root pushes a commit replacing `/private/.acl` with one granting themselves Control, plus edits to any resource regardless of its own sidecar. Write becomes Control over the whole pod; the push also bypasses quota entirely and can resurrect deleted content from history.
- Confidence: medium — the single pod-root check is stated; that nothing finer runs inside the CGI is inferred from no further gate being shown.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — MCP carries a reproduced CRITICAL finding (anonymous reads / WAC sidecar bypass) whose only mitigation is a default-off flag
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-08.15, SP-10.8, SP-02.3
- Evidence: README status records "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass" as a reproduced CRITICAL finding (SP-08.14 note; SP-10.8 F2, lib.rs:370). The sole mitigation is that MCP is off by default (main.rs:292) — one CLI flag away — and the tool catalogue includes `read_resource`, `write_acl` and `call_remote_pod` (mcp/tools.rs:175, :583, :677).
- Failure: An operator enables `--mcp` for its advertised agent-tooling purpose; anonymous clients read private resources and sidestep ACL sidecars on a surface that also exposes ACL writes and a federation proxy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (README audit finding quoted at SP-08.14 and SP-10.8 F2)

### F-03 — COPY never checks acl:Read on the source: any write grant anywhere exfiltrates any resource
- Topics: SP-03.8, SP-02.11, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "COPY authorises Write on the DESTINATION only — it never checks acl:Read on the SOURCE" (lib.rs:2563/2565, SP-03.8 DIVERGENCE; window W6). The source is fetched server-side (lib.rs:2585/2591) and COPY is registered unconditionally by raw method bytes (lib.rs:4778, SP-02.11) — live in a default build.
- Failure: A principal with Write on any container of the pod COPYs a private sibling into their own container and reads the body. Confidentiality bypass requiring nothing but an ordinary write grant and a guessed path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, SP-03.8; W6, SP-10.4)

### F-04 — Glob GET merges children that carry stricter ACLs; the index.html branch is the same class, unmarked
- Topics: SP-03.9, SP-03.2, SP-10.4
- Evidence: The glob gate is acl:Read on the folder only (lib.rs:2640), then every turtle/n-triples/n3 child is concatenated into one body (lib.rs:2667): "A child carrying its own, stricter acl sidecar is still included in the merged body" (SP-03.9 N2; W7). Same class, unmarked: container GET serves the `index.html` child "verbatim" on container Read alone (lib.rs:1248, SP-03.2) with no child-sidecar check shown.
- Failure: A principal granted Read on a folder receives full bodies of children whose own ACLs deny them; likewise a sub-container's index.html under a stricter sidecar. Default build, no flags.
- Confidence: high for the glob half (stated); medium (inferred) for the index.html sibling
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes for glob (DIVERGENCE SP-03.9 N2; W7); no for index.html

### F-05 — NIP-98 and DPoP replay protection is process-local: every restart and every second replica reopens the replay window
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-05.7, SP-10.4, SP-10.6
- Evidence: The freshness window is two-sided 60 s (nip98.rs:28, SP-05.2); the single-use store "loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL" (SP-05.5 trait note; defaults 120 s / 10 000 entries, replay.rs:78-86); "a second replica shares nothing" (W3). The DPoP jti cache is the same shape (60 s / 10 000, replay.rs:55/59). D6: the seam has exactly one implementor (replay_store.rs:90).
- Failure: An intercepted NIP-98 event replayed against a second replica — or within one TTL after any restart, which rolling deploys guarantee — executes the write as the victim. Same for DPoP proofs. Holds only in single-replica, never-restarted deployments.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (W3; process-local note SP-05.5; D6)

### F-06 — Payment state is non-atomic behind a process-wide lock, and the whole /pay/* surface has no off switch
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-07.14, SP-10.4, SP-10.8
- Evidence: PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK is a process-wide mutex held across replay-check → mutate → persist because "The storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive" (lib.rs:200, SP-07.15); "Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock" and the README records "non-atomic payment state" as a reproduced critical finding (W4; F4). The /pay/* family registers unconditionally with no feature flag (lib.rs:4625, SP-07.14 N2). Crash recovery retains — with only a warning — any intent whose debit has no receipt, because "the ledger cannot restore a debit it has no receipt for" (pay.rs:246).
- Failure: Two replicas (or a crash mid-mutation) double-credit or double-spend the ledger, order book or pool; a crash between debit and receipt strands funds silently; an operator who heeds the README warning still cannot turn the surface off.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (W4/F4; DIVERGENCE SP-07.14 N2; README warning)

### F-07 — Breaking payment changes shipped while consumers still call the deleted surface, and one consumer runs a pre-fix version line
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-07.17
- Evidence: `/pay/.deposit` now returns 501 for non-MRC20 bodies (pay.rs:467, SP-07.17), and ADR-2008 records that agentbox "still proxies {txo_uri, amount_sats} … and will surface the new 501 once its build exercises an alpha.11-or-newer binary" (ADR-2008:151-152) — while agentbox's Nix pin is now v0.5.0-alpha.12 (SP-09.7). VisionClaw consumes 0.4.0-alpha.15, "the pre-pivot line" (SP-09.7), and ADR-2008 D8 lockstep adoption "is still listed as not built".
- Failure: agentbox deposits fail with 501 against the currently pinned binary today. VisionClaw's embedded pod may lack the recorded security fixes (sidecar elevation P0-2/P0-3, replay capacity refusal) — the estate runs two divergent authorization stacks and nothing enforces convergence.
- Confidence: high for the agentbox 501; medium (inferred) for the VisionClaw skew — which fixes 0.4.0-alpha.15 contains is not stated
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt bullets, SP-09.7; Open, D8)

### F-08 — One valid key can exhaust the replay store and fail-closed the entire authentication surface
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-05.1, SP-10.10
- Evidence: At capacity with no expired entry the store returns CapacityExhausted and REFUSES the credential (replay.rs:275, SP-05.4). `check_and_record` runs after successful Schnorr verification (SP-05.1 order), so a holder of one valid key can mint unlimited fresh, unique, correctly-signed event ids, each consuming a slot; defaults cap at 10 000 / 120 s (replay.rs:78-86) ≈ 83 auth/s. SP-10.10 B5 notes the bound "fails the REQUEST" but never that it is attacker-fillable.
- Failure: One authenticated attacker (or one leaked key) at ~100 req/s fills the store and keeps it full: every NIP-98 authentication pod-wide fails closed until the flood stops plus one TTL. The DPoP cache (SP-05.7) has the same shape.
- Confidence: high (mechanism fully stated; exploitability is a direct implication)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — The anchoring subsystem cannot be operated: storage faults read as "no trail", genesis is unreachable, errors mislead, and concurrent anchors double-spend
- Topics: SP-07.12, SP-07.3, SP-07.20, SP-07.11
- Evidence: `load_trail` maps every storage read error to Ok(None) (trail_store.rs:94-100; copied at :186), so a transient fault is indistinguishable from "no trail" and `build_anchorer` returns None, reported as 'not configured' (SP-07.12 Debt). The handler still answers "must have a minted trail" though build_anchorer no longer requires a pay-token (prov.rs:383 vs :128, SP-07.3 N4 Drift). Nothing outside tests calls GitmarkAnchorer::genesis (mempool.rs:943) and no route/CLI/boot writes a git-mark trail file (trail_store.rs:191) — the alpha.12 git-mark path is unreachable from the product (SP-07.3 N5 Open). `anchor` holds no lock across load→advance→broadcast→save (mempool.rs:994, SP-07.20 Debt). The default explorer is public testnet4 mempool.space, one URL, no fallback (mempool.rs:60, D7).
- Failure: A storage blip silently suppresses anchoring and misdiagnoses it as configuration; inferred: because genesis's "trail already stored?" check reads through the same Ok(None) mapping, a read fault during genesis could overwrite an existing trail file and fork the on-chain history; two concurrent anchors double-spend the head after the first has saved; new pods cannot start a git-mark trail at all from this repository.
- Confidence: high for the stated faults; medium (inferred) for the overwrite consequence
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt ×2, Open, Drift, DIVERGENCE D7)

### F-10 — The README's custody claim is false in a default deployment, and Epoch anchors fail silently even when provenance is on
- Topics: SP-10.7, SP-01.7, SP-07.2, SP-07.7, SP-07.6
- Evidence: README: "every write to a pod is a git-mark commit" (R1) versus the server's default = [], which compiles git_mark_write to the no-op shim recording ZERO marks (lib.rs:3650; SP-01.7 D1; D4). Under Epoch policy "a failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark" (lib.rs:3626, SP-07.7 note), and there is no pod-wide provenance enumeration to ever notice (Rec-11/D8, SP-07.6).
- Failure: An operator on the default binary relies on the documented provenance guarantee — nothing is recorded. An operator who enables git and uses Epoch sees no signal when batch anchoring repeatedly fails, so commits accumulate unanchored while the custody claim reads as satisfied.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for the default-off half (DOC-DRIFT R1; D4); no for the swallowed epoch failure

### F-11 — Crash windows in the write path: body and .meta are two renames, and write→mark→sidecar is three non-atomic steps
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.3, SP-10.4
- Evidence: The 2026-08-19 audit records that "filesystem writes still violate the advertised atomic-storage contract … the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (fs.rs:111; F5/W2). The write→git-mark→.prov.ttl sequence is three steps the receipt only makes observable: "The three steps are still not atomic" (SP-10.3; W1, lib.rs:1478).
- Failure: A crash mid-put serves a body with another version's ETag/meta — clients, caches and any conditional update validate against the wrong digest. A crash after put before mark leaves an unmarked resource discoverable only through that one request's receipt.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (F5, W1, W2)

### F-12 — The shipped server's default group resolver is the no-op: every acl:agentGroup clause silently denies
- Topics: SP-04.15, SP-04.1
- Evidence: "NoGroupMembership — always false, **the server default**" (evaluator.rs:28, SP-04.15); the same topic's note warns an unwired resolver "makes every acl:agentGroup clause deny silently … without any error" (N1). No topic in SP-02 or SP-04 shows the server wiring a real registry into `evaluate_access_ctx_with_registry`.
- Failure: An operator writes standard WAC group grants; those agents receive plain 403s, no log names the cause, and debugging points at the ACL document instead of the missing resolver. Group-based authorization never works out of the box.
- Confidence: high that the default is the no-op (stated); medium (inferred) that no override is wired anywhere
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — it appears only as an implementer-facing invariant note (SP-04.15 N1) and is absent from the SP-10 registers

### F-13 — Solid-OIDC verification is divergent in three named ways, including a baseline invariant the material itself calls broken
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6, SP-10.1
- Evidence: aud is not validated — only claim presence (oidc/mod.rs:812; D1); extract_webid has no cnf.webid branch, LWS10 C.1 unshipped (:864; D2); discovery advertises only ES256/RS256 while the verifier dispatches EdDSA (:184 vs :763) — "Invariant 6 is the one currently BROKEN in the shipped direction" (SP-10.1 N; D3).
- Failure: A library consumer (the server's own LDP path takes only NIP-98/dev-bearer, SP-05.1) accepts a token minted for a different audience as the caller's identity, and any embedding that bounds algorithms by discovery will reject or mis-accept EdDSA tokens relative to what actually verifies.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (D1–D3; SP-10.1 invariant-broken note)

### F-14 — The default binary exposes Argon2 password endpoints with the rate-limit feature compiled out
- Topics: SP-01.7, SP-01.8, SP-02.9, SP-05.18, SP-05.21
- Evidence: The server default is [] (server Cargo.toml:123) and rate-limit is a non-default pass-through (server Cargo.toml:129), while POST /login/password, /account/password/reset and /account/password/change are registered (lib.rs:4634/4654/4658, SP-02.9). Login is an Argon2-style check (credentials.rs:104), and SP-05.21's invariant — the limiter must run before any hash to avoid a "CPU-amplification oracle" — holds only when the feature is compiled. SP-10.5's default-off register does not list rate limiting.
- Failure: On a default deployment the password endpoints are unthrottled: online brute force proceeds at line rate and a modest botnet pins every worker on Argon2 hashing, starving all other traffic.
- Confidence: medium — assembled from the empty default, the unconditionally registered routes and the Argon2 note; no statement says the routes are themselves feature-gated
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — A production pod is observable only through logs: no metrics endpoint exists and the two shipped counters are opt-in
- Topics: SP-10.12, SP-02.9
- Evidence: "Prometheus export is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE (metrics.rs:4) … a bare solid-pod-rs-server exposes NO metrics endpoint at all. There is no /metrics route in SP-02.9 to SP-02.11" (SP-10.12 N). Only two counters exist (SSRF blocks, dotfile denials), wired only via opt-in `with_metrics` builders (ssrf.rs:150, dotfile.rs:87), and "WAC denials, replay rejections, quota refusals and provenance failures are logged but never counted" (N3/N4).
- Failure: During an incident there is no signal for an auth flood (F-08), a replay wave, quota exhaustion or provenance failure except log scraping; a deployment that never calls with_metrics records literally zero even for the two counted events.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE — declared out of scope at SP-10.12)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Conditional requests and lost updates.** Whether PUT/PATCH honour If-Match/If-None-Match against `meta.etag`, and whether `mint_unique_target`'s probe-then-put (lib.rs:1494/1501) is serialized. SP-07.15 states storage has no CAS primitive, so concurrent editors or two same-Slug POSTs may be last-write-wins data loss — nothing in SP-03 says either way.
2. **WebSocketChannel2023 subscription authorization.** The legacy channel WAC-checks subscriptions as an invariant (SP-08.4); the 2023 manager's subscribe/pump (SP-08.1/08.2) shows no principal or authorizer. Whether private-resource change events leak to arbitrary subscribers needs the code.
3. **The git CGI interior.** F-01 hinges on it: whether receive-pack applies any per-path WAC or quota after the single pod-root check (service.rs beyond what SP-06.12 shows).
4. **`/account/password/reset` proof of identity.** SP-02.9 registers the route; nothing states who may call it, what secret or session it requires, or how tokens are bound.
5. **The content of the 0.4.0-alpha.15 line VisionClaw pins.** Its changelog relative to P0-1..P0-4, F7 and the replay-capacity refusal determines whether the estate's largest embedding already has the authorization fixes this material records.

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — solid-pod-rs production incident

Basis: the distilled record of solid-pod-rs at `verified_commit d64131b` (workspace `0.5.0-alpha.12`). The incident is reconstructed from defects the record itself flags as open; where the record does not characterise a mechanism, that is stated and the reasoning is marked inferred.

---

### R-1 — Payment state is protected only by a process-local mutex: a second process over the same storage double-executes credits and debits
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-07.13, SP-07.14, SP-07.17, SP-07.18, SP-10.4 (W4), SP-10.8 (F4), SP-02.7, SP-09.7
- Chain of events: Value flows through `/pay/*` — the routes are always registered with no feature flag, and agentbox already proxies deposits into `/pay/.deposit`. Load grows and a second process serves the same pod storage — deliberately as a replica, or accidentally: `bind_available` shifts to a free port with only a warning rather than failing, so a stale instance and a new one can coexist over one data root. Each process holds its own `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK`; the storage layer has no cross-resource CAS. The same deposit (or two concurrent swaps/withdrawals) hit different processes; each, under its own mutex, runs check_replay → read_state → mutate → commit_state → record_replay; both see the replay key absent; both credit the outpoint (or both debit the same balance). The ledger is a stored number — ADR-2008 D4/D5 are not built — so nothing on-chain reconciles it, and the divergence is invisible until manual reconciliation because no payment counters exist (only SSRF and dotfile events are counted).
- Evidence: `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` is process-wide, "two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock", and the README audit records "non-atomic payment state" as a reproduced critical finding (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:200`, SP-07.15); "The storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive" (SP-07.15); the guarded sequence is `check_replay` (`handlers/pay.rs:302`) → `read_state` (:169) → `commit_state` (:198) → `record_replay` (:306); the outpoint-receipt dedup lives in storage but is only safe under that lock (`payments.rs:450`, SP-07.13, SP-07.17 N3); `/pay/*` always on with the README's "do not carry value" caveat (`lib.rs:4625`, SP-07.14 N2); stored-number ledger with D4/D5 not built and D7 unmet — "nothing states what replaces it" (SP-07.18); busy-port shift instead of failure (`main.rs:146–162`, SP-02.7); payment events logged but never counted (SP-10.12 N3).
- Warning signs visible today: audit finding F4 — the lock "narrows it, does not close it" (SP-10.8); ADR-2008 makes fixing this a precondition of the port with no replacement designed (SP-07.18, open bullet); the README's explicit instruction not to carry value (SP-07.14).
- Confidence: high for the defect as described; the two-process deployment is inferred — no replica deployment is shown in this material.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Enabling MCP exposes the reproduced-critical anonymous-read / WAC-sidecar bypass
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-08.15, SP-02.2, SP-02.3, SP-10.5, SP-10.8 (F2), SP-04.5
- Chain of events: The operator turns on `--mcp` / `JSS_MCP` (a single CLI flag) to serve agent clients — the direction the estate is already moving (agentbox, tool surfaces, SP-08.16). The README-recorded reproduced CRITICAL finding "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass" becomes reachable; its only mitigation is the default-off flag. Suspected locus (inferred): the LDP path centralises sidecar elevation in one function so that reading or writing a `.acl` demands Control of the governed resource (`effective_acl_target`, SP-04.5), while the MCP tools run their own per-tool `wac_check` — if that path does not reuse the same elevation rule, `read_resource` or `read_acl` against a sidecar (or a private resource as an anonymous caller) succeeds without Control, and a sidecar read discloses the whole authorization graph of the governed resource. Client data is exposed; the client leaves.
- Evidence: "the README's status section records 'anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass' as a reproduced CRITICAL finding and says to keep MCP disabled. The endpoint is off by default (SP-02.3) — that default is the mitigation" (SP-08.14 note); audit register F2, "mitigated by MCP being off" (`lib.rs:370`, SP-10.8); `mcp_enabled = cli.mcp AND NOT cli.no_mcp` (`main.rs:292`, SP-02.2); MCP tools include `read_resource` (`mcp/tools.rs:175`) and `read_acl` (`:479`) behind a per-tool `wac_check` (`:83`); `write_acl` carries "its own lockout guard" (`:583`) — a separate implementation from the LDP guard; the one-function sidecar rule the LDP path enforces (`wac/mod.rs:252`, SP-04.5).
- Warning signs visible today: the README instructs operators to keep MCP disabled (SP-08.14); the flag ships in the CLI register (SP-02.3); the estate's agent consumers create pressure to enable it (SP-08.16).
- Confidence: high that the vulnerability exists as recorded (reproduced critical per the README); the precise mechanism and the enabling event are inferred — this material does not characterise the bypass.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Anchoring is unreachable from this repo and its failures are swallowed: provenance receipts vouch for chain commitments that were never made
- Topics: SP-07.1, SP-07.3, SP-07.4, SP-07.5, SP-07.7, SP-10.3
- Chain of events: A pod is provisioned from this repository; nothing in any route, CLI verb or boot step starts a trail — `GitmarkAnchorer::genesis` is test-only, and the MRC20 branch requires an already-issued, pay-token trail. The operator configures `AnchorPolicy::Always` (or `Epoch`) believing writes become Bitcoin-anchored; `build_anchorer` returns `None`, so no anchorer is ever built. Every write still succeeds: the git-mark commits, `record_receipt` never returns an error, and provenance never changes the HTTP status — the `X-Provenance` header is the only place the absent anchor appears. For Epoch policy specifically, even a filled epoch's batch-anchor failure "is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark". Months later a counterparty disputes provenance; `_prov` point lookup returns the git commit but there is no on-chain anchor to walk, and the anchoring claims sold to the client are unsupported.
- Evidence: "nothing outside the tests calls GitmarkAnchorer::genesis (`mempool.rs:943`), and no route, CLI verb or boot step writes a git-mark trail file (`trail_store.rs:191`). A new pod therefore has no way to reach the git-mark branch" (SP-07.3 N5, OPEN); `build_anchorer` yields an anchorer only for an existing MRC20 trail or a started git-mark trail, else `None` (`handlers/prov.rs:124–153`, SP-07.3); "record_receipt never returns an error at all" (`provenance.rs:514`) and provenance "NEVER changes the response status" (SP-07.4); "A failed batch anchor is swallowed" (`lib.rs:3626`, SP-07.7); the tier reached is visible only in `X-Provenance` headers (SP-07.5, SP-10.3); the handler's error message still blames a missing pay-token, misdirecting the operator (`handlers/prov.rs:383`, SP-07.3 N4).
- Warning signs visible today: the OPEN and DRIFT notes themselves; the README's git-mark claim already carries a `--features git` caveat (SP-10.7 R1) while the anchoring half carries no caveat in prose at all.
- Confidence: high that anchoring is unreachable for pods provisioned from this repo (explicitly stated); the client-reliance step is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Container Read on a folder leaks every RDF child regardless of stricter per-child ACLs (glob GET)
- Topics: SP-03.9
- Evidence: "the merge gates on the FOLDER only. A child carrying its own, stricter acl sidecar is still included in the merged body" — `enforce_read_ctx` on the folder (`lib.rs:2640`), child filter (`:2651`); the folder gate itself is the P0-1 fix, the child-level hole is flagged separately.
- Failure: a principal granted Read on a container requests `/container/*.ttl` and receives the concatenated bodies of children whose own sidecars deny that principal — direct disclosure of private resources.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-02 — COPY checks Write on the destination but never Read on the source
- Topics: SP-03.8
- Evidence: "COPY authorises Write on the destination only — it never checks acl:Read on the SOURCE, so a principal with write rights on the destination can copy a resource it could not have read directly" (`lib.rs:2563/:2565`); the source's ACL sidecar travels with the copy (`:2598`).
- Failure: a principal with Write on any writable container COPYs a private resource into it and reads it there, exfiltrating content their credential could never fetch directly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — NIP-98 single-use replay store is process-local: every restart, and any second process, reopens the freshness gap
- Topics: SP-05.2, SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-10.4 (W3)
- Evidence: "A process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL" (SP-05.5 note); "a second replica shares nothing, and a restart reopens the window for one TTL" (`lib.rs:192`, SP-10.4 W3); the 60 s two-sided tolerance "still leaves a replay gap. That gap is what the single-use store … closes" (`nip98.rs:28`, SP-05.2).
- Failure: a rolling restart (or the accidental second process of SP-02.7) lets a captured kind-27235 event be replayed within its 60 s window; payment routes carry an additional persisted replay guard, but LDP writes repeat — duplicate POST creations, repeated state PUTs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (open-window register / DIVERGENCE note)

### F-04 — ACL JSON depth guard counts braces only: nested arrays may bypass the pre-parse cap
- Topics: SP-04.4, SP-04.9, SP-09.11, SP-09.12
- Evidence: "check_json_depth — counts braces without parsing, ignores braces inside string literals" (`wac/mod.rs:39`); the invariant requires the cap before serde because "serde allocates stack proportional to nesting depth — removing the cap reopens a parse-bomb DoS (CWE-400)". The material does not state brackets are counted.
- Failure: if `[` nesting is uncounted, an ACL of deeply nested arrays passes the depth gate and exhausts the stack inside serde_json during policy resolution — a request-path DoS under any container whose inherited ACL an attacker with Control somewhere could plant. No CI job runs `cargo fuzz` (SP-09.11), so such a fault reaches main unchallenged.
- Confidence: medium — inferred; void if the implementation counts brackets too.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Unset mempool config anchors against the public testnet4 explorer, one URL, no fallback
- Topics: SP-07.11, SP-07.20
- Evidence: "DEFAULT_MEMPOOL_URL is the PUBLIC mempool.space testnet4 explorer" (`mempool.rs:60`), read from `JSS_PAY_MEMPOOL_URL` (:56); "ONE URL, no fallback chain" (DIVERGENCE); GitmarkAnchorer refuses on stored-network mismatch (`mempool.rs:1012`).
- Failure: a default-configured pod creates its trail under testnet4; when the operator later points at mainnet, every anchor errors with network mismatch and anchoring is bricked for that trail — while every anchor and lookup discloses pod commit hashes and query patterns to a third-party public explorer.
- Confidence: high for the default and mismatch behaviour; the operational sequence is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-06 — Forged IdP identity: the mitigation is "don't expose the router", not a fix
- Topics: SP-10.8 (F3), SP-05.17, SP-05.18
- Evidence: audit register: "forged IdP identity — mitigated by not exposing the optional IdP router" (SP-10.8); the axum binder exposes registration, credentials, password change and account delete (`axum_binder.rs:92`, SP-05.17); with passkey/schnorr-sso features off those login routes resolve to null backends (SP-05.18 note).
- Failure: an operator wires the IdP router for password login; per the audit, identity forgery follows, and a forged WebID/did binding authenticates as a victim principal wherever ACLs trust it.
- Confidence: medium — the finding is recorded but its mechanism is not characterised in this material.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (README audit register)

### F-07 — GitmarkAnchorer.anchor is unserialised: concurrent anchors double-spend the trail head
- Topics: SP-07.19, SP-07.20
- Evidence: "anchor holds no lock across load, advance, broadcast and save. Two concurrent anchors on one trail would both spend the same head, and the second broadcast fails as a double spend after the first has saved" (`mempool.rs:994`); a mark that does not spend its predecessor is BrokenLink and counts as a verification failure (`blocktrail.rs:647`).
- Failure: two epoch fills, or an explicit `_prov/anchor` racing a write-path anchor, both spend the same head UTXO; one transaction is orphaned while trail state has already been saved — voucher value spent with no recorded mark, or a saved head that never confirms and later fails whole-trail verification.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-08 — load_trail folds every storage read error into Ok(None): a transient fault is indistinguishable from "no trail"
- Topics: SP-07.3, SP-07.12
- Evidence: "load_trail maps every storage read error to Ok(None) (`trail_store.rs:94–100`) … load_gitmark_trail copies the fault (:186), so a transient storage fault makes build_anchorer return None, which the anchor handler reports as 'not configured'"; the handler message still blames a missing pay-token (`handlers/prov.rs:383`, SP-07.3 N4).
- Failure: a permissions blip or transient I/O error during an anchor causes the anchor to be silently skipped while the operator is misdiagnosed ("not configured" / "mint a trail"); anchors lapse while marks and receipts keep reporting success at the mark tier.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt / Drift)

### F-09 — Anchor verification passes an unconfirmed head and never checks amounts
- Topics: SP-07.12, SP-07.19
- Evidence: "is_intact also accepts a head still in the mempool. verify answers with is_intact, so an unconfirmed head passes" (`blocktrail.rs:849`, `mempool.rs:752`); "the amount is not checked on this path … The key and the spend order are the whole check" (`blocktrail.rs:1068`).
- Failure: an anchor transaction evicted or replaced from the mempool continues to verify as intact; a counterparty settling on `verify` gets a false positive, and dust-value marks pass, so any value assumption attached to "verified" is unsupported.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Access-token aud is presence-checked, never validated
- Topics: SP-05.7, SP-05.8, SP-10.6 (D1)
- Evidence: "aud is NOT validated — only the claim's PRESENCE is enforced … A token minted for another audience verifies here; a deployment needing audience restriction must enforce it above this API" (`oidc/mod.rs:812`, `:670`).
- Failure: behind a gateway sharing one IdP with sibling services, a token minted for another resource server — leaked via logs or a referrer — is accepted by the pod as the subject's credential: cross-service token replay into pod data.
- Confidence: high for the behaviour; the shared-IdP deployment is the inferred trigger.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-11 — extract_webid ignores cnf.webid: subject binding weaker than the current Solid-OIDC delta
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6 (D2)
- Evidence: "extract_webid reads only the top-level webid claim and a URL-shaped sub. There is NO cnf.webid branch … CnfClaim exists (`oidc/mod.rs:685`) but is not consulted here" (`oidc/mod.rs:864`).
- Failure: an IdP emitting the WebID only under cnf.webid yields tokens whose agent URI becomes the issuer-shaped sub; WAC evaluates the wrong principal — grants intended for the WebID silently fail, or an unrelated URL-shaped sub is honoured as identity.
- Confidence: high for the gap; the exploitation shape is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-12 — The acl:origin gate is compiled out of every default build
- Topics: SP-01.6 (O1), SP-04.7, SP-04.8
- Evidence: "acl-origin is NOT in any default feature set (SP-01.6), so a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential" (`evaluator.rs:352`); Control alone bypasses the gate when it is compiled in (`evaluator.rs:335`).
- Failure: a browser page that induces a NIP-07 extension to sign a kind-27235 event can drive writes with no origin restriction anywhere in the authorization decision; the WAC mitigation (acl:origin clauses) does not exist in a stock binary.
- Confidence: high for the default; the NIP-07 abuse chain is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-13 — Git smart-HTTP credential is method-*, URL-prefix and body-unbound; no replay gate is shown on that path
- Topics: SP-05.1, SP-05.3, SP-06.12
- Evidence: "GitLenient — * method plus URL-prefix" (`nip98.rs:93`); "the git bridge also passes body = None, so a push body is NOT hash-bound" (`solid-pod-rs-git/src/auth.rs:148`); one token "covers every smart-protocol sub-request" (`auth.rs:154`); the single-use replay store is invoked in the server's extract path (`lib.rs:547`, SP-05.1) — the material shows no `check_and_record` call inside `BasicNostrExtractor` (`auth.rs:115/:144`).
- Failure: a push token captured in transit or from a CI log is replayed within its 60 s freshness window on a second connection and accepts arbitrary packfiles under the same event — the 60 s gap SP-05.2 says only the single-use store closes stays open on this path.
- Confidence: medium — inferred; the absence of the gate is an absence-of-evidence claim.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — agentbox's deposit proxy hits the deleted stand-in route: every legacy deposit returns 501 at the current pin
- Topics: SP-07.14, SP-07.17, SP-09.7
- Evidence: "agentbox's payments route still proxies {txo_uri, amount_sats} to this server's /pay/.deposit and will surface the new 501 once its build exercises an alpha.11-or-newer binary" (ADR-2008 `:151–152`); the stand-in was deleted and a non-MRC20 body gets 501 unconditionally (`handlers/pay.rs:467`, SP-07.17); agentbox's Nix pin is already `v0.5.0-alpha.12` (SP-09.7).
- Failure: at the current pins the breakage is live — every agentbox deposit of the old shape fails with 501; funds do not move (fail closed) but the client-facing deposit flow is broken.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-15 — Body and .meta sidecar are two renames: a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4 (W2), SP-10.8 (F5)
- Evidence: "the rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (`storage/fs.rs:111`, audit finding F5); the ETag comes from meta (`fs.rs:107`); a reader in the window sees "the OLD pair, never a half-written one" (`fs.rs:255`) — the guarantee holds per read, not across the two renames.
- Failure: a crash between the two `atomic_write` calls leaves new bytes with the stale ETag and content type; conditional GETs then 304 or serve the wrong version and ADR-2002 cache decisions key off corrupted metadata — silent divergence between what a client cached and what is stored.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / audit register)

## visionclaw / critical

### F-01 — Drag broadcasts bypass the pubkey visibility filter and leak private node data to all clients
- Topics: VC-13.2, VC-03.9, VC-13.6
- Evidence: The one-shot snapshot path applies `compute_private_opaque_ids` + `apply_drop_set` with fail-closed semantics for anonymous pubkeys (VC-13.6, `position_updates.rs:704-715`). However, `handle_node_drag_update` and `handle_node_drag_end` broadcast through `BroadcastNodePositions, every graph node encoded with encode_node_data_with_live_analytics` reaching "every client through broadcast_to_all" (`position_updates.rs:1204-1211`). The material states: "neither the pubkey drop-set of the snapshot path nor the pre-flagged type bits of fetch_nodes apply to these frames, ADR-2003 is not honoured here." The ClientCoordinatorActor broadcast path in VC-13.3 also applies only a per-client node-id filter, not the pubkey ownership drop-set.
- Failure: A user drags a node they own. Every connected client — including unauthenticated ones in dev mode, or users who should not see private nodes — receives positions, velocities and analytics for every node in the graph, including private nodes owned by other users.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION at VC-13.2)

### F-02 — client-messages WebSocket accepts any non-empty token without verification
- Topics: VC-04.25
- Evidence: The handler checks `token = Authorization Bearer OR ?token= query param` and rejects only when empty (`client_messages_handler.rs:131-142`). The material states: "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes." The in-code comment says "Currently allows but logs unauthenticated connections" — but the code rejects empty tokens while accepting any non-empty string, so the comment describes intent the code does not implement.
- Failure: An attacker connects to `/ws/client-messages` with `Authorization: Bearer x` (any single character) and receives the full client message stream, including messages intended for authenticated sessions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT at VC-04.25, but the drift framing understates the security impact)

### F-03 — Three distinct graph-mutation paths coexist with no single write gateway
- Topics: VC-04.10, VC-04.1, VC-04.4, VC-04.20
- Evidence: `graph_state_handler` writes via `graph_adapter` CQRS handlers (VC-04.10), `api_handler::graph` reads via `graph_query_handlers` CQRS (VC-04.1), and `bots/files/graph_state` write via `graph_service_addr.send(AddNodesFromMetadata)` actor messages (VC-04.4). `schema_handler` reads via a directly-injected `Addr<GraphStateActor>` (VC-04.20). The material confirms: "three distinct graph-mutation paths coexist in this commit with no single write gateway" and a fourth and fifth read path. A write through one path may not be visible through another until some unspecified reconciliation occurs.
- Failure: A user adds a node via `/api/graph/nodes` (graph_adapter path). The schema cache and the analytics actors still see the old graph. An analytics operation runs against stale data, or a subsequent bulk reload via `/api/graph/update` (actor path) silently overwrites the CQRS-written node because it rebuilds from metadata, losing the direct write.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE at VC-04.10)

### F-04 — Settings persist failure returns 200 success; GPU propagation failure also returns 200
- Topics: VC-06.3
- Evidence: After the actor update succeeds, the handler calls `sqlite_settings_repository.set_setting(...)` and on failure: "warn only — the response still succeeds" (`settings_routes.rs:648-654`). Similarly, GPU propagation during the ~6s startup fallback window can fail with "error — physics will NOT propagate to GPU" but the response is still 200 with the new settings (`settings_routes.rs:593-595`). The client believes its settings are persisted and live.
- Failure: An operator changes physics parameters. SQLite is briefly locked. The response is 200. The server restarts. The physics settings revert to the old values, causing a different graph layout than the operator configured. No error surfaces until someone notices the discrepancy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the "warn only" is stated but not marked as a problem)

### F-05 — NIP-98 replay cache is process-local; no cross-replica replay protection
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-03.5
- Evidence: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (VC-03.4). The replay cache is `Mutex<HashMap>` under a `REPLAY_CACHE` static (`nip98.rs:221`). With more than one instance behind a load balancer, a token claimed on replica A is fresh on replica B.
- Failure: The system is deployed with two replicas for availability. An attacker captures a NIP-98 HTTP auth event and replays it against the other replica within the 60-second freshness window. The second replica's replay cache has no entry, so the token is accepted and the attacker acts as the legitimate user.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (inferred for multi-instance deployment; the material notes the limitation but does not state whether production is single-instance by design)
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a fact in a note, not flagged as a finding)

### F-06 — Multi-MCP discovery path is entirely simulated; the real discovery service is never constructed
- Topics: VC-05.13, VC-09.12
- Evidence: "the discovery path never reaches MultiMcpAgentDiscovery. The work inside the circuit breaker is SIMULATED: a fastrand 20 per-cent synthetic ConnectionRefused :304-310 and a 100 ms sleep :312, then Ok :313" (VC-05.13). "MultiMcpAgentDiscovery :100 (impl :109) is never constructed anywhere in src/" — its env-configured server list is "dead configuration on the live path."
- Failure: An operator configures CLAUDE_FLOW_HOST, RUV_SWARM_HOST and DAA_HOST to point at real MCP services. The dashboard shows discovery results that are fabricated. Decisions based on those results are wrong. The operator cannot tell because the circuit breaker makes the simulated path look like it succeeded or failed randomly, mimicking real behaviour.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE at VC-05.13)

### F-07 — Enrichment rationale gate trusts the agent's self-declared risk tier with no lower bound
- Topics: VC-05.7
- Evidence: "the tier consulted is the tier RECORDED on the case, which today is the proposing agent's own declared risk_tier. PRD FR3's effective_tier, which would bound that declaration from below, is a nostr-rust-forum clause and has not landed. A case with no row yet carries no tier and the gate does not fire" (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:332-338`). The gate refuses only when `tier is high or critical AND outcome is a human verdict AND rationale under 20 chars`.
- Failure: An agent proposes a high-risk change but declares it as low-risk. The rationale gate does not fire. The change is approved without human justification. The agent could also omit the case row entirely (new case) so the gate never triggers.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN at VC-05.7)

### F-08 — Elevation actor and REST decision paths write incompatible records to the same store with no correlation
- Topics: VC-05.17, VC-05.7
- Evidence: The elevation actor writes `StoredDecision{decision_event_id:Some(event_id), ...}` with signed-event correlation (`elevation_actor.rs:1749`). The REST path writes `StoredDecision{decision_event_id:None, ...}` every time (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:554-498`). The material confirms: "the two producers only converge at the SQLite table, no handler route calls into either actor, and case authority/failure/restart receipts are not modelled here." The inbox projects "EITHER kind of row into the SAME BrokerCase shape, indistinguishable to the bridge."
- Failure: A case is decided via the human-approval forum (elevation actor). Later, a different case with the same ID arrives via the REST write-back path. The bridge cannot distinguish them. Audit trail is corrupted — there is no way to tell which decisions had genuine signed human approval and which were API calls.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE at VC-05.17, and at VC-05.7 for the decision_event_id:None)

### F-09 — ValidationMiddleware is defined, unit-tested, but never wired into the middleware chain
- Topics: VC-03.1, VC-04.19
- Evidence: "ValidationMiddleware (src/middleware/validation.rs:126) is defined and unit-tested (content-length cap, JSON content-type check) but NOT in this .wrap() chain — grep across src/main.rs finds no .wrap(ValidateInput::...) call" (VC-03.1). Limits it would enforce: MAX_REQUEST_SIZE 1MiB, MAX_ONTOLOGY_SIZE 10MiB, MAX_STRING_LENGTH 100KiB. The validators are reachable only via the explicit `/api/validation/test/{type}` dry-run route (VC-04.19).
- Failure: A client POSTs a 500MB JSON payload to any API route. No middleware rejects it. The handler attempts deserialization, consuming memory. Multiple concurrent requests cause OOM. The size limits exist in code and pass tests, giving false assurance.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT at VC-03.1)

### F-10 — PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER is read once into OnceLock; runtime toggling has no effect
- Topics: VC-03.9
- Evidence: "env is read ONCE via OnceLock and cached for process lifetime (:43-58) — toggling PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER at runtime after first call has NO effect" (VC-03.9). This is a security-critical flag (ADR-2003). Combined with the security profile assertion reading it at boot (VC-09.4), changing it requires a restart. If an operator discovers a privacy issue and tries to enable the filter (it defaults ON but can be explicitly disabled), or disable it for debugging then re-enable, they must restart — during which the flag is read again and takes effect, but there is a window where they believe they've changed it and haven't.
- Failure: An operator sets `PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER=0` for debugging, then sets it back to `1` and believes privacy is restored. It is not — the filter remains disabled until the process restarts. Private node data continues to flow to all clients.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (requires a specific operator action sequence but the consequence is a privacy leak)
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a NOTE at VC-03.9, not flagged as TENSION/DRIFT/DEBT)

### F-11 — /api/auth/nostr/api-keys routes cannot succeed due to Path extraction mismatch
- Topics: VC-05.3
- Evidence: Route `"/api-keys"` at `nostr_handler.rs:58-59` has no `{pubkey}` path segment, but both `update_api_keys` and `get_api_keys` take `web::Path<String>` as the pubkey parameter (`:262,:290`). "actix Path extraction against a pattern with zero dynamic segments fails, these two routes cannot succeed as registered" (VC-05.3).
- Failure: Any attempt to manage API keys via the REST API returns an extraction error. The feature is broken. No one can set or retrieve API keys through this route, silently disabling any downstream feature that depends on them.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE at VC-05.3)

### F-12 — Backpressure tokens depend on client BroadcastAck; non-acking clients halt all broadcasts
- Topics: VC-13.3
- Evidence: "tokens come back two ways. The bucket refills with time (enable_time_refill) and each client BroadcastAck is relayed ClientBroadcastAck, PositionBroadcastAck, backpressure.acknowledge()" (`binary_protocol.rs:50-70`, `client_coordinator_actor.rs:1830-1840`, `force_compute_actor.rs:4181-4192`). The bucket has max_tokens=100, refill 30/s (`backpressure.rs:67-70`). If clients do not send BroadcastAck (0x34), the bucket drains.
- Failure: A network issue or client bug prevents clients from sending BroadcastAck messages. The backpressure bucket drains to zero. All position broadcasts stop. The graph appears frozen. The operator sees no error — just a static display. The system does not recover until clients start acking again or enough time passes (30 tokens/second refill vs 8 Hz broadcast rate — margin is thin if many frames are skipped).
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the material does not state what happens when the bucket is empty for extended periods, but the mechanics imply broadcast cessation)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Two class-charge tables uploaded by different actors with last-writer-wins semantics
- Topics: VC-11.5
- Evidence: ForceComputeActor uploads `class_id` with charges 0.6 known / 1.2 unknown (`force_compute_actor.rs:1185`). GPUResourceActor uploads the same ids with charges 0.3 and 2.5 (`gpu_resource_actor.rs:228-229`). The material states: "two charge tables, the later upload_class_metadata call wins" (`force_compute_actor.rs:1191`). Which upload is later depends on message arrival order between two actors.
- Failure: On boot, GPUResourceActor's upload (0.3/2.5) arrives after ForceComputeActor's (0.6/1.2). The physics simulation uses the wrong charges. Graph layout differs from what the settings specify. On a restart with different timing, the other table wins, producing a non-deterministic layout.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT at VC-11.5)

### F-14 — GPU self-initialisation bypasses supervisor accounting and health reporting
- Topics: VC-10.6, VC-10.3, VC-10.4
- Evidence: ForceComputeActor's `initialize_own_gpu_context` (`force_compute_actor.rs:762`) builds a SharedGPUContext itself when none has arrived, with 3 retries and 1s/2s/4s backoff. The material states: "a context built this way is invisible to ResourceSupervisor accounting and GetSubsystemHealth, so the boot line 'GPU context self-initialized successfully' means the supervisor path failed" (VC-10.6). The supervisor reports Degraded while the actor is actually computing on GPU.
- Failure: The supervisor chain fails (e.g. message race at boot). ForceComputeActor self-initialises. Physics runs correctly. But `GetSubsystemHealth` reports Degraded because ResourceSupervisor never saw the context. The operator sees a degraded health status and investigates a non-existent problem, or ignores real degradements because this false signal trains them to.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE/RECORDED at VC-10.6)

### F-15 — Route registration order is load-bearing but enforced only by a test, not by the compiler or framework
- Topics: VC-01.10, VC-01.11
- Evidence: "actix matches scopes in registration order by path segment and does NOT fall through a matched scope prefix. The broad /ontology scope inside api_handler::ontology::config would shadow /ontology/derived and /ontology/class-count to 404, so both MUST register before api_handler::config" (`src/main.rs:1115-1129`). "Guarded by tests/resd_class_count_route.rs" (VC-01.10). Similarly, `/api/health` collides between `consolidated_health_handler::configure_routes` and `api_handler::config` — "first registration wins" (VC-01.7). A reordering of two lines in `main.rs` silently breaks routes.
- Failure: A developer moves `api_handler::config` registration above `configure_ontology_derived_routes` during a refactor. The test catches it, but if the test is removed or the CI is bypassed, `/api/ontology/derived` and `/api/ontology/class-count` silently return 404 in production. No compile error, no runtime error.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as INVARIANT with a test reference, but the fragility itself is not marked as a problem)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual production deployment topology** — whether the system runs single-instance or multi-instance. This determines whether the process-local replay cache (F-05) and in-process rate limiter are actual vulnerabilities or accepted design constraints.

2. **The real MultiMcpAgentDiscovery implementation** — whether the service at `multi_mcp_agent_discovery.rs:100` would work if wired in, or whether it too is incomplete. The material states it is never constructed but does not evaluate its code quality.

3. **Cross-store transaction behaviour under concurrent load** — the material notes "no cross-store transaction" between Oxigraph and SQLite (VC-01.1 DIVERGENCE) but does not show what happens when a decision commit to Oxigraph succeeds and the SQLite write fails, or vice versa. The actual partial-failure recovery path is not diagrammed.

4. **The `sync_corpus` binary's exit behaviour on partial sync** — VC-09.11 notes "any sync error now exits non-zero :116" but does not show whether the Oxigraph store is left in a consistent state after a partial sync failure.

5. **The `visionclaw-actors` crate's SupervisorActor drain** — VC-02.6 states it has "no non-test sender of InitiateGracefulShutdown," meaning graceful drain is test-only, but the material does not show what happens to in-flight messages when the production server receives SIGTERM beyond `server_handle.stop(true)`.

## visionclaw / premortem

# Post-mortem — VisionClaw server (reconstructed from the VC diagram set)

Basis: the twelve topic files as stamped. Every claim below is either stated in the material or explicitly reasoned past it (marked "inferred"). Nothing here is remediation advice; each item is a defect report for verification against source.

---

### R-1 — Private nodes are broadcast to every connected client during drags and on the continuous position stream; the pubkey visibility filter only covers the snapshot
- Topics: VC-13.2, VC-13.3, VC-13.6, VC-03.9
- Chain of events:
  1. Multi-user deployment; nodes carry per-owner private visibility metadata, enforced on the read path by the PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER drop-set (VC-03.9).
  2. A client connects to /wss and subscribes; the one-shot snapshot (`fetch_nodes`) applies the drop-set, so private nodes are correctly hidden (VC-13.6).
  3. Any authenticated client starts dragging a node (`nodeDragStart`/`Update`/`End` — pubkey required, VC-13.2/VC-16.1).
  4. The drag handler answers with `BroadcastNodePositions` encoding **every graph node** with `encode_node_data_with_live_analytics`, sent through `broadcast_to_all` — "neither the pubkey drop-set of the snapshot path nor the pre-flagged type bits of fetch_nodes apply to these frames, ADR-2003 is not honoured here" (VC-13.2 TENSION note).
  5. Independently, the continuous 8 Hz push goes through `ClientCoordinatorActor.broadcast_with_filter`, whose per-client filter is a node-id/type filter, explicitly **not** the pubkey filter — the drop-set "gates the one-shot snapshot … and initial state sync, not this ClientCoordinatorActor broadcast" (VC-13.3 note).
  6. A client or auditor observes other users' private node ids, positions, velocities, SSSP distances/parents, cluster/anomaly/community/centrality values; the leaked compact ids also make private nodes addressable on the relations/expand reads (VC-04.2). Operator loses the exposed client.
- Evidence: VC-13.2 TENSION at position_updates.rs:1204-1211, binary_protocol.rs:580-586, client_coordinator_actor.rs:1475; VC-13.3 invariant note at position_updates.rs:15-24; VC-03.9 (drop-set exists only on the snapshot path, fail-closed for anonymous); VC-03.9 note that `client_filter.rs::recompute_filtered_nodes` is a quality/authority filter, **not** pubkey ownership.
- Warning signs visible today: the TENSION note itself, authored and left in place; VC-13.3's own note confining the privacy filter to snapshot + initial sync; the default class ids used on drag frames meaning even type-level metadata is wrong on those frames.
- Confidence: high — the defect is stated outright by the material; only the client-loss narrative is framing.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — /pay/{resource} debits the balance and reports success but never delivers the resource; deposits are a 501 stub
- Topics: VC-04.27, VC-09.13
- Chain of events:
  1. `solid-pod-embed` is in the default feature set (VC-01.5 note, Cargo.toml default) and the /pay routes are mounted **unconditionally** (main.rs:1081 per VC-04.27; VC-09.13 N5), inert only while `PAY_ENABLED` is unset.
  2. Operator sets `PAY_ENABLED=true` (with `PAY_COST_SATS`, `PAY_LEDGER_DIR`) to monetise inference/image-gen/analytics.
  3. Client calls `GET /pay/{resource}`; the handler extracts the NIP-98 caller pubkey, computes the tiered cost (inference ×10, image-gen ×100, analytics ×5), and calls `store.debit(&did, cost)`.
  4. Debit succeeds → `200 {resource, charged, balance}` with `X-Balance`/`X-Cost` headers — but "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (pay_handler.rs:503-505)".
  5. The client is billed for nothing. Deposits are `501 Not Implemented — "Contact the server operator for manual funding"` (pay_handler.rs:487-492); no refund path appears anywhere in the material.
  6. Balances live in the host-owned `FsPaymentStore` under `PAY_LEDGER_DIR`; ADR-2111, which would delete this store and the /pay route set, remains **proposed** and unimplemented (doc :83, :95-96).
- Evidence: VC-04.27 sequence and stub note (:503-505); deposit stub (:487-492); VC-09.13 N5 (routes mounted unconditionally under solid-pod-embed at main.rs:1073-1081, inert until `PAY_ENABLED=true`); ADR-2111 status quoted in VC-04.27.
- Warning signs visible today: the stub itself; the 501 deposit; the existence of ADR-2111 conceding the host should not own a sats ledger at all.
- Confidence: high — the charging-without-delivery defect is stated verbatim; the production-enablement step (PAY_ENABLED=true) is inferred, since the material does not show the flag set.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — RBAC role deletion is not revocation: an offboarded user retains write access (or Admin) through ambient authority
- Topics: VC-03.8, VC-03.7, VC-05.1, VC-03.6
- Chain of events:
  1. An Admin offboards a user via `DELETE /api/admin/rbac/users/{pubkey}/role`, believing this revokes access (VC-05.1).
  2. `remove_checked` deletes the explicit row only; the API returns 200 with an advisory `note` "warning the target may still hold effective_after by ambient default" (VC-05.1; VC-03.8).
  3. `effective_role` then resolves: no explicit row → **Admin** if the pubkey matches `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS` → else `configured_default`, which defaults to **Editor** (VC-03.7, role_store.rs:359, parse_default_role :195).
  4. Editor maps to `AccessLevel::Authenticated` — read plus WriteGraph; every mutating non-settings /api route requires only WriteGraph, which "denies Viewer — Viewer maps to ReadOnly only" (VC-03.7, VC-03.6).
  5. The ex-user continues writing graph data, triggering bulk rebuilds via `POST /api/files/update_graph` (no RequireAuth wrap; only the scope-wide mutating classification applies, VC-04.4), and can compound with R-1 to exfiltrate private nodes.
  6. Even a demoted power-user retains Admin ambient authority; "Genuine revocation requires an explicit Viewer assignment" (VC-03.8).
- Evidence: VC-03.8 note (removal is NOT revocation, role_store.rs:500); VC-05.1 (`RemovalOutcome{…effective_after, authority_reduced}` plus note field); VC-03.7 precedence and lattice; VC-03.6 required_level classification.
- Warning signs visible today: the API response itself carries the warning note; `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE` can only narrow to editor/viewer, so the ambient floor is an Editor with WriteGraph; `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS` silently maps to Admin "when unassigned" (VC-03.7 precedence).
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose defaults grant anonymous reads and Editor to any unassigned key
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-33.6
- Evidence: Code is fail-closed — `public_reads_enabled()` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` (rbac_gate.rs:126-133) and main.rs:807-813 refuses to start owner-less unless `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` is set. The shipped compose inverts both: `docker-compose.unified.yml:111-112` sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS=1` (cited at :106/:107 in VC-33.6 after two block moves — the posture is unchanged, the citations are not). An unassigned authenticated pubkey then resolves to Editor (role_store.rs:359, default_authenticated rbac.rs:68-70), i.e. write access.
- Failure: The system is deployed with the shipped compose; any holder of any Nostr key becomes Editor and can mutate settings/graph state, and all reads are public — on a box assumed to be protected by the fail-closed Rust defaults.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (CORRECTED ADR-2070; the open posture is called ADR-2027's "deliberate demo default")

### F-02 — A valid `owl-class` marker overrides an explicit `public: false`, publishing private pages
- Topics: VC-21.2, VC-21.12
- Evidence: `is_kg_included` is an OR — `public true or owl_class is Some` (vault/mod.rs:162-163). VC-21.12 records "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy... a valid owl-class overrides public false". The same file's fail-closed INVARIANT ("public absent/false and no owl-class means private") is therefore not an opt-out guarantee.
- Failure: An author marks a sensitive/draft page `public: false` but it carries (or retains) an `owl-class` frontmatter key; ingest makes it a KG node (VC-21.3, `metadata public=true`), served to any reader — compounded by F-01's public-reads default. This is silent publication against the author's explicit intent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VC-21.12)

### F-03 — FORCE_FULL_SYNC silently destroys governed runtime ontology writes
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-20.7
- Evidence: The governed write door writes OWL classes/axioms into GRAPH_ONTOLOGY assert (`add_owl_class`/`add_axiom`, application/ontology/directives.rs, named "governed write door" in VC-22.10). A force-full sync runs `rebuild_assert_graph`, whose "CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:930-932)", and "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph — only re-derivation from the corpus... restores it after the next rebuild" — i.e. it is not restored, it is gone unless someone re-authored it in Markdown.
- Failure: Operator runs a routine `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1`; every accepted governance addition since the last rebuild is deleted from the assert graph with no provenance record that it ever existed. Additionally, `clear_graph()` runs before a long batch re-ingest, so a crash mid-rebuild leaves GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE empty/partial with no Oxigraph backup to restore from (see F-04).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as plain notes, not flagged as a defect) — this is the contradiction between the "governed write door" framing and the sync behavior

### F-04 — Provenance graph custody is unsound in both directions: irreplaceable and un-erasable
- Topics: VC-22.4, VC-22.7, VC-22.10, VC-22.11
- Evidence: GRAPH_PROVENANCE is append-only, no DELETE/DROP/CLEAR (provenance_emitter.rs:32-33) and "restore-only because it cannot be re-derived" (github_sync_service.rs:1032-1041, VC-22.7). Meanwhile "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup" (VC-22.11) — PROPOSED ADR-2103 adds one; it is not implemented. Erasure is equally unresolved: PROPOSED ADR-2102 offers crypto-shredding or declaring provenance out of erasure scope — neither is chosen, so no mechanism exists to erase a subject from provenance.
- Failure: Disk loss on the Oxigraph volume permanently destroys the only copy of signed PROV-O attribution (including decision attribution deliberately not re-materialised into the corpus, github_sync_service.rs:1032-1041). Conversely, an erasure request touching provenance cannot be honoured at all — quad structure and content persist verbatim.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED ADR-2102, PROPOSED ADR-2103, and the explicit "NO point-in-time backup" note)

- Topics: VC-25.3, VC-27.13
- Evidence: The hub is a `broadcast::Sender` with `HUB_CAPACITY=256` that "drops oldest under backpressure" (hub.rs:22, :32-34, ingest.rs:138). A "separate passive tap task... loop rx.recv().await on /wss/agent-events hub" (kpi_compute.rs:630, :650) feeds `record_agent_trajectory` → `kpi_agent_events`, the input to augmentation_ratio, Trust Variance and HITL Precision (VC-25.3).
- Failure: A burst of agent actions (>256 queued) while the tap task is delayed drops the oldest events before they are recorded; agent_volume and every ratio built on it silently under-report, with no error anywhere — the metrics the governance loop reads are wrong in the calm direction.
- Confidence: medium (drop-oldest and the tap are both stated; whether the tap's `recv()` loop survives a lag notification is not shown — inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The production deployment's actual env/compose values** — whether `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS`/`RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS`/`RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE`, `REDIS_URL`, `ALLOW_INSECURE_DEFAULTS` and `FORCE_FULL_SYNC` usage match the shipped compose or the fail-closed code defaults (decides F-01's live severity).
2. **`validate_read_only_sparql` implementation** — whether it tokenizes/parses or does substring keyword matching, and its case handling; this determines bypassability of the SPARQL and NLQ write fence (VC-20.6, VC-25.7).
3. **Whether `UnifiedApiClient.executeWithRetry` gates retries by HTTP method or idempotency** — the decisive check for F-07.
4. **Oxigraph operational reality** — volume persistence, any external snapshots or restore drills not visible in the repo (decides F-04's loss half).
5. **Whether more than one visionclaw instance is ever run** — feature flags, in-memory sessions, `NEXT_NODE_ID` and the NIP-98 in-process replay cache (VC-26.4) are all per-process; single-instance assumptions are nowhere confirmed or denied.

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

*Post-mortem written against the verified-commit state cited in the material; every claim below is checkable at the path:line given. Where the incident narrative goes beyond what the diagrams assert, Confidence says "inferred".*

### R-1 — Shipped deployment posture makes the whole knowledge graph anonymously readable and every unknown authenticated pubkey an Editor
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-33.6, VC-33.1, VC-21.12, VC-21.2
- Chain of events:
  1. The operator deploys with the shipped compose file, which sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS=1` (with a `:-1` default), so the server boots owner-less with public reads on.
  2. Any visitor hits a safe GET such as `/api/graph/data`; `required_level` returns `None` for public reads and the gate passes the request through unauthenticated.
  3. The graph they read contains not only `public: true` pages but every page carrying a valid `owl-class` marker — the inclusion gate is an OR, so an explicit `public: false` is overridden by a class marker and the page is still ingested as a KG node.
  4. Separately, any user who installs a NIP-07 extension and logs in is client-asserted with no server verify round-trip; with no role assignment and no Owner, the server resolves their unassigned pubkey to the default authenticated role, Editor.
  5. A competitor or scraper exfiltrates the vault's private-by-intent pages; the client whose confidential content was marked `public: false` terminates the contract.
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:111` and `:112` set both flags (VC-22.9); re-anchored at `docker-compose.unified.yml:106-107` in VC-33.6's doc-drift note. Code fails closed — `rbac_gate.rs:126-133` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` and `main.rs:807, :813` refuses owner-less boot unless flagged — but "the shipped compose inverts both… so an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (`role_store.rs:359`)… The open posture is ADR-2027's deliberate demo default and stays." `models/rbac.rs:68-70` `default_authenticated()` → Editor; public reads pass through at `rbac_gate.rs:248-253`. Login is client-asserted with no verify endpoint (`nostrAuthService.ts:404-408`, VC-33.1). The inclusion-gate OR: "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy — is_kg_included is an OR, so a valid owl-class overrides public false (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`)" (VC-21.12, recorded as DIVERGENCE).
- Warning signs visible today: the estate's own CORRECTED ADR-2070 note documents the code/compose posture split; VC-33.6 records that the client never fetches its real role and enforcement is only post-hoc 401/403; VC-21.12 records the OR-gate divergence as known policy.
- Confidence: high (every mechanism is stated); the specific 12-month incident is inferred only in its narrative detail.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Erasure is structurally impossible for agent memory: the Pod delete has no RuVector counterpart, so "deleted" memory stays searchable, and the server now reads it directly
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.4, VC-22.10
- Chain of events:
  1. A client demands erasure of an agent's memory; the operator runs `deleteAgentMemory`.
  2. The client deletes the Pod JSON-LD resource; `deleteResource` treats 404 as success, and the UI reports `deleted-or-absent` as a boolean true.
  3. No code on this path ever calls `mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint — there is no delete-propagation hook — so the embedding row persists in the external Postgres and remains searchable.
  4. Meanwhile the server has gained a direct read-only RuVector client, giving the orphaned embedding a second consumer, so the stale vector is not merely forgotten data but actively served.
  5. The client discovers their "deleted" memory still retrievable; meanwhile the provenance graph is append-only by invariant (no DELETE/DROP/CLEAR ever issued against it), and the ADR that would reconcile provenance with erasure (crypto-shredding or explicit out-of-scope declaration) is still only PROPOSED, so the erasure obligation cannot be met anywhere.
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE: no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:87`)"; "this Pod-side delete never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint" (`agentMemory.ts:191-198`, `ldpClient.ts:210-215`); the second consumer drift citing `Cargo.toml:133-138` (VC-22.6). Append-only invariant with `append_only_verified` test (`provenance_emitter.rs:32-33`, `:908`), retraction adds only `dl:validTo` (`provenance_writer.rs:218,478-502`), and "PROPOSED ADR-2102 … the alternative is to declare provenance out of erasure scope explicitly — ADR-2102 exists to force that choice rather than leave it to omission" (VC-22.4, VC-22.10).
- Warning signs visible today: the gap is already documented as a standing divergence in the data-authority doc; ADR-2102/2103-era erasure work is proposed, not implemented.
- Confidence: high on the gap; the incident framing is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — GRAPH_PROVENANCE has no backup of any kind and cannot be re-derived: one storage failure destroys the signed custody record permanently
- Topics: VC-22.7, VC-22.11, VC-22.4, VC-22.10, VC-22.2
- Chain of events:
  1. The operator runs `scripts/backup-sqlite.sh` on schedule; REQUIRED_DBS covers only settings/enrichment/kpi (liveness optional), so the Oxigraph store is never captured.
  2. The Oxigraph volume suffers corruption or disk loss. GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE, the assert graph, inferred graph, summary/observed graphs are all re-derivable — from the corpus via CorpusSource, from Whelk re-reasoning, or from SQLite decisions.
  3. GRAPH_PROVENANCE is not: it is append-only, deliberately never re-materialised ("attribution is deliberately NOT re-materialised here — the signed PROV-O attribution stays in GRAPH_PROVENANCE (ADR-049)"), and the rebuild path re-derives only summary `dl:DecisionRecord` quads from corpus decision pages.
  4. The hash-chained audit record — the only signed evidence of who decided what — is gone with no recovery path; the operator cannot answer the client's custody/audit questions and loses the account.
- Evidence: "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup - that bullet stands" (VC-22.11); "PROPOSED ADR-2103: Oxigraph gains a point-in-time snapshot as a REQUIRED backup member … GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only because it cannot be re-derived" (VC-22.7); backup membership `backup-sqlite.sh:75-76`, abort semantics `:242-245` (VC-22.2, VC-22.11); append-only invariant `provenance_emitter.rs:32-33`; non-re-materialisation `github_sync_service.rs:1032-1041` (VC-22.10).
- Warning signs visible today: ADR-2103 exists precisely because the gap is acknowledged and unimplemented; the backup script's required set enumerates only the four SQLite WALs.
- Confidence: high on the exposure; the storage-failure event itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Routine force-full-sync silently deletes governed runtime ontology classes and axioms
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-20.7
- Evidence: "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (`github_sync_service.rs:930-932`)"; "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph - only re-derivation from the corpus … restores it after the next rebuild" (VC-22.10); trigger at `github_sync_service.rs:348, :603-604`.
- Failure: an operator sets `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` after a config change; every class/axiom accepted through the governed write door since the last full sync disappears with no error and no record that it existed.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Erasure and the append-only provenance invariant are unreconciled (ADR-2102 still proposed)
- Topics: VC-22.4, VC-22.10
- Evidence: "PROPOSED ADR-2102: keep the append-only invariant and satisfy erasure by crypto-shredding the per-subject key … the alternative is to declare provenance out of erasure scope explicitly" (VC-22.4); "one durable erasure record, five store acknowledgements, and a partial erasure that is recorded and retryable rather than a silent success" (VC-22.10, proposed).
- Failure: a data-subject erasure demand arrives; plaintext facts in GRAPH_PROVENANCE can neither be deleted (invariant + test forbid it) nor crypto-shredded (not implemented), so the operator is non-compliant however it responds.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — PROPOSED ADR-2102)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — All secrets sit in plaintext `.env`, including the one shared governance signing key; SOPS never landed
- Topics: VC-22.1, VC-24.8, VC-24.11
- Evidence: Credentials → ".env plaintext filesystem"; "PROPOSED ADR-2104 … a 43MB scripts/sops binary dated 2026-05-09 is present and gitignored, but no .sops.yaml, secrets.enc.yaml or sops-env.sh exists (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:112`)" (VC-22.1); "bridge bot keypair VISIONCLAW_NOSTR_PRIVKEY shared by both publisher and bridge (`nostr_bead_publisher.rs:18`, `nostr_bridge.rs:12`)"; "three separate relay connections under three different panel identities … all using the same bridge secret" (VC-24.11); "NIP-26 delegation not wired, service-signing only (`nostr_bridge.rs:29,65,169`)".
- Failure: host or backup compromise of `.env` yields `PRIVATE_REPO_GITHUB_PAT`, the agent key and the bridge/panel secret; the attacker re-signs governance events indistinguishably from the operator, and with service-signing-only there is no cryptographic agent/operator distinction to fall back on.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the compromise event is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — PROPOSED ADR-2104)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — NIP-98 single-use replay cache is a per-process in-memory HashMap
- Topics: VC-26.4, VC-32.1
- Evidence: `REPLAY_CACHE nip98.rs:215 (Mutex<HashMap>)`; `claim_event_id` at `nip98.rs:234` with the 2×-window TTL `nip98.rs:178`; token freshness window `TOKEN_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` 60s `nip98.rs:169,362` (VC-26.4).
- Failure: a restart (or, if more than one process ever serves auth, a second replica — inferred) empties the cache; a token captured immediately before a restart is replayable inside its 60-second window, defeating the single-use guarantee the signed WebSocket subprotocol relies on (`http_handler.rs:160-173`, VC-32.1).
- Confidence: high on the structure; the replay scenario is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium
