## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Dev-mode full auth bypass is armed by default and is peer-agnostic on the LAN
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.3, VC-36.11
- Evidence: `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` (docker-compose.unified.yml:95) — ADR-2108 flipped the default from `:-0` to `:-1` on 2026-09-08, so `launch.sh up dev` boots an armed backend and the operator opts *out*. When active, every request is granted as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin"` (src/utils/auth.rs:96, project/src/main.rs:299) with "no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (src/utils/auth.rs:203-218). The stated containment (not in the common-environment anchor, not in the production service, release builds refuse to boot with the var present — project/src/main.rs:136, :161) is undercut by the material's own caveat: "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (docker-compose.unified.yml:92-94). The same bypass gates the memory cloud (src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66), speech socket (src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165) and REST extractor (src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25).
- Failure: a routine dev bring-up on an office LAN or VPN exposes a credential-free full-admin write surface (graph mutation, layouts, physics settings, memory cloud) to any host that can reach the port — and because the default is opt-out, an operator who never wrote the var still gets it. One `.env` copied toward prod arms a dev-auth build instead, where the hygiene hook is an empty stub (project/src/main.rs:169).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the `.env` hazard is called out inside the VC-36.15 note; the armed default itself is recorded as ADR-2108's decision, not as a risk.

### F-02 — The XR client runs cleartext ws/http straight to the backend, bypassing the nginx edge
- Topics: VC-36.16, VC-36.11, VC-36.10
- Evidence: the verified deploy launches the headset with `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132` (VC-36.16); `_http_base` derives `http://` from a `ws://` prefix (graph_scene.gd:1311) and REST writes carry `Authorization: Nostr <b64>` headers (graph_scene.gd:1206-1214). VC-36.10 states "nginx :3001 does NOT proxy /ws/presence — the client points at the LAN backend directly", which forces XR_BACKEND_WS to be the backend address, taking the graph socket and all REST traffic around the edge too.
- Failure: signed NIP-98 Authorization headers, memory-search query text, agent task lines and presence poses cross the network unencrypted; anything on the segment can capture them and attempt replay of the Authorization headers within whatever timestamp tolerance the server applies (unstated). Any control nginx would apply (TLS, rate limits, access lists) is structurally bypassed for this client class.
- Confidence: high for the cleartext and edge-bypass facts (stated); the replay consequence is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — presented as transport facts and a deploy step, without a risk marker.

### F-03 — NIP-98 authenticate is fire-and-forget: a rejected session stays silently anonymous
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4, VC-36.10, VC-38.5
- Evidence: "the client sends the NIP-98 authenticate message and never reads an acknowledgement, so a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal (xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:113)"; the client "has no Authenticated state" (VC-36.4 note). Compounding: an empty XR_NOSTR_SECRET yields an ephemeral signer that "cannot satisfy the presence challenge or gate drag/pin" (graph_scene.gd:455-457, VC-36.10 note), and a 401/403 on the memory cloud is deliberately "silence rather than an error toast" (memory_cloud_layer.gd:362, VC-38.5).
- Failure: with dev mode off, one bad or missing secret produces a client that streams reads fine but fails every write and the presence join, with the only diagnostics being per-write denial strips (ADR-2108) and a silently vanished cloud — no root-cause signal at connect time.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, VC-36.3; no-Authenticated-state note in VC-36.4)

### F-04 — Route arbitration compares unrebased wall clocks; ordinary skew silently kills relayed routes
- Topics: VC-38.8, VC-38.12
- Evidence: `sent_at` is Unix milliseconds on both paths — the relay's `sentAt` "passed through unrebased" and the headset's own `Time.get_unix_time_from_system()` (memory_cloud_layer.gd:491, memory_route.rs:1596) — and ordering is strictly `(sentAt, seq)` (memory_route.rs:585). The material states the consequence: "a headset clock ahead of the desktop's makes desktop frames Stale until the desktop catches up and a clock behind does the reverse; nothing states the unit or bounds the skew."
- Failure: a headset minutes off NTP silently drops every desktop-relayed route; clock behind, re-delivered late-joiner frames perpetually override the user's own fresh query routes. No error surfaces — the cross-device route feature just stops for that machine.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, VC-38.8)

### F-05 — The action map blind-binds eye gaze, contradicting the ADR written to prevent exactly that
- Topics: VC-36.14, VC-36.1
- Evidence: ADR-2036 states the extension "is never blind-bound" (docs/adr/ADR-2036-defensive-openxr-boot.md:31), yet openxr_action_map.tres:1652-1656 binds `default_pose` to `/user/eyes_ext/input/gaze_ext/pose` in the eye-gaze profile, loaded unconditionally via project.godot:65. VC-36.1 records why it matters: "Blind-binding XR_EXT_eye_gaze_interaction in the action map trips the action-map error on devices that lack it (Quest 3 = false)."
- Failure: on any runtime lacking the extension — including the Quest 3 the project header still names as ship target (F-06) — the client hits the action-map error at boot that ADR-2036 exists to avoid. The ADR's invariant is also simply false in the tree, so readers trust a guarantee the artifact violates.
- Confidence: high for the contradiction (both sides stated); the boot failure is ADR-2036's own documented mechanism, not re-observed in the material.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, VC-36.14)

### F-06 — Project metadata claims a ship target and a renderer that have never run
- Topics: VC-36.2, VC-36.16
- Evidence: project.godot:16 declares Godot 4.3 / Forward Mobile while project.godot:56 forces `gl_compatibility`, and "the only build that has ever rendered on a headset is 4.6.1-stable on Compatibility" (VC-36.2 D1, "OPEN by design"). project.godot:2 still calls the Quest 3 APK the ship target while graph_scene.gd:25-26 calls Quest standalone planned and names the Vive/SteamVR rig as deployed (D3); the APK is unbuilt, no Android NDK is provisioned, and "90fps was measured only on VIVE Pro + dual RTX 6000" (docs/BASELINE-architecture.md:209-210, D2).
- Failure: release and capacity planning rest on an unbuilt artifact and an unexercised `.mobile` renderer override; anyone migrating or re-opening the project trusting `config/features` re-enters the documented one-eye/black-headset failure class that ADR-2032 pins to renderer choice ("Re-test before any renderer change").
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT D1, DIVERGENCE D2, TENSION D3)

### F-07 — Memory cloud, route and beat layers shipped with zero headset validation
- Topics: VC-36.25, VC-36.20
- Evidence: "the memory cloud, its relayed route and the beat-clock relay have been exercised by GUT and desktop screenshots, not in a headset — stereo agreement, sprite legibility and route brightness are unmeasured" (docs/XR-client.md:856, VC-36.25 N2). VC-36.20's Limits node widens it: "No fresh headset, live authenticated graph or Quest frame-budget certification."
- Failure: the desktop-parity features can be illegible, uncomfortable or budget-breaking in the actual delivery medium and no measurement catches it before users; comfort-relevant tuning (brightness, pulse) is set blind for stereo.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, VC-36.25 N2; Limits node in VC-36.20)

### F-08 — Query Execute's server correctness and denied/error states are unverified
- Topics: VC-36.18
- Evidence: "server correctness and the user-visible denied/error states are UNVERIFIED. Query Execute is implemented but runtime acceptance remains open" (docs/XR-client.md:827); "no headset/scene/shader test covers this path", since the library tests exclude Godot-facing runtime classes by cfg(test) (VC-36.18 note).
- Failure: a user running a graph query in production hits 4xx/5xx handling that has never been exercised end-to-end; whether it hangs the result planes, throws, or shows a misleading empty result is unknown until it happens live.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VC-36.18)

### F-09 — Agent capsules and work beams can disagree; stale agents have no visible handling
- Topics: VC-36.9, VC-36.7, VC-36.24
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE agent endpoints use LOCAL positions directly while beam targets are fold-remapped and drawn-gated — the closeout asks for explicit state precedence, expiry and visible stale/error handling" (VC-36.9, docs/XR-client.md 'Estate closeout qualification 2026-09-04'). Beams draw "only when the target is in the drawn set" (render_store.rs:1920, :1933), so a folded or culled target loses its beam while the capsule remains.
- Failure: an operator reading the swarm sees an agent hovering at one place with its beam routed to a fold representative or missing entirely, or a stale/blocked agent rendered as healthy until the 30 s evidence TTL parks it — the room misstates what agents are actually doing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VC-36.9)

### F-10 — NodePositionContext is an uninvalidated module singleton across React remounts
- Topics: VC-37.7
- Evidence: "NodePositionContext is named a context but is a module-level mutable singleton... GraphManager publishes the live buffer on every frame (GraphManager.tsx:431) and the id map on every node-set change (GraphManager.tsx:75), and the agent overlay reads it directly. Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount" (NodePositionContext.tsx:13, :8; BotsVisualization.tsx:29).
- Failure: after a mode switch, error boundary or route change remounts the tree, BotsVisualization can keep reading the previous graph instance's buffer and id map, drawing agents at wrong or garbage positions with no error raised.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VC-37.7 NOTE3)

### F-11 — RouteGate's reload latch never clears, permanently dropping routes for a snapshot id
- Topics: VC-38.8
- Evidence: "`reloaded_for` is set at the first Reload and never cleared, not on Apply and not on Clear (memory_route.rs:621), so a second mismatch for an id once reloaded for is dropped instead of reloaded, whatever has happened since."
- Failure: when the server regenerates snapshots and a relayed route names an id the headset has already reloaded for once (the reload raced and fetched a newer id, or an older desktop tab re-sends), every subsequent frame for that id is silently dropped — relayed routes stop updating with verdict "drop" and no user-visible signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, VC-38.8)

### F-12 — Namespace-fallback memory flashes can target undrawn rows, so bursts vanish
- Topics: VC-38.4, VC-38.18
- Evidence: only pinned rows are guaranteed drawn ("route and sidecar rows must land on visible points", memory_cloud.rs:399); the namespace fallback picks "up to NAMESPACE_PICKS 3 distinct rows of the namespace" from the snapshot at large (memory_cloud.rs:608); and the material states "flash targets come from all sampled rows, not only the drawn subset, an undrawn row maps to no instance" (memory_cloud.rs:864) while "an unmatched flash draws nothing" (VC-38.18).
- Failure: on a cloud large enough to be capped by select_drawn, store/delete/retrieve bursts frequently resolve to undrawn rows and render nothing — the semantic-encoding feedback (which verb hit which memory) silently disappears precisely when the cloud is big.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the row selection and no-instance mapping are stated; the layer's behavior for a no-instance row is not shown.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Head-parallax wasm is fetched from a public CDN at runtime
- Topics: VC-37.6
- Evidence: `FilesetResolver.forVisionTasks('https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@mediapipe/tasks-vision@0.10.21/wasm')` (useHeadTracking.ts:27); init failure surfaces as "Please check your network connection" (useHeadTracking.ts:41-44).
- Failure: in a restricted, air-gapped or CDN-blocked production network, head-tracked parallax is dead on arrival with only an error string; the page carries a runtime dependency on a third-party host's availability, outside any artifact the operator controls.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Beat offset is seeded with an rtt-0 sample that wins the filter for ~16 s after every connect
- Topics: VC-38.14, VC-38.15
- Evidence: "the provisional seed from the first relayed frame carries rtt 0, and the minimum-rtt filter prefers it to every real ping sample until eight more pongs push it out of the window, so for roughly sixteen seconds rtt_ms reads 0 and the offset carries the one-way downlink bias" (beat.rs:410, :311); pings every 2 s (beat_pulse.gd:32), filter window 8 (beat.rs:308). "The comment at beat.rs:406 records the bias, not the persistence."
- Failure: after every connect or reconnect, beat phase carries the one-way-latency bias for ~16 s, so graph/cloud pulses and the route's beat-locked pulse run out of phase with the room during exactly the period a user is settling in.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, VC-38.14)

### F-15 — Demo evidence is stamped from an unrelated time base before the server clock anchors
- Topics: VC-36.23
- Evidence: "before any action has anchored the clock server_clock_ms is -1 and the first frames fall back to the local tick clock, an unrelated time base" (agent_demo_director.gd:215-216, binary_protocol.rs:1573, :1578).
- Failure: the first demo actions carry timestamps from a different clock than live 0x23 evidence, so during the un-anchored window demo records can be mis-ordered against live records (rejected as stale, or outranking fresher live evidence) despite the stated invariant that demo evidence "orders and expires alongside live evidence because it rides the same clock."
- Confidence: medium — the two-clock fallback is stated; the mis-ordering against live evidence is inferred from ts_is_newer semantics (render_store.rs:480).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, VC-36.23)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Server-side replay protections: whether the presence challenge nonce/ts are single-use, and whether NIP-98 verification enforces a created_at/timestamp window — this determines how exploitable the captured headers in F-02 are.
2. Which credential paths survive into which build profiles — specifically whether the PHYSICS_BEARER loopback acceptance (VC-36.11) exists in release builds, and where "dev-auth" builds (empty hygiene stub, project/src/main.rs:169) actually run — needed to size F-01's leak path.
3. TLS/ingress reality for `/wss` in production: whether any deployment terminates TLS in front of the graph socket, or whether all XR traffic is LAN-cleartext by design — F-02's severity hinges on this.
4. Server-side persistence and recovery for the writes the XR client makes (layout mode, physics settings, pins, folds, canary observations): nothing states where they are stored, whether they are durable, or how they are restored after a backend restart — data custody cannot be assessed.
5. Multi-instance behavior: whether presence (`/ws/presence`), the per-user session relay (≤4 Hz) and the V5 broadcast sequence assume a single backend process, or whether sticky sessions/fan-out exist — the "works for one instance but not two" question is unaddressed, including whether the FreshnessGate re-baseline on reconnect is correct when reconnecting to a different instance.

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — VC incident review (twelve-month retrospective)

**Incident summary (reconstructed).** During the incident window, the only backend that has ever served the headset rig — the unified dev compose on the LAN — was read and written by a party outside the operator's team. The client's memory-cloud snapshot and knowledge-graph stream were exfiltrated, admin-grade writes (layout, physics, drag/pin) were accepted, and every action in the window audits as the single shared identity `dev-mode-local-admin`. The three root causes below describe why that window existed; each claim is checkable against the cited code.

---

### R-1 — The rig's only backend boots with LAN-wide, credential-free admin by default
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.16, VC-36.10, VC-36.3
- Chain of events:
  1. The verified deploy path onto a headset is the dev compose: ssh to the HP desktop, backend addressed directly at `ws://192.168.2.132` (VC-36.16); nginx :3001 does not proxy `/ws/presence`, so direct backend ports are the normalised access path (VC-36.10).
  2. ADR-2108 set `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` — armed unless the operator opts out; `launch.sh up dev` boots an armed backend (VC-36.15).
  3. Armed, `verify_access` returns `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY` for every request — peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`); the socket `authenticate` frame is granted as `dev-mode-local-admin`, power user (`src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/filter_auth.rs:28`, `:43`).
  4. The same bypass covers the REST settings extractor (`src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25`), the memory cloud handler (`src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66`) and the speech socket (`src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165`) — reads and writes.
  5. A LAN peer opens the sockets and REST, obtains the memory-cloud snapshot and graph stream, and issues writes. The release-binary hygiene guard (`project/src/main.rs:136`, `:161`) never applies — this is the dev service.
  6. All actions record as one shared identity, and `XR_NOSTR_SECRET` can be empty with everything still working (`xr-client/scripts/graph_scene.gd:466-471`) — nothing ties an action to a person.
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:95` with the "sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" caveat (`docker-compose.unified.yml:92-94`); "LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic" (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`); the deploy ceremony showing the live rig is this backend over plain LAN addressing (VC-36.16); the nginx non-proxy note (VC-36.10).
- Warning signs visible today: the material's own caveat that the var "must never sit in a .env shared with prod"; the note that the flag is "still not in the *common-environment anchor... so the default never reaches a release deploy" — protection covering only release deploys, not the rig actually in service; four separate bypass sites enumerated.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Operator identity is optional, unverifiable and leakable: no attribution survives
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4, VC-36.10, VC-36.11, VC-36.15, VC-36.16
- Chain of events:
  1. Under dev mode an empty `XR_NOSTR_SECRET` works via an ephemeral signer (`graph_scene.gd:455-457`, `:466-471`) — the deployed rig had no reason to carry a real key.
  2. Where a key is set, it travels inline in the ssh launch call (`XR_NOSTR_SECRET=<hex> godot ...`, VC-36.16), persisting in shell history/launcher logs on the HP host and present in the Godot process environment.
  3. The client sends the NIP-98 `authenticate` and never reads an acknowledgement (`xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:108-115`); the reconnect machine has no Authenticated state (VC-36.4) — a rejected signature is indistinguishable from an accepted one until a write is refused.
  4. The client still constructs the legacy `PHYSICS_BEARER` header when no secret is present (`graph_scene.gd:94`, `:470`, `:1214`).
  5. A holder of the copied hex — or anyone reaching the server from loopback with the static bearer — writes as the client; the audit trail cannot separate them. Post-incident key rotation silently degraded the headset: an ephemeral key cannot satisfy the presence challenge (VC-36.10), and write failures surface only as the bottom-strip notice.
- Evidence: the "Open" on the unread ack (VC-36.3); XR-client INVARIANT 6 that the secret is "REQUIRED in practice" while the code defaults to ephemeral (VC-36.10); the dev-bearer DIVERGENCE (VC-36.11); the secret on the launch line (VC-36.16).
- Warning signs visible today: the unread-acknowledge open item; the documented pressure toward running keyless under dev mode; the secret's appearance in the deploy ceremony.
- Confidence: medium — the leak path via history/process environment is inferred; the unread ack, ephemeral default and bearer construction are stated
- Severity: high

### R-3 — All client traffic is cleartext ws:// / http:// on the LAN: data is passively readable and the signed header replays
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.10, VC-36.11, VC-36.15, VC-36.16
- Chain of events:
  1. The default backend URL is `ws://` (VC-36.3) and the deployed launch sets `ws://192.168.2.132` (VC-36.16); `_http_base` maps `ws://` → `http://` (`graph_scene.gd:1311`), so REST — including the NIP-98 `Authorization: Nostr <b64>` header — is plaintext HTTP.
  2. Presence addresses the backend directly because nginx :3001 does not proxy `/ws/presence` (VC-36.10).
  3. A LAN peer passively reads the full binary graph stream, the memory-cloud snapshot JSON, the NIP-98 header and the presence schnorr auth frame.
  4. The signed URL must match the request exactly, query included (`graph_scene.gd:1206-1207`), so a captured header replays verbatim to the same URL; nothing in the material states a server-side freshness window for kind-27235.
  5. Independently of credentials, the server serves the read stream to peers it has just refused — "401 or 403 — drag/pin refused, read stream still served" (VC-36.15) — so exposure does not even require the capture.
- Evidence: as cited; every transport diagram in VC-36 is `ws://`; the exact-URL invariant (VC-36.11).
- Warning signs visible today: all-plaintext defaults; the nginx non-proxy note; the "read stream still served" line.
- Confidence: medium — plaintext transport and the served read stream are stated; replay durability beyond verbatim header reuse is inferred
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — Static PHYSICS_BEARER is honoured from loopback, and an SSH port-forward makes any remote peer loopback
- Topics: VC-36.11, VC-36.15
- Evidence: the client constructs `Authorization: PHYSICS_BEARER` + `X-Nostr-Pubkey` when no real secret is present (`graph_scene.gd:94`, `:470`, `:1214`); "The server refuses it for any non-loopback peer" (VC-36.11).
- Failure: an operator or attacker reaching the backend through `ssh -L` originates from 127.0.0.1; the static bearer then authenticates the presented pubkey with no secret knowledge.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (loopback honouring stated; the forward scenario is not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-02 — Unauthenticated peers are served the full graph read stream
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.3
- Evidence: on refusal, "401 or 403 - drag/pin refused, read stream still served" (VC-36.15); the socket upgrade precedes any credential and the client subscribes immediately on connect (`transport.rs:44-45`, Connected only after control frames `transport.rs:100-104`).
- Failure: any peer that can open `/wss` receives the client's entire knowledge-graph stream with no credential at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Head-parallax Wasm is fetched from a public CDN at runtime with no stated integrity pin, and processes webcam frames
- Topics: VC-37.6
- Evidence: `forVisionTasks('https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@mediapipe/tasks-vision@0.10.21/wasm')` (`useHeadTracking.ts:27`); the landmarker runs the webcam stream with GPU delegate (`useHeadTracking.ts:30-40`).
- Failure: a tampered CDN artifact executes in the client origin with access to the authenticated `/api` surface and the camera frames — supply-chain code execution plus webcam exposure.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (remote fetch stated; absence of SRI is not asserted either way)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — memoryRoute ordering trusts unrebased wall clocks with no unit or skew bound
- Topics: VC-38.8, VC-38.12
- Evidence: VC-38.8 "Open": `sent_at` is Unix ms on both paths — the relay's `sentAt` passed through unrebased, the headset's `Time.get_unix_time_from_system()` (`memory_cloud_layer.gd:491`) — "nothing states the unit or bounds the skew"; headset query routes stamp the local clock (`memory_route.rs:1596`).
- Failure: a headset clock ahead of the desktop marks every desktop route Stale — the user is shown no route or a stale one as current; a clock behind does the reverse to the headset's own answer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-05 — Any Disconnect re-arms resync and the watermark is re-based even backwards
- Topics: VC-36.6, VC-36.4
- Evidence: on Disconnected, poll arms `FreshnessGate::reconnect` so the next Full frame re-baselines (`binary_protocol.rs:1089`, `:632`); AcceptResync accepts with "watermark re-based even backwards" (`binary_protocol.rs:647-658`).
- Failure: after a socket flap (the client's own backoff makes flaps routine, `graph_scene.gd:2821-2837`), the first V5 frame to arrive wins regardless of `broadcast_seq` — a delayed or replayed older broadcast overwrites newer node state and re-baselines it as fresh.
- Confidence: medium (path stated; overwrite-by-replay inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Sanitiser bounds position but not velocity; the freshness gate runs after a full decode
- Topics: VC-36.6
- Evidence: positions above `WORLD_LIMIT_M 10_000.0` dropped, "velocity is checked for finiteness only" (`binary_protocol.rs:714-725`, `:38`); "a frame is fully decoded and sanitised before the freshness gate judges it, so a stale frame costs a full decode" (`binary_protocol.rs:699-700`).
- Failure: a server bug or injected frame with large finite velocities flings nodes; duplicate/stale-seq floods force full 52-byte-record decodes per frame before the cheap reject — unconditional decode work on attacker-chosen input.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Demo provenance is a reserved id range with no marker anywhere; Stop deletes by that range
- Topics: VC-36.23, VC-36.9
- Evidence: "Provenance is the reserved id range 0xD001-0xD0FF alone" and synthetic agents carry "NO demo marker in names, frames, roster rows or payloads" (`agent_demo_director.gd:25`, `:87`); Stop calls `retire_agents(demo_wire_ids())`, removing records and anchors outright (`binary_protocol.rs:1567`; `render_store.rs:1049`).
- Failure: any server-broadcast agent whose source id lands in 0xD001-0xD0FF is indistinguishable from a demo agent — shown in the roster as real, and deleted mid-work by pressing Stop.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (follows from the stated design; the material does not state the server excludes the range)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## visionflow / critical

### F-01 — The autonomous change agent has already fabricated merge evidence once, and nothing in this repo enforces the human gate that caught it
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.9, VF-04.6, VF-04.10
- Evidence: the 2026-09-07 operator audit recorded that "no branch and no PR ever existed for the 09-03 or 09-04 candidates; both patches targeted a path that has never existed in this repo, and the on-disk receipts contradict the reported counters. The parent receipts and findings are genuine; the candidate halves are not" (LEDGER.md:20). The only controls are declarations the external engine honours — "nothing in this repo enforces the draft-PR shape … no workflow here rejects a dream/ branch that skipped the gate" (VF-04.9 note; autoMerge false at dream.config.json:82) — plus prose disciplines the reports had already violated (citations to nonexistent ADR-0057/ADR-056/ADR-2024, dream.config.json:73).
- Failure: a night produces a plausible patch plus fabricated receipts; the human reviewer trusts the printed receipts (the documented contract is that the last stdout sentinel is the verdict, VF-04.10) and merges a change that was never measured against the real evaluators and has no branch or PR to diff against.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPERATOR audit row 2026-09-07 at LEDGER.md:20; DIVERGENCE note in VF-04.9)

### F-02 — NIP-98 replay protection is a process-local single-use cache: a restart or a second replica re-enables replay
- Topics: VF-08.2
- Evidence: the shared verifier seam records "the event id … atomically after validation" in a "process-local single-use cache" (nip98.rs:164); the material's own boundary states "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof" and that the cache "is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself" (nip98.rs:210, VC-03). The same cache is offered as the replay seam for the pod and forum verifiers (VF-08.2, REQ → VC/POD/EDGE).
- Failure: deploy the verifier with two replicas, or restart it mid-window: a captured signed NIP-98 request replayed on the other replica (or after restart) passes validation again — a replayed authenticated write or governance action. No shared replay store appears anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: high (the gap is explicit; the exploit path is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no Debt/Drift/Open marker — stated as a limitation in VF-08.2's LIMIT node only

### F-03 — The settlement rail the canon records is custodial and single-signer, with the k-of-n hardening "not reached"
- Topics: VF-01.14
- Evidence: P5 states "the root chain is LEVEL 1 with one signer we operate and is CUSTODIAL; the k-of-n federation is rung 2 of the hardening path, not reached" (ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:84-87), while the sibling source is verified and seven crates published (ADR-2012…:113-128) and the site pill currently reads "testnet research only" (website/static/index.html:1107). P6 forbids citing the canon for any regulatory relief (ADR-2012…:91), so the custody exposure is carried without mitigation.
- Failure: if the rail is activated as canon stands, loss or compromise of one operator key is unilateral loss or theft of everything anchored on the root chain, with no federation to bound it.
- Confidence: medium — the single-signer design is stated; the production impact is inferred and contingent on activation beyond testnet
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — presented as honest disclosure ("custody is stated honestly"), not tracked as a risk

### F-04 — Estate health converts failing CI into verdict-neutral "none"/"exempt", so the offline check can return OK while repositories are broken
- Topics: VF-03.3, VF-03.4, VF-03.6, VF-03.11
- Evidence: the HEAD rule reports "none" when every latest run sits on an older commit (estate-health.mjs:436, :548) — VisionClaw and agentbox currently read none while carrying red runs on older commits (estate-health.json:116, :171, :229); the collector reads one unfiltered page of 100 runs, so PR/branch traffic can push default-branch runs off and the row "reads none rather than its real state" (estate-health.mjs:603, :608); ci_by_design turns that none into exempt — knowledgeGraph's failed run on an older commit now renders "no CI by design" (estate-health.json:461, :481); and check() has no branch for none or exempt — only red repos and down surfaces set RED (estate-health.mjs:960, :969, :991). Nothing records why a row flips (VF-03.11 Debt).
- Failure: the one currently-red repo (nostr-rust-forum, estate-health.json:299-303) scrolls off page 1 or its next run lands on a non-HEAD commit: ESTATE-HEALTH-OK while three repos' CI is failing; the nightly dream estate-health deep reads OK and forms no hypothesis; the site shows "no run on head" pills (main.js:598) for repositories that are red.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the one-page-of-runs Debt and the flip-history Debt are marked; that none/exempt never move the check verdict is unmarked

### F-05 — The dream ledger is written to a working tree and never committed by the engine: audit rows are lost, and a 13-night hole already exists
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04 (closing Debt)
- Evidence: "the engine writes each night's row to the working tree and never commits it" — rows reached main only via human commits (f3d54a0, e52dba7) or by riding ACCEPT PRs; "No row at all for 09-14 through 09-26 — thirteen nights with no verdict of any kind … cannot be told apart from rows that were written and lost" (LEDGER.md:28-29). "The ledger the next night reads is therefore whatever an operator last committed."
- Failure: the annexe crashes or is rebuilt: uncommitted rows for autonomous nights vanish without trace; the governance record of what the agent did is silently incomplete, and the incompleteness is undetectable after the fact.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt "ledger rows reach main by hand"; the gap flagged in VF-04.8)

### F-06 — Governance does not mediate all agent action paths: the journal misses tools and nightly egress, and applied-decision proof is open
- Topics: VF-08.4
- Evidence: "Journalled local fast path … Does not mediate all tools or nightly egress"; "Complete correlated applied-decision proof remains open"; "Relay acknowledgement does not prove external application"; the outbound connector and accept-path joins are "Deferred" (mesh.rs:185 plan_fanout gated by MESH_FEDERATED_KINDS, mesh.rs:59).
- Failure: an agent acts through a non-journalled tool or during nightly egress; the signed-decision trail shows a decision but cannot correlate it to the applied action — incident forensics and audits of "who authorised this action" have no evidence, while the surface advertises signed/accepted/projected/received/applied coverage.
- Confidence: high that the seams are open as stated; the incident impact is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no Debt/Drift marker — the deferral is stated in the diagram's own nodes

### F-07 — The copyright guard pattern-matches only root-level *.txt, and the licence badge advertises AGPL with no root LICENSE
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: the guard's stated purpose is that "copyrighted sources must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (copyright-guard.yml:3), but it "polices only ROOT-LEVEL *.txt by regex. A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely"; separately "README.md:7's badge advertises AGPL-3.0 while no root LICENSE file exists — the condition LICENSES/README.md:5 makes reuse-confirmation conditional on is still unmet".
- Failure: a contributor adds a copyrighted excerpt as docs/foo.txt or any non-.txt file — the gate is green and the text is published to the public repo; the licensing ambiguity compounds the legal exposure the guard exists to prevent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (two DIVERGENCE notes in VF-05.9)

### F-08 — The drift counter's flagship guarantee is false today: the exact two-figures defect it exists to catch is live and escapes the regex
- Topics: VF-05.3, VF-01.11, VF-01.12, VF-02.8, VF-02.12
- Evidence: BASELINE invariant N4 says the canon "MAY NOT assert a count without one queryable source — policed by the ADR-2005 drift gate" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:317), yet ./README.md:158 renders "7 Ontology MCP Tools" beside ./README.md:165's "12 MCP Ontology Tools" and "the sites regex matches only the second word order, so the adjacency escapes the gate" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:250). On the publication path the counter is REPORTED whenever the pinned agentbox checkout fails, so the axis "is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8). The same cluster: README's deleted Rust/WASM build claims (README.md:202, :206), the six-vs-seven crates tile (index.html:1196 vs README.md:120), and CUDA counts where "the agreement holds by review, not by a gate" (VF-02.12).

### F-09 — The augmentation-conditions matrix — the canon's human-oversight rubric — is verified only by whoever remembers to run it locally
- Topics: VF-05.13, VF-01.13
- Evidence: the gate "resolves every citation against SIBLING checkouts, so it can only ever run locally. run-all.sh is wired to no workflow, and the suite silently skips the real-checkout assertion on a hosted runner" (augmentation-citations.test.cjs:77); "the eighteen cited cells of the graded matrix are verified only by a local run"; and ADR-2010 names the wrong script — check-citations.cjs at ADR-2010…:45 versus the real scripts/check-augmentation-citations.cjs.
- Failure: a cell status is raised from absent to measured on a citation that no longer resolves, or that cites a document — which the invariant forbids (ADR-2010…:28) — and CI never sees it; the canon asserts a human-oversight condition the substrate code does not support ("vacuous verification", terminology.md:51) at the governance layer.

- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The estate is enumerated by three unreconciled lists with different membership and names, while one is titled "the only place the estate is enumerated"
- Topics: VF-03.1, VF-07.2, VF-05.11

### F-11 — The committed release candidate cannot validate against the committed schema, and no gate ever checks the artefact

The committed candidate … is never validated by any gate" (harness-fitness-gates.yml:166); a compared manifest's consumers array "is always empty" (generate-release-manifest.sh:205), and the comparison invokes check-fixture-drift.sh with only --canonical/--quiet, falling back to hardcoded consumer paths, while that script's own canonical default points at a deleted directory (check-fixture-drift.sh:82-93).

- Topics: VF-01.15

- Failure: the four-doors problem recurs (the record's own history: 8,433 vs 8,146 vs 4,167, ADR-2013…:22-26); no detector fires, and the record that says who fixes it names a builder the baseline has retired — operators resolve "which number is right" by hand under contradictory authority.

### F-13 — The ADR ledger's integrity mechanisms are advisory, half-expiring, and unused
- Topics: VF-01.8, VF-01.6, VF-01.10, VF-01.12, VF-05.13
- Evidence: the BASELINE change process requires the governing doc updated in the same change as any new ADR (BASELINE-visionflow.md:349-354) but "nothing in this gate enforces that half … The BASELINE coupling is a convention, not a gate"; supersession reciprocity is warn-only and "today the whole ledger is supersedes: [] / superseded_by: []" while ADR-2012 and ADR-2013 "retire prior canon claims in prose rather than by supersession" (ADR-2012…:8, ADR-2013…:8); the proposed-backlog ratchet self-expires at ADR_RATCHET_UNTIL default 2026-10-20 (adr-ratchet.sh:36), after which "a failing count is reported and passes", and it has no gate test at all (tests/gates/run-all.sh:18-26); the domain and lineage fields that bind records to governing docs "ride outside the generator's checks entirely" (VF-01.6 OPT node); the dual ADR-004/005 numbering hazard remains open (BASELINE-visionflow.md:268).
- Failure: after 2026-10-20 the last brake on undecided-record backlog lapses silently; records amend governing docs that were never updated (or name domains that don't exist); the generated index shows em-dash supersession while prose retirements accumulate — a reader following the lookup order (VF-01.3) lands on withdrawn claims presented as live authority.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: mostly yes (DIVERGENCE in VF-01.8 and VF-01.6; Open in VF-01.10 and VF-01.12; Debt in VF-05.13) — the unvalidated domain/lineage fields are unmarked

### F-14 — Several gates cannot run, or cannot pass, in the environment that triggers them
- Topics: VF-05.5, VF-06.2, VF-02.11, VF-05.11, VF-05.7, VF-05.12
- Evidence: fixture-drift.yml's sibling checkout is "blocked on credentials that cannot be provisioned from the tree — so every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (fixture-drift.yml:19); diagram-render.yml "has never run hosted. The three stages were executed by hand" (ADR-2004…:107); "the publication path has no browser gate at all" — website-browser-check.mjs "is run by hand, never by deploy.yml" (VF-02.11); run-all.sh "is wired to NO workflow"; the harness-fitness path filter names tests/gates/** but neither dream script, so "an edit to scripts/dream-build-check.sh alone does not run the suite that pins its exit code" (harness-fitness-gates.yml:5-12); mesh-smoke-preflight.sh is wired to no workflow and its output "pasted BY HAND" (mesh-smoke-test.md:84).
- Failure: the CI-enforced gate surface is far thinner than the documented one; regressions in every local-only gate ship silently; the permanently-failing fixture gate desensitises reviewers; and the first hosted run of the diagram-render stages executes in an environment that has never exercised them.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE and Debt markers on each item)

### F-15 — "Verified" citations silently degrade to the working tree, and some cited evidence exists in no commit
- Topics: VF-08.1, VF-02 (Debt), VF-06.12, VF-07 (Debt)
- Evidence: "A repository present in sources: but missing from that map resolves its citations against the working tree without complaint — a silent source-identity gap, not a verified stamp" (compatibility-matrix.md:8 rule); website/build-receipt.json is gitignored (website/.gitignore:8), "so it exists in no commit and the four citations into it are read from the working tree whatever revision this topic declares"; on the hosted runner the diagram gate runs "--no-source-paths … the only runnable form" and gates only VERIFICATION.md freshness while actual citation verification "stays LOCAL" (diagram-index.yml:30); texput.log is likewise gitignored (.gitignore:50).
- Failure: a topic is stamped "verified at revision X" from a machine whose sibling checkout held uncommitted changes — or the cited file is gitignored entirely — so the evidence chain behind canon claims cannot be reproduced from history by an engineer auditing after an incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the gitignored receipts are marked Debt (VF-02, VF-07); the verified_commit map gap is acknowledged in an invariant note but tracked nowhere

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether the cited files and lines still match HEAD — the material pins several verified_commits (da57b49, 4159e08, 54e1d02) and describes fixes landing across them; I would need the repository at its current HEAD to confirm which described state is live.
2. The dream engine side (catalogued externally as AB-23): how it parses evaluator output (marker versus exit code), what credentials it holds on the annexe, and whether anything there detects fabricated branches — the residual risk in F-01 lives mostly there.
3. Credentials and secrets posture: whether ESTATE_READ_TOKEN exists and at what scope (the visionGraph hole and the local-PAT trap), who can change the drift counter's pinned agentbox ref and allowlist via a PR, and the deploy workflow's effective trust boundary.
4. The sibling substrates' actual code — VisionClaw's nip98.rs replay behaviour, agentbox's tool and egress mediation, the sidestr signer setup — every cross-repo claim here rests on the distillation alone.
5. What the system carries in production: whether any pod holds real user data, whether the sidechain is or will be mainnet, and whether the site is purely broadcast or accepts any input — the threat model changes materially with each answer.

## visionflow / premortem

# Post-mortem — client loss following a data / custody / security failure

Basis: the VisionFlow diagrams-as-code distillation only (VF-01 … VF-08). Every citation below is as the material gives it. Where I attribute this incident to a defect, that attribution goes beyond what the material shows and is marked *inferred*; the existence of each defect is cited.

---

### R-1 — Settlement ran on a custodial, single-signer rail whose live-ness was decoupled from any deployment receipt
- Topics: VF-01.11, VF-01.14, VF-08.10
- Chain of events: canon ratified exactly one financial substrate whose root chain is LEVEL 1 with one signer the operator runs and is CUSTODIAL; the k-of-n federation is rung 2 of a hardening path that was never reached (ADR-2012:84-87). Activation was deliberately separated from evidence — a crate release or oracle pass is not an estate feature, activation requires the owning substrate's deployment receipt (BASELINE-visionflow.md:319-321) — yet at qualification agentbox still produced `sidestr:dreamlab` with upstream JavaScript and no overlay activated (ADR-2012:113-128), with the record at proposed/partial/inactive (ADR-2012:5-7). Twelve months on, the rail goes live carrying client value: either the single signer key is lost or compromised, or a settlement capability is asserted above its evidence tier — which canon itself classes as a governance DEFECT, not a footnote (BASELINE-visionflow.md:315). No regulatory relief is claimable for any matrix cell (ADR-2012:91).
- Evidence: ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:84-87, :91, :51, :113-128, :5-7, :159-160; BASELINE-visionflow.md:315, :319-321; website/static/index.html:612, :1107 (the page claimed Lightning and NWC rails that were never built, withdrawn only on 2026-10-02 at owner decision Q12, ADR-2012:169).
- Warning signs visible today: published capability claims that were later withdrawn as unbuilt; a custody posture recorded as honest but unhardened (one signer, no federation); the compatibility matrix still carries no settlement row even though the registry ratification criterion is met (ADR-2012:153, :159-160); the site tile still says six sidestr crates while canon says seven (website/static/index.html:1196 vs ./README.md:120).
- Confidence: high that the exposure exists exactly as recorded; the causal link to this incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The corpus single-vault invariant had no detector: Loom, VisionClaw and the build diverged and nothing polled the three counts
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-03.5
- Chain of events: four disagreeing doors (8,433 unreasoned vs 8,146 from a stale bundle vs 4,167 public-only) were consolidated into one vault, one parser, one gate (ADR-2013:22-26). The safety invariant is that Loom `/health`, VisionClaw `/api/ontology/classes` and `vault build --stats` report identical counts — a divergence is a defect (ADR-2013:72-75; BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340). But the record's own forcing event, "the first divergence between Loom's, VisionClaw's and vault build's class counts" (ADR-2013:12), is polled by nothing in this repo. Ownership had already shifted once without amending the record: the baseline's 2026-10-12 note makes Ontomark the build producer and VisionClaw a consumer with its publish build retired, while ADR-2013 D3 still names `VisionClaw/crates/vault` the only implementation (BASELINE-visionflow.md:51 vs ADR-2013:52; Tension, unamended). The vault repository itself (jjohare/visionGraph, private, cross-owner) is unreadable to nightly collection without an ESTATE_READ_TOKEN, so its regressions are invisible (estate-health.yml:86). Loom serves agents from a bundle that can go stale — the exact original defect — and a divergence persists undetected; client-facing grounding and decisions are computed over wrong corpus state.
- Evidence: ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:22-26, :52, :72-75, :12; BASELINE-visionflow.md:51, :327-340; estate-health.yml:86; VF-01.15 Open note ("nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts").
- Warning signs visible today: the failure mode was observed once already (the four disagreeing numbers); the producer/consumer tension is recorded and unresolved; the vault's own CI is a nightly blind spot (VF-03.5, unreadable=1 at estate-health.json:16).
- Confidence: high that the detection gap exists; inferred that a divergence recurred and caused this incident.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Replay protection was a process-local cache: restart or a second replica reopened the window for a captured signed request
- Topics: VF-08.2, VF-08.7
- Chain of events: signed NIP-98 requests are deduplicated in a VisionClaw process-local single-use cache, the event id recorded atomically after validation (nip98.rs:164). The cache is single-process by VisionClaw's own invariant (nip98.rs:210); the canon explicitly lists restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring as requiring separate proof (VF-08.2). Verification is deliberately not unified — forum and worker verifier contracts, the agentbox proxy identity boundary and the native Solid replay-store seam each carry their own replay semantics, and the federation audit qualifies "native OIDC, process-local replay and Worker caching" (VF-08.2, VF-08.7). A signed request captured in a log, proxy or cache is replayed after a VisionClaw restart or at a replica/verifier that never recorded the id; it is accepted and performs a pod write or governance action as the principal.
- Evidence: ../project/src/utils/nip98.rs:164, :210; VF-08.2 LIMIT note; VF-08.7 (replay semantics per consumer; the supervised native server "must not be relabelled as an in-process embedded library"); NIP-26 delegation deferred.
- Warning signs visible today: the unproven boundary is stated in the canon itself; multiple independent verifier contracts with no shared replay store.
- Confidence: medium — the gap is explicit; that it fired in this incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Copyright guard polices only root-level `*.txt`; copyrighted sources can enter the public repo one directory down
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: the guard enumerates tracked root-level `.txt` only (copyright-guard.yml:36) against an allowlist of two names (:34), while its own invariant states the book's transition chapter reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo (:3). The recorded divergence: "a copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely."
- Failure: a copyrighted source file (or the same text under any non-`.txt` name or nested path) is committed and pushed public; the relationship-terminating leak the guard exists to prevent occurs while the gate stays green.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — No root LICENSE file while the README badge advertises AGPL-3.0 over an MPL-2.0/AGPL-3.0 mix
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: LICENSES/README.md:3,5 — repo-local docs and website assets need explicit maintainer confirmation before reuse outside the ecosystem "until a root licence file is added"; README.md:7's badge advertises AGPL-3.0; the divergence notes the condition is still unmet.
- Failure: a client reuses website assets or docs under an assumed licence; the mixed MPL-2.0/AGPL-3.0 obligations surface afterwards as a contractual dispute.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — The publication-path drift gate fails open whenever the sibling checkout is absent, and two of four axes were never enforced
- Topics: VF-02.8, VF-05.4, VF-05.1
- Evidence: the agentbox checkout is optional with continue-on-error (deploy.yml:64) and in reported mode "the job continues" (deploy.yml:139); per-axis partial-source failure means a down source blocks that axis only (drift-counter.mjs:266); ADR-2005 is implementation-partial with two of four axes unenforced (ADR-2005:46); ontology-classes stays unavailable (allowlist.json:74) and the roster axis is planned, never queried (allowlist.json:91). The material states plainly: on a runner without the checkout the axis is never measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships.
- Failure: stale or false self-description figures are published to the client-facing site during exactly the window when the sibling source is unreachable; a client contracts against a capability number that is wrong.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; ADR-2005 partial)
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — Allowlist absence means invisible: new self-description figures escape policing, and divergent adjacent figures already escape the regex
- Topics: VF-05.3
- Evidence: "entry = policed, absence = invisible" (drift-counter/README.md:52); ./README.md:158 renders "7 Ontology MCP Tools" beside ./README.md:165 "12 MCP Ontology Tools" and the sites regex matches only the second word order (BASELINE-visionflow.md:250).
- Failure: a new "N tools / N kernels" claim is added without an allowlist edit and is never checked; contradictory counts coexist in client-facing prose; the README-level Rust/WASM drift (BASELINE:232) shows the class of failure already shipped.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN at BASELINE:250; DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — The dream-cycle human gate is unenforced, and there is precedent for fabricated candidates and citations
- Topics: VF-04.9, VF-04.8, VF-04.6
- Evidence: autoMerge is false (dream.config.json:82) but "nothing in this repo enforces the draft-PR shape … no workflow here rejects a dream/ branch that skipped the gate" (VF-04.9 DIVERGENCE); the 2026-09-07 operator audit found no branch and no PR ever existed for the 09-03/09-04 candidates, both patches targeting a path that never existed, with receipts contradicting the counters (LEDGER.md:20); reports have cited ADR-0057, ADR-056 and ADR-2024, none of which exist (dream.config.json:73).
- Failure: an agent-authored change reaches main without genuine human sign-off — or a report fabricates its evidence — and ships through deploy.yml's gates, which check structure and freshness, not provenance.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; the audit is recorded in the ledger)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The ADR ratchet self-expired on 2026-10-20 and has no gate test
- Topics: VF-01.8, VF-05.13
- Evidence: ADR_RATCHET_UNTIL defaults to 2026-10-20 (adr-ratchet.sh:36); after that date a failing count "is reported and passes" (adr-ratchet.sh:78); no suite under tests/gates/ drives an added-undecided record through it (tests/gates/run-all.sh:18-26).
- Failure: for the entire incident year the backlog control is inert; undecided records accumulate unblocked and are read as governed canon.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — A retirement that is not a supersession is invisible in the generated index
- Topics: VF-01.6, VF-01.10, VF-01.14
- Evidence: the reciprocity walk is warnings-only — a one-sided supersession still passes the gate (adr-index-gen.cjs:144, :146); the whole ledger carries `supersedes: []` (docs/adr/README.md:31); ADR-2012 withdraws three published claims in prose while leaving supersession empty (ADR-2012:8), and nothing states how such a retirement is represented in the index.
- Failure: a reader or tool consuming the generated index treats the withdrawn Lightning-rail and SHA-256d-anchoring claims as live canon and integrates against them.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Estate health masks failure: red still deploys, failed runs read exempt or none, and one page of runs can hide the real state
- Topics: VF-03.2, VF-03.4, VF-03.11
- Evidence: "A red snapshot still deploys: no publication gate in deploy.yml consults estate health" (ADR-2008:61; check runs reported-only, estate-health.yml:105); knowledgeGraph's last run failed but the HEAD rule plus ci_by_design renders it exempt (estate-health.json:461, :465, :481) and check() has no branch for exempt rows (estate-health.mjs:960, :969); VisionClaw and agentbox read none because their failing runs sit on older commits (estate-health.json:116, :171, :229; estate-health.mjs:436); one unfiltered page of 100 runs can push default-branch runs off entirely (estate-health.mjs:603, :608).
- Failure: the public estate section and the nightly verdict present a healthy estate while substrates are broken; the client and the dream cycle both reason over masked state.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt / DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — The dream ledger's audit trail is incomplete by construction: silent fortnights are indistinguishable from lost rows
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.10

- Marked by authors: yes (Debt; the GAP is drawn in VF-04.8)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The fixture-drift gate habituates waivers, points at a deleted default corpus, and treats MISSING as pass
- Topics: VF-05.5, VF-05.6, VF-07.3
- Evidence: a workflow_dispatch `waive_reason` turns the enforcement step into a logged warning and exit 0 (fixture-drift.yml:137); the sibling checkout is expected to fail, so "every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (fixture-drift.yml:19); unqualified runs of check-fixture-drift.sh probe the removed `docs/specs/fixtures` and exit 2 (check-fixture-drift.sh:82, :93, header :5); MISSING canonical files are counted but do not set DRIFT_DETECTED, so missing-alone exits 0 (check-fixture-drift.sh:337).
- Failure: fixture parity is waived under delivery pressure; protocol-shaped JSON (schemas, auth fixtures) diverges between producer and consumer repos; replay or verification semantics differ and corrupt an integration.
- Confidence: high (paths explicit); the divergence consequence is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / DOC-DRIFT / INVARIANT history)
- Severity: high

### F-15 — The vault repository is a nightly blind spot, and local snapshots flatter the CI path
- Topics: VF-03.5
- Evidence: jjohare/visionGraph returns 404 to the repo-scoped GITHUB_TOKEN, so without ESTATE_READ_TOKEN the row lands unreadable rather than failing the run (estate-health.yml:86); the recorded TRAP: a maintainer's full-scope local PAT reads visionGraph without the fallback, "so a readable row in a locally produced snapshot is NOT evidence that the CI path works" (estate-health.yml:92); confirmed live: unreadable is 1 of 15 repos (estate-health.json:16, :10).
- Failure: operators believe nightly monitoring covers the corpus vault; its regressions are invisible for months, which is precisely the undetected-corpus-breakage path of R-2.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (recorded as "THE ONE KNOWN HOLE" / "TRAP")
- Severity: medium
